# Robotics Economics — integrated research report

Edition 0.46, operating-role funding and actual awards checkpoint, 9 November 2026. The four intelligence arrangements now join actual staffing, title, entry, qualified resources and customer awards for one target with a human-led rival. Fixed total capital can leave the responsible operator unfunded, also without robots. Fourteen families contain 259 bounded worlds. Empirical economics and development/learning/recovery composition remain partial. This is **an early checkpoint of the same active campaign, not completion**.

## 1. Executive argument and strategic implications

Authoritative chapter: [research/integrated_decisions.md](research/integrated_decisions.md).

### A completed project does not price the service, edition 0.43

The [utility cost audit](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md#project-expenditure-replication-and-realized-service-edition-043) supplies historical project expenditure and exposes separately funded site work. This improves the evidence beyond an unpriced demonstration, while leaving current accepted-service costs and realized gains unresolved. The strategic test is which decision useful information changes, after all operating, access and support costs. Innovation funding and project-budget performance cannot substitute for that test.

### Accepted information can be the product, edition 0.42

The [utility operator audit](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md#specialist-inspection-and-the-wider-service-edition-042) shifts the first purchase question to qualified information and access. An economically useful robot service can retain specialist operators and reviewers; its comparison must include a feasible alternative and all paid duties. Labor removal, production availability and capability value require different evidence. Neither a multi-shift headcount nor a successful component result establishes whole-program savings. The three decision packs now carry that test without transferring source rates or changing synthetic outputs.

The new 7 November decision campaign adds [three provisional deployment and entry packs](research/deployment_entry_decisions.md), preceded by a reassessment of all six deep cells. The strongest immediate questions are residual paid coverage in hospitals, residual mandatory visits in utilities, and workcell/asset-generation qualification in automotive production. These are conditional research decisions; the integrated execution bridges and challenge cases remain work in progress.

This campaign asks where a humanoid company should deploy, what must improve, and which organizational and commercial arrangements can sustain useful production. The early evidence supports a conditional strategy organized around customer services and their bottlenecks. It does not yet support a calibrated economy-wide penetration forecast or a claim that one morphology will replace a fixed share of physical labor.

The November restaurant comparison sharpens the customer hurdle: model-led folding must compete on the full accepted napkin service against qualified specialist equipment and a measured human baseline. Advertised speed and base wages do not reveal a removable paid shift. [FG-CMP-03](research/customer_value.md#restaurant-napkin-workflow-comparison) defines loading, acceptance, rework, handoff and support plus separate customer/provider cash and consolidated resources. It narrows the required evidence without establishing an ROI or winner.

The US labor atlas establishes a broad economic frame. The six deeper cells are automotive assembly/material presentation, third-party logistics handoffs, contract floor cleaning, hospital internal logistics, construction installation and utility inspection. These are deliberately contrasting production systems. They differ in access, quality standards, process authority, environmental variation, customer relationships and the alternatives already available. Cases outside the United States inform mechanisms but do not become US labor observations.

A production system is valuable when it delivers the required service, at the required quality and time, within the available resources and customer demand. Robot capability is one part of that system. A successful route, attractive demonstration, larger fleet, financing announcement or open repository can be relevant evidence without establishing the completed service or its economics. The dossier chapters retain those distinctions so that a commercial decision can be traced back to its actual evidentiary basis.

### A deployment portfolio, rather than a generic robot market

The six cells suggest different bounded entry questions. For automotive assembly, the question is whether a configured system can reliably complete a defined sequence within the production process, and how that compares with fixed or specialized automation. Public pilot evidence can help establish the workflow; it does not disclose a plant-wide labor substitution rate. For contract logistics, the end customer, site operator and robotics supplier may be different organizations. A commercial RaaS agreement is important evidence of an arrangement, while undisclosed price, support and intervention terms still prevent a complete investment case.

Floor cleaning illustrates why simpler embodiments and contract duration matter. The useful outcome is an accepted cleaned area under the site's service standard. A specialized machine can be a stronger competitor than a more general robot. A facilities-service contract may end sooner than the robot asset's economic life, creating utilization and residual-value risks even if a steady-state task comparison looks attractive. Transferring the asset to a service provider reallocates those risks; it does not remove them from the production system.

Hospital logistics tests the difference between a bounded delivery service and an occupation. Useful nonurgent transport can coexist with limited ability to perform time-sensitive or complex nursing workflows. Doors, elevators, handoffs and support work can decide the value of a mobile system. The public evidence should therefore change the definition of addressable work before it changes a productivity coefficient.

Construction installation involves changing sites, access, sequencing and multiple contractors. A bounded technical demonstration cannot establish a general site deployment rate. The next question is which workflow can be repeated with an explicit acceptance standard and sufficient authority over the surrounding process. Utility inspection similarly requires more than access: measurements must be usable, reviewed and linked to an appropriate decision or action. Fixed sensing, wheeled platforms, quadrupeds, drones and human teams may substitute for one part of the chain or complement different parts.

These distinctions prevent a false aggregation. A robot supplier's potential sales, a contractor's labor bill, the customer's value added and the final product's revenue are not additive measures of the same opportunity. The atlas identifies employment coverage and residuals; it does not turn headcounts into physical-work hours by assuming a universal time share. Competing systems share the same underlying work, and service providers can appear in several value chains without creating several labor pools.

### Capability progress changes a constrained production function

The capability evidence makes hardware generation, policy version, task distribution, assistance and observation period explicit. The relevant improvement is a change in accepted output, intervention, adaptation or cost under comparable conditions. Neither a growing demonstration dataset nor a benchmark score establishes a general improvement exponent across markets. Transfer can be useful within a tested setting and fail in another, including when a larger mixed dataset performs worse on a particular target.

The modeling consequence is to keep base capability, directed learning, coordination, hardware qualification and customer readiness separately inspectable. A policy improvement may widen technical eligibility while leaving manufacturing, commissioning, support or demand as the binding constraint. A new generation does not automatically upgrade installed fleets. A replacement consumes supply without increasing net active stock. A component commitment is not a finished robot; a finished robot is not a commissioned customer service.

This is also why learning value needs a counterfactual. If useful experience changes future operating cash flows, its benefit belongs in the comparison of policies with and without that learning, including collection and validation costs. Adding a separate data premium would count the same gain again. Information that changes a later investment decision is another channel and should not be confused with a measured policy improvement.

### The digital counterfactual moves too

Workstream H materially changes the robotics comparison. Human-led and incumbent operations can improve through software, models, routing, planning and better information before or alongside a robot deployment. Comparing a future robot with a permanently frozen human baseline overstates the incremental case. The report therefore uses the four-way logic of current operations, additional digital improvement, additional robotics, and their combination, with matched output and quality.

A combined system can outperform the sum of isolated changes, underperform it, or merely count the same removed bottleneck twice. The common engine exposes a synthetic interaction rather than copying an effect size from a digital study. Evidence from customer support, software development, professional-task benchmarks, simulated enterprises and embodied control remains tied to its own population, system and evaluation conditions.

Coordination is similarly conditional. The updated peer-reviewed agent study narrows the earlier blog's claims and explicitly treats its capability threshold as a within-domain selection rule. That finding supports testing architectures against a strong simpler baseline. It does not prove a universal benefit from adding agents, and it does not transfer a numerical coefficient into a physical fleet. A digital planner, conventional scheduler, enterprise transaction system and robot controller also operate at different timescales and authority boundaries. Connecting them is an engineering and operating claim that requires more than citing each component separately.

### Customer formation and provider strategy are different decisions

A customer-side AI-native newco sells an end-market product or service. Its advantage could come from a different operating design, an earlier adoption window, or access to capacity when human labor is constrained. Those are distinct hypotheses. None follows simply from access to the same robot that an incumbent can also buy. The comparison must include a prompt-adopting incumbent, customer acquisition, ramp losses, working capital, demand that can actually be won and delivered, and the possibility of price or retention responses.

A holding company that creates operating businesses, one that acquires existing firms, a standalone entrant and a hybrid face different constraints. Acquisition can bring customers and operating access while importing process and contractual commitments. Greenfield formation can allow redesign while requiring new customers and complementary assets. Thrive's disclosed structure is a useful organizational comparator, but its financing and digital deployment claims do not establish the economics or financeability of a humanoid-enabled industrial entrant.

The provider decision is separate: sell equipment, finance assets, license software, integrate, manage operations, form partnerships or operate part of the customer's value chain. These choices move cash requirements, operating risks and control rights between actors. They do not create free capital or necessarily change total production value. The published BrainOS terms make a further distinction concrete: ownership of operational data is different from access, improvement-use rights and exclusivity. A customer cannot assume it retains an exclusive learning advantage merely because it owns the data.

### Qualified supply and supported operation limit scale

The physical and digital supply analyses converge on a practical rule: qualify the resource and the workflow before pooling a constraint. Embedded LPDDR memory and datacenter HBM have different configurations and applications. Actuator specifications, environmental qualification, spare compatibility and field support likewise cannot be replaced with an undifferentiated robot count. Product specifications establish an architecture; they do not disclose manufacturing cost, yield or available qualified capacity.

Supplier investment and customer commitments can support expansion but have different accounting meanings. Micron's take-or-pay arrangements illustrate how supply assurance and supplier financing can coexist with customer commitments and restricted uncommitted availability. TSMC's reported capital expenditure illustrates investment already made, without identifying a robot OEM's allocation. Deposits, expenditure, revenue and delivered output must remain separate. A supply backcast gives necessary conditions for a scale target; it does not establish demand or adoption.

For a humanoid company, these findings favor a sequence of testable service propositions with explicit alternatives, measurable acceptance, known process authority and visible support obligations. They do not establish a universal sector ranking. The next investment-worthy evidence is a matched cohort showing accepted output, all human support, retention, full costs and rights. Public disclosures often establish only some of those links. Where they stop, the report preserves a bounded conclusion and a precise next question rather than inventing a margin or penetration rate.

### What the deeper investigations change

The hospital follow-up strengthens the case for defining service eligibility before discussing automation rates. The Guro study excludes urgent requests from its evaluated mission population, while routine handling and a trained accompanying safety operator remain part of the observed arrangement. Its reported success percentage disagrees with the ratio implied by its eligible and completed counts. The report retains both and does not select a convenient reliability coefficient. The Qilu comparison supplies distribution and retrieval phase timings; those shorter phases cannot establish whole-shift payroll removal. The operational question becomes whether a proposed service can satisfy its acceptance standard after counting all residual handling, oversight, failure and recovery work. The source-specific denominators and unresolved economic methods appear in the hospital follow-up in the customer chapter.

The non-Spot quadruped investigation reaches a similarly useful but narrower conclusion than a broad inspection-adoption claim. TenneT's operator-coauthored offshore study shows that a legged platform can traverse and inspect portions of a difficult environment. Instrument visibility varies by room; inaccessible doors, prohibited areas, safety rules and unfinished integration constrain what service the platform can complete. Later onshore pilot material shows additional inspection development, but project duration and a programmed mission repertoire do not disclose productive exposure, retained fleet use or realized avoided labor. The evidence supports a configured inspection proposition with explicit exclusions. It does not establish abandonment, successful scale, or the economics of replacing a human visit. Both possibilities remain open where the operational record stops.

The learning-data follow-up changes the denominator for a capability-investment decision. Cobalt's cloud-cost comparison concerns one collection-stage resource, and its simulated collection and separate physical evaluation have different populations. DexWild demonstrates useful human-data collection and robot-grounding experiments, while changes in dataset size and within-batch sampling mix are different interventions. Neither paper establishes a general price per unit of commercially validated capability. A robotics company choosing between teleoperation, portable human capture, simulation and additional robot deployment needs the full incremental collection, filtering, training, recovery-coverage and held-out validation cost at a fixed target. Lowering one input burden may be valuable; the value depends on which bottleneck actually limits the target policy.

The benchmark reconstruction also makes a material comparison less ambiguous. TheAgentCompany's archived displayed costs include non-perfect attempts, whereas Astranomy's reported conditional cost covers a successful subset. A cheaper-looking number can therefore measure a different event. The reconstructed table improves source-side accounting without resolving omitted infrastructure, other model use, human work or the executed monetary-limit configuration. Astranomy's paused simulation further separates action-clock success from elapsed-time service in a changing environment. Those distinctions constrain any attempt to price physical orchestration from an agent benchmark.

Finally, the pinned coordination interfaces locate a concrete systems question: what is known after a request is accepted, a response times out, or cancellation is acknowledged? Admission, assignment, execution and reconciliation have distinct authorities. A retry may repeat an action whose first acknowledgement was lost; cancellation acceptance need not reverse partial effects or establish a physical stop. A durable business identifier helps maintain intent, but it cannot alone make an external action and a local record one atomic transaction. A credible coordination contribution needs explicit failure handling and a strong deterministic baseline before a language model is added. Local fault tests can establish software behavior under declared assumptions; a compatible installed stack and target operating data are still needed for production claims.

The onboard-compute follow-up gives that timing boundary a concrete implementation example. NVIDIA's published Thor/GR00T optimization recipe is inspectable and reports a large reduction in its component-sum timing. The released measurement code, repeated observation, warm-up rules and conflicting source tables constrain the claim: it does not establish sustained sensor-to-actuator deadlines, runtime memory headroom or the target robot's accepted service. Module specifications and a faster benchmark remain useful inputs to qualification. A deployment decision still needs the exact workload, concurrent perception, action horizon, thermal/power conditions and closed-loop outcomes. The [onboard qualification analysis](research/onboard_workload_qualification.md) records the missing run artifacts and preserves the distinct task populations.

### Decisions to make next, and the evidence that could reverse them

The next deployment decision should begin with one accepted service and a credible alternative, chosen from the broader atlas. Define the customer, process authority, output and quality standard, timing, exclusions and residual human work. Then identify the mechanism that could make the proposed configuration better: a different operating design, improved technical coverage, lower total service cost, additional useful capacity, or a temporary adoption lead. Each mechanism has a different counterexample. A simpler machine may defeat a general embodiment on stable work; a digitally improved human process may remove the apparent savings; a prompt incumbent may eliminate an entrant's timing advantage; unsupported operating work may consume the released labor.

Next, separate economic value from commercial terms and liquidity. Match all alternatives on the same delivered service before allocating price, title, maintenance, control and data rights. Test the cash dates as well as lifetime margins. A refundable deposit, asset lease or supplier commitment changes who bears funding and performance exposure, and may change whether execution is possible. It is not an additional resource saving. The current annual fleet and separate monthly entrant reference illustrate parts of this logic, but their separate resource pools still prevent a joint financeability conclusion.

Research should then target the observation most likely to change that decision. For an inspection proposition, retained measurement quality and avoided follow-up visits may matter more than another mission count. For hospital logistics, complete handling and recovery exposure may dominate a faster transport phase. For an entrant, customer qualification, collections, retention and funded commissioning may matter more than the next robot-price announcement. For a coordination contribution, a reconciled adverse trace may be more informative than another agent score. Public-source work should stop at a documented missing artifact when additional searches merely repeat the same disclosure. Target-site measurement or practitioner validation can follow when separately authorized.

The accepted learning sequence can begin from this report while the campaign continues. Amar's first task is to explain these boundaries, challenge the six initial theses and identify the smallest observation that would change a preferred course. Understanding is not inferred from having a dashboard or reading a report. The thesis register and guided walkthrough supply prompts; they do not predetermine a startup, employer, investment or technical contribution.


### Utility service gate, edition 0.19

The [inspection comparison](research/utility_inspection_decision.md) exposes a discrete adoption boundary: the last required reading can retain a complete paid visit, and complete remote coverage can still leave staff or physical duties paid. Qualified fixed sensing is an explicit challenger to mobile sensing. Provider cash must fund the work; a customer payment alone does not establish it. These exact synthetic counterexamples sharpen the utility recommendation while leaving empirical lifecycle costs and monitoring benefits unresolved.

### Automotive process and generation gate, edition 0.20

The [workcell analysis](research/automotive_workcell_decision.md) makes fixed vision, real buffering and a competent human baseline explicit challengers. A synthetic six-versus-seven-tick changeover switch changes timely kit service; it belongs to a validated process input, not an embodiment label. Prepared inputs retain upstream labor, and purchased replacement hardware retains separate qualification. These tests narrow a useful humanoid proposition to capabilities and process access that alternatives cannot supply economically on the same boundary. Public evidence still does not disclose matched full costs, actual staffing removal or supported lifecycle terms.

### R&D gains must survive qualification, edition 0.21

[The new qualification analysis](research/learning_qualification.md) supports useful digital contributions while narrowing what their scores establish. Candidate construction, held-out physical service, retained-cohort regression and supported delivery have different resource and evidence requirements. Prioritize the experiment or operating record that resolves the binding stage. Generated-data volume, self-assessment completion and a newer model label cannot fill missing target acceptance or supplier commitments.


### Funded qualification follow-up, edition 0.22

The [executable learning follow-up](research/learning_execution.md) adds unique eligible operating data, a paid candidate, target/retained-cohort trials, delivered compatible runtime modules and finite support. It preserves source/target boundaries and all prior numerical scenarios. Candidate performance remains a disclosed hypothesis; real costs, rights, qualification distributions and supported scale remain unresolved.

### Economic evidence must identify the realized organizational change

Edition 0.34's [hospital economic audit](research/field_support_denominators.md#hospital-economic-claims-edition-034) changes the interpretation of an apparently attractive labor-saving opportunity. A released hour, an avoided vacancy, a projected staff-cost reduction and an observed payroll change are separate benefits. Compare complete service and retained work before funding a rollout from claimed labor savings. Preserve incompatible source versions and distinguish a supplier fee from provider resources. Acquisition, standalone entry and incumbent deployment each need their own transition, authority, cash and support evidence; an ownership label does not supply it. No synthetic coefficient or historical result changes.

### A staffing reduction can be feasible and still fail the deployment comparison

[The shared-workforce execution](research/shared_workforce.md) makes one paid crew and a second, separately invoiced workload real within the architecture/resource/Treasury loop. Notice can reduce a declared staffing commitment while both obligations remain complete. That benefit is insufficient to rank deployment: the stronger assumed human-dispatch path has lower total resource cash. Scarcity or unfunded extra capacity can also turn a proposed saving into missed service. Keep unlike work units separate, include transition/fallback and compare every payer before choosing an organization or technology. The result is a conditional mechanism, not target payroll or ROI evidence.


### Historical workforce evidence, edition 0.36

The [staffing and operating-window audit](research/field_support_denominators.md#employment-operating-windows-and-fallback-edition-036) strengthens the hospital wait gate: redeployment, estimated staffing needs without AGVs and removed payroll are different outcomes. A receiving duty and robot-failure fallback can require the same worker. Verify their joint calendar, control/repair authority and funding before changing a paid commitment. Utility and automotive inherit these requirements without hospital coefficients. Existing synthetic worlds and all empirical missingness remain intact.

### Funding fallback before optional purchases, edition 0.37

[The prospective purchase test](research/shared_workforce.md#prospective-purchase-reserve-edition-037) joins a declared liquidity decision to the actual operator, physical stock, shared crew and Treasury. Keeping cash can preserve complete cleaning and competing work after a staffing notice, while leaving the OEM with paid unsold hardware. Retained cash cannot create qualified workers or fund a separate supplier; late activation cannot undo an acquisition. An excessive reserve can also delay a feasible staffing transition. The stronger human path remains a required comparator and uses less bounded resource cash in the matched surge world. No optimal reserve, target ROI or empirical staff reduction follows.

This completes a bounded decision bridge, not the campaign. Prioritize joining customer retention/entry and contractual recovery to feasible service and payer cash, with direct digital coordination and model qualification retained. Six-cell joint execution, matched empirical whole-service cost and substantial H/I/J/K acceptance remain partial.

### Acquired contracts, actual retention and payer solvency, edition 0.38

[Joint entry and renewal](research/entry_transitions.md#actual-renewal-and-company-entry-edition-038) separates acquired contract volume from new awards and internal reassignment. A platform can own a larger customer book without creating service or new demand. A transition interruption changes actual delivery and later retention; locked contracts can worsen the missed-work consequence. Overall market service can stay complete while the acquired company accrues unpaid bills, because a separate operating firm supplies the work. Keep the parent, target, provider and growth accounts distinct and test inherited liabilities after purchase, rather than treating acquisition price or group ownership as working capital.

The stronger paid human/incumbent alternative remains consequential. Its assumed digital capacity and declared response can prevent entrant wins at lower bounded resource cash. These experiments deepen entry/competition integration; recovery, full shared staffing/resources, learning, whole-service evidence and six-cell joint feasibility remain open.

### Cash recovery, physical reuse and service retention, edition 0.39

[Thirteen combined worlds](research/entry_transitions.md#funded-recovery-and-actual-retention-edition-039) show that a service recovery attributed to a robot transaction can actually arise from the seller's restored human operation after debt cure. The original unit is manufactured and paid for in the same history; title never follows automatically from company shares. Later failed qualification retains the asset payment and stranded unit, yet can leave market service unchanged. A fully staffed human buyer can already cover the obligation without buying the asset.

The all-payer boundary includes outside customer switching resources and separately retains creditor claims, legal cash and physical assets. Exact asset-purchase funding can crowd out operating cash. Apply these gates to all three packs and six cells alongside H software/coordination alternatives and I/J/K configuration/support rights; transfer no synthetic cleaning coefficient. Complete empirical service economics and full resource/learning composition remain partial.

### Time savings, service capacity and actual labor cost

The [digital labor-outcome audit](research/digital_labor_outcomes.md) strengthens the moving human alternative without asserting a universal gain. Expectations, observed work allocation, administrative labor outcomes and accepted service use different denominators. Capacity can be valuable before payroll falls; customer cost can move to a provider without reducing consolidated resources. The three decision packs now require a feasible staffing and all-payer bridge before counting paid savings. Historical digital occupations supply a measurement challenge, not a physical labor coefficient.

### Crew feasibility can reverse the apparent entry advantage

Edition 0.41 joins paid crew commitments to actual awards and retained work. A staffing cut can transfer customers, and a larger rival can rescue cleaning while leaving the original firm's outside obligations unmet. In the matched surge, prepaid fallback serves everything but costs more than the strong human-only comparator. Follow [the conserved crew results](research/shared_workforce.md#paid-crews-and-actual-customer-awards-edition-041) for exact assumptions and cash. Underwriting must require the same complete service, qualified roster and dated funds across the transition; another subsidiary's capacity is not automatically transferable.

### The staffing decision needs forward qualified coverage

Edition 0.44 joins dated provider resources and qualified parts to paid crews and actual customer awards in one execution. [The mechanism challenge](research/shared_workforce.md#qualified-support-parts-and-binding-staffing-edition-044) shows that a successful month can trigger a later binding cut which outlives robot support. Keeping staff can then deliver more service at lower all-payer resource cash than the apparent staffing saving. A competent digital-improved human-only alternative remains cheaper in the matched synthetic window. The strategic gate is a dated whole-service and payer plan across the notice horizon; neither prior success nor purchased hardware alone supplies it. Actual target rosters, support rights, parts timing, all-payer cost and changed operating decisions remain empirical requirements.

### Fund the party that must deliver

Edition 0.45 joins intelligence roles to actual awards and paid crews. [The equal-capital comparison](research/shared_workforce.md#intelligence-roles-staffing-payers-and-actual-awards-edition-045) holds total modeled cash fixed while moving funds from the outsourced operator to the customer. Delivery then fails with and without robots: the causal constraint is the contract and working-capital location. An acquisition does not automatically move separately owned operator assets or staff. The decision must identify the employer, asset owner, support payer, current funds and payment timing; aggregate cash or fees cannot substitute for that map. Strong funded human service remains the reference, and real target economics remain unmeasured.

### More information can create work, edition 0.46

A permitted [utility operator closeout](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md#virtual-inspection-and-retained-field-work-edition-046) separates better defect visibility from an economical whole-service replacement. Value the maintenance decision and accepted service, including residual physical checks, identity reconciliation, review and escalation. Detection may create useful repair work rather than remove payroll. Use the strong manual/hybrid comparator and every payer; the historical report supplies no current all-payer ROI.

The separate [AATIS closeout](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md#demonstration-approval-and-the-build-versus-buy-decision-edition-046) adds a build-versus-buy boundary: technical progress does not compel continued bespoke development when a better outside option emerges. Keep sunk work, remaining qualified delivery, approval and assignable rights explicit. The operator's commercial judgment does not establish that a replacement was purchased or delivered savings.

### Focused follow-up: Deployment and entry decision packs

Authoritative analysis: [research/deployment_entry_decisions.md](research/deployment_entry_decisions.md).

#### Separate innovation funding from service economics, edition 0.43

The [GRAID project-cost audit](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md#project-expenditure-replication-and-realized-service-edition-043) gives the utility pack a concrete accounting challenge: a project can finish under its revised budget while requiring owner funds and excluding site construction. A historical project account, equipment replication indication and realized service cash series are different cost objects.

All three packs now require the same accounting boundary, without transferring specialist inspection prices: trace funding once, include costs outside the project, qualify the current service, and identify an actually changed customer decision. Newco, acquisition, hybrid and provider options retain their distinct payer and rights requirements. The six-cell selection and every prior numeric scenario remain unchanged.

#### Utility operator challenge, edition 0.42

[QOF-AUDIT-SERVICE-02](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md#specialist-inspection-and-the-wider-service-edition-042) makes a qualified information/access purchase distinct from paid labor removal and wider production value. The utility recommendation now requires the feasible alternative and actual retained work before pricing savings. The six-cell selection is unchanged.

Hospital and automotive packs inherit the accounting test, not nuclear methods or staffing levels: retain receipt/quality, review, response and support; test any effect on the complete service and paid commitment. A newco must fund that organization, an acquisition must establish usable rights and qualified people, and a provider must price every service duty. Public operator activity still leaves target costs, payroll and causal outage effects unknown. Prior synthetic mechanisms and the 53 bounded met / 16 partial legacy audit are unchanged.

Decision campaign, integrated checkpoint 0.43 · 9 November 2026 · Sole-writer synthesis and review. The [active contract](DEPLOYMENT_ENTRY_CAMPAIGN.md) runs through 14 November. These provisional decisions remain under challenge; this is not the final Deployment and Entry Decision Edition.

The current research supports a deployment sequence conditional on the service boundary, not a universal winner among humanoids, quadrupeds, specialist machines and people. Hospital logistics tests whether routine transport releases an actual paid commitment once urgency, handling and escort are included. Utility inspection tests whether better information eliminates a mandatory visit or instead buys additional monitoring. Automotive presentation tests whether a flexible configuration beats fixed automation at a declared takt and survives a generational change. These are different units and different reasons to buy; their source metrics must not be pooled.

#### Reassessment of the six deep cells

The machine-readable review and three packs extend [market_evidence.json](data/market_evidence.json). The original six cells, fifteen market families, official employment records and synthetic assumptions are unchanged. A selection for deeper decision analysis is not an ROI ranking or a claim of representative market coverage.

| Existing cell | What makes the comparison informative | Most consequential challenge | Current treatment |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Automotive assembly/material presentation | Production-connected loading evidence and an explicit newer mixed sequencing workflow | Fixed automation may meet stable geometry more cheaply; old-generation output cannot qualify the successor | Selected manufacturing pack |
| 3PL tote handoff | A bounded AMR/conveyor interface and operator-confirmed service arrangement | Unknown accepted-output exposure, support, price and retention; common robot access leaves entry economics unresolved | Retained comparison and contract test |
| Commercial floor cleaning | Repeated specialist-machine use and digitally informed scheduling | Edges, refills, dispersed sites and retained shifts can absorb the apparent saving | Retained simple-machine and enterprise-model comparator |
| Hospital internal logistics | Route studies reveal urgency selection, handling and accompanying labor | Faster successful transport need not remove an escort or courier coverage | Selected service pack |
| Construction installation | Digital plans and specialist drilling show process redesign with an operator | Holes drilled omit setup, site changes and the rest of installation | Retained digital/specialist comparator |
| Utility inspection | Operator-linked coverage studies expose selected points and residual visits | More readings need not remove a visit or cause avoided outages | Selected inspection pack |

Automotive presentation is the strongest *bounded humanoid manufacturing challenge case* in this selection because the accepted evidence connects a configured robot with production and distinguishes its successor workflow. Floor cleaning remains a strong challenge to general-purpose embodiment, and tote handoff remains useful for service-contract analysis. The record does not support selecting any of these as the highest-return business.

#### Hospital logistics — qualify the entire obligation

The accepted service is a uniquely requested permitted item received by the designated person in the required condition and custody state before its urgency-specific deadline. The demand ledger includes ineligible requests, urgent work, deferrals, failed attempts and human fallback. A successful selected robot journey is only one part of that obligation.

Existing Guro evidence distinguishes routine loading and receipt, a trained accompanying operator, and corrective failures. Its conflicting overall success figures remain unresolved. Qilu supplies a manual comparator and lower reported transport-phase means, but its accessible methods do not establish the complete staffing and ROI boundary. The ChristianaCare intervention-count conflict remains open. These studies support different mechanisms and populations; none calibrates a general US nursing-labor saving. See [the denominator audit](research/field_support_denominators.md).

A new adverse workforce account sharpens the qualification question. WSNA reports that members at Tacoma General and Good Samaritan experienced obstructive robot movement and inter-floor escort requirements without meaningful time savings. Its March 2026 article also reports a 2025 withdrawal, partly relying on newspaper reporting; a direct operator closeout was not retrieved. The account is evidence of attributed worker experience and a reported exit, with unspecified respondent selection and exposure. It does not identify a causal failure rate, contract return or the reason for withdrawal. [FSD-E005; WSNA](https://www.wsna.org/news/2026/saying-goodbye-to-moxi-the-ai-powered-robot)

The first comparator is a digitally improved human courier: common dispatch, route batching, congestion information and electronic receipt. Measure it on the same request population. A specialized wheeled carrier is the next alternative where elevators, load, custody and access are qualified. A mobile manipulator or humanoid earns inclusion only when its extra handling or access ability closes an important service gap. A mixed system can assign routine work to robots and urgent/excluded work to people, but the residual roster is still paid. No free digital or human fallback is assumed.

**Provisional recommendation:** proceed with the analytical qualification of a bounded nonurgent workflow and its improved human comparator; wait on scale entry or payroll substitution. A deployment case passes its proceed gate only with complete request-level acceptance, all support work, authorized site/process control and funded fallback. This is a research recommendation, not initiation of a field trial. Reject a specific case that misses mandatory service or assumes removal of a shift that must remain for urgent work. A credible reversal toward automation requires either an actual avoidable paid commitment or independently valued additional service capacity after support. Digital-human improvement, unavoidable escort or low eligible demand can reverse it again.

##### Repair and replacement are part of the hospital service gate

The [hospital contract audit](research/hospital_support_contracts.md) adds an explicit reason to withhold a deployment recommendation based on hardware price plus maintenance. A retrieved T-2.5 schedule separates site work and recurring support, while its attached remedy does not promise a maximum restoration time or a funded substitute. A UK hospital award attributes maintenance dependence to its installed closed system. Neither source measures a US hospital outcome. Proceed now requires a current configured order and repair/exit rights **plus a funded fallback capable of the same accepted service**. Reject fee relief as a substitute for delivered medication, and reject an entry case that assumes cheap or immediate replacement without evidence. The comparative staffing studies and their conflicts remain unchanged.

##### Compare the best feasible human dispatcher

The [conventional human evidence follow-up](research/conventional_coordination.md#empirical-challenge-a-human-dispatcher-with-better-information) adds a real patient-flow implementation to the baseline challenge. Its observational associations are not medication-service coefficients. Require a competent conventional dispatcher and complete due-request, cancellation, affected-group and staff-activity records before attributing incremental agent or robot value. Aggregate request elapsed time cannot establish removed paid labor. This strengthens the hospital proceed/wait/reject gates while leaving all prior scenario quantities unchanged.

#### Utility inspection — separate information from the residual visit

The accepted service is a required asset/measurement set, at the specified quality and interval, with reviewer disposition. Maintenance authorization and completed physical intervention are downstream activities. A correctly interpreted absence of an anomaly can be useful information; the model must not assign every anomaly an avoided failure loss.

TenneT's historical coverage results and selected-route planning show why traversing a site and reading selected instruments do not imply complete inspection coverage. Point visibility, doors, operating restrictions, expert interpretation and the original mandatory route remain material. The later public planning record leaves financial results unfilled. These observations are preserved in [the operator follow-up](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md). A targeted later-operator search did not retrieve a new closeout; TenneT's site was blocked to the retrieval tool. This establishes a disclosure/access gap, not abandonment or continuing deployment. The earlier pilot searches are not restarted as a source-count exercise.

Compare improved human inspection and digital reporting with fixed sensing, wheeled systems where routes permit, configured quadrupeds where terrain or viewpoints matter, and mixed aerial/ground/human work. A drone delivering different measurements is a complement, not a cheaper substitute. A manipulator may improve door or instrument access but requires its own qualified configuration and support economics. Retain calibration, communications, charging, review, exceptions and corrective work under every relevant alternative.

**Provisional recommendation:** prioritize a coverage-and-disposition service contract. A monitoring case can pass its proceed gate with valued incremental information, complete measurement quality and explicit operating responsibility even if payroll stays constant. Wait on replicated scale economics until recurring exposure, support, costs, rights, retention and accessible supply are known. Reject the labor-removal claim when the same paid visit remains mandatory; reject a quantified avoided-outage claim without a defensible counterfactual. These rejections do not imply that added monitoring is worthless. Fixed sensing can become preferable for stable accessible points; quadrupeds can become preferable when mobility closes enough required work to avoid a whole visit at acceptable total burden. Mixed service remains plausible when residual work is substantial.

#### Automotive presentation — qualify the workcell and the generation

The accepted service is a correct component placed and verified in its required fixture or sequence position before takt, with the handoff accepted. Welding, complete vehicles and downstream assembly are separate activity. Production contribution is useful evidence of context; it is not the value of an entire car produced by a robot.

The existing BMW records already distinguish Figure 02's body-shop contribution from Figure 03's sequencing project. The latter includes subsequent tugger/STR transport and human assembly. BMW also describes digital tools for manual-process design and quality feedback. Those are reasons to use an evolving human/process comparator, not measured coefficients of robot benefit. The new generation needs its own operating denominator. [CAP-E001–003; BMW's workflow account](https://www.press.bmwgroup.com/global/article/detail/T0458778EN/bmw-group-advances-the-use-of-physical-ai-in-production-with-figure-03-project-in-spartanburg)

Figure's September 2026 account adds a lifecycle constraint: the supplier says most Figure 02 units have been decommissioned, with a few stored, and describes older-fleet maintenance and disassembly as competing for technical resources as newer systems advance. It discloses neither unit-level dates nor customer contract, salvage or service-life values. This is not a new shutdown date for BMW and supplies no Figure 03 productivity result. It does make indefinite support and automatic fleet upgrades particularly unsuitable defaults. [CAP-E024; Figure](https://www.figure.ai/news/f-02-decommission)

Compare digital-human/AMR presentation, a fixed arm/fixture or redesigned transfer, a bounded humanoid station, and the declared mixed sequencing workflow. A flexible machine must earn its additional burden through accepted part diversity, reduced validated changeover or otherwise unavailable access. Geometry, takt, reject/rework, line-stop consequences, interlocks, recovery, service staffing, spares and upgrade qualification belong to the same workcell comparison. No arbitrary specialist-cost factor settles the real choice.

**Provisional recommendation:** use this cell to test whether configuration flexibility pays for itself; wait on broad replacement or greenfield entry claims. Proceed only when the specified workcell's acceptance, recovery and support/upgrade obligations are matched to real demand. Reject extrapolation from vehicles supported to vehicles assembled, from retired assets to available capacity, or from a supplier's disposal choice to a customer's salvage value. Stable geometry and volume favor testing fixed automation first; demonstrated frequent reconfiguration can change that preference. Early withdrawal of support or expensive requalification can reverse a favorable lifetime calculation.

#### Funded entry and inherited obligations

The [entry-transition comparison](research/entry_transitions.md) implements status quo, build, share acquisition and hybrid on the same cleaning-zone calendar and physical ledger. The price goes to the selling owner; the target needs separate operating funds. Share ownership does not move customers, lease title, unpaid liabilities or pre-close receivables to new legal accounts. Hybrid growth is separately committed and uses only already specified unmet/new demand. A real hospital, utility or automotive purchase still needs its own legal, service, rights, capital and transition evidence; these invented prices and output units do not calibrate those packs.

#### The digital baseline and coordination burden

Each decision needs four matched branches: human work with existing conventional systems; that work with a specified additional digital intervention; the robot-assisted process with its required embedded software; and the combined process with the same additional intervention. The robot increment after digital improvement is `Y11 − Y01`; interaction is `Y11 − Y10 − Y01 + Y00`. Keep output, cash and resources as separate outcomes. Missing or infeasible branches remain missing. Savings that overlap between dispatch software, process redesign and robotics are booked once.

The earlier transaction fixture establishes failure-state reasoning under a cooperative mock lookup/finality contract; its naive resend baseline remains deliberately weak. The [new conventional comparator](research/conventional_coordination.md) now exhaustively schedules the currently observed bounded request queue under shared resource, deadline, standby and funded-execution constraints. It can wait and replan after arrivals or outages. Equal-cost, zero-delay proposal labels add no benefit; a delayed synthetic proposal can miss a mandatory request unless a conventional urgent path remains. No LLM is run, and the local executor does not establish installed durability or physical qualification. This strengthens the comparator without converting synthetic service-window cash into lifecycle economics or a real clinical policy.

Similarly, model/R&D improvement needs a chain from generated candidate to validated physical capability. Log code or reward candidates, rejected proposals, simulation exposure, robot-data rights, training resources, held-out qualification, and delivery of compatible updates to the affected cohort. A larger dataset, cheaper collection stage or better simulation score does not itself create accepted service. Keep no-learning and no-transfer worlds. Useful experience requires rights and validation; it never produces a separate financial premium on top of an already counted capability gain.

This H work joins the existing digital ecosystem, infrastructure, learning-data and onboard-workload evidence. Peak compute specifications and summed component timings remain insufficient for sustained deadline qualification. Embedded memory, remote inference and training resources retain their distinct grades and allocations. No new model, GPU or robot run is authorized or claimed by the CPU research fixture.

#### Required bridges before a combined economic ranking

The monthly enterprise case shares actual physical cohorts, customer assignment and cash. [The calendar bridge](research/calendar_digital_execution.md) now drives that loop with annual digital levels under a new explicit cleaning-zone contract, preserving cohort vintage, paid upgrade resources and dated expenses. The [funded architecture extension](research/monthly_intelligence_execution.md) now maps four intelligence profiles to actual role accounts and resource obligations for one active cleaning-service business. Neither bridge infers a historical floor-bundle conversion or imports the annual fleet. Broader integration must preserve these contracts:

| Bridge | Required rule | Failure the rule prevents |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Accepted work | Declare `work_id`, accepted unit, quality, geography/site and service window. A synthetic monthly floor-service bundle needs an explicit assumed conversion before relating it to annual equivalent hours. Hospital deliveries, components and measurement sets stay separate. | Summing incompatible activity or treating employment context as service demand |
| Time | Map calendar months to annual parameter levels without dividing a level or probability by twelve. State release/qualification dates and exposure windows; prorate only quantities whose units warrant it. | Retroactive capability, double uptime discounts or unexplained annual-to-monthly scaling |
| Fleet/cohort | Reuse actual commissioned/supported unit IDs. Retire, repair, replace and upgrade explicitly; preserve custody, title and configuration vintage. | Available robots created by a financial contract or old-generation evidence applied to new units |
| Architecture costs | Resolve each architecture's adaptation, training, inference, support and license obligation once. Allocate startup costs and monthly resources on declared dates; fees cancel only at the proper consolidation boundary. | Adding an allocated lifetime cost to already-paid monthly investment, or fees to the same provider resources |
| Support and learning | Allocate support and validation from finite shared pools before expansion. Tie learning/updates to eligible data, rights, paid resources, target evaluation and compatible cohorts. | Free maintenance, free learning, cross-embodiment transfer by label or duplicate data value |
| Finance | Fund a draw from a real counterparty; accrue interest by a stated convention, settle dated obligations, mature principal and apply an explicit default/cure/recovery policy. Keep liabilities after a missed payment. | Turning a commitment, receivable or unfunded loan into cash, or deleting an insolvent party's obligations |
| Reinvestment | Allow new starts only after operating/support and policy reserves, and after actual available funds pay the required obligations. | Reinvesting accounting profit or all future receivables before collection |
| Entry and ownership | Transfer existing customer assignments/assets and pay purchase/transition obligations for acquisition; create genuinely new demand only in a separate input. Hold technology access equal under prompt/delayed response. | Acquisition producing market growth or one demand unit served by both firms |

The finance contract now has a bounded implementation in [financed execution](research/financed_execution.md): funded draws, exact monthly interest, maturity, cash recovery, declared default/cure and a later-start reserve. A bounded unit/calendar/cohort/digital-cost bridge now executes with paid staffing and shared resource allocations. Four-architecture roles/costs now have a bounded funded implementation. Annual component-pool integration, broader competition, asset recovery and optimized reinvestment remain open. The other rules are implementation acceptance conditions, not a claim that all bridges exist. Target contracts remain unknown. Synthetic interest, support, rights and recovery terms can test a decision but must not be presented as available financing, legal advice, observed adoption or calibrated ROI.

#### Counterfactual challenge set

All fourteen named challenge families now have **bounded implementations across 187 complete worlds**, including thirteen combined architecture/resource executions. The shared finance, calendar, intelligence-role, exact conventional coordination and share-transition cases preserve their own service and actor boundaries. Eighteen [inspection worlds](model/results/utility_inspection.json) and eighteen [workcell worlds](model/results/automotive_workcell.json) add service-specific alternatives. Sixteen [learning worlds](model/results/learning_execution.json) preserve eligible data, paid physical qualification, retained cohorts and compatible module supply. Seventeen [renewal worlds](model/results/competitive_renewals.json) derive customer assignments from current funded capacity, firm prices, expiry and prior service. Sixteen [support/expansion worlds](model/results/support_expansion.json) distinguish announced capacity, actual resources, finite growth requests and payer cash. These are separate conditional executions, not additive deployment activity, empirical learning curves, a competitive equilibrium or full investment optimization. Complete inputs, source hashes, replay and relevant invariants identify their bounded scope; measured whole-service economics remain incomplete.

| Challenge family | Intervention and decision test |
| --- | --- |
| DE-CF01 retained-paid-shift — bounded implementation | Four matched branches deliver 4,180 synthetic zones and retain $38,400 of paid shifts despite sharply different task hours; staffing optimization and observed paid-work removal remain open |
| DE-CF02 urgent-human-fallback — bounded implementation | The exact mixed queue retains paid courier coverage; removing its reserve preserves four completions but misses mandatory service. Delay, urgent fast path, outages and full-attempt cash are explicit; real service evidence remains open |
| DE-CF03 residual-inspection-visit | Bounded executable: complete accepted-point coverage, review capacity, physical duties and actual paid commitments jointly determine whether a funded visit disappears |
| DE-CF04 fixed-cell-substitute | Bounded executable: fixed vision/buffering, competent humans, paid presentation and qualified generations meet or miss the same unique-kit deadlines; exact changeover and funding switches remain synthetic |
| DE-CF05 prompt-equal-tech-incumbent — bounded implementation | Current funded capacity, firm bids, switching/onboarding and prior service determine renewal; prompt response or delayed receipts can erase a synthetic entry cash window, and first crossing can reverse |
| DE-CF06 acquire-without-new-demand — bounded implementation | Funded share transfer changes ultimate owner while the target legal account, customers, physical units, leases, receivables and liabilities remain; price is not working capital |
| DE-CF07 hybrid-transition — bounded implementation | Separately funded growth uses only existing unmet/new demand; conditional closing, retained diligence, interruption and subsidiary liquidity are explicit; no automatic synergy or asset-purchase result |
| DE-CF08 architecture-and-fees — bounded implementation | Four funded role mappings, itemized resources, fee/internal-transfer controls, false rights gate and equal-capital allocation; real costs/rights and broader competition remain open |
| DE-CF09 qualified-supply-delay — bounded runtime-module implementation | Funded compatible configuration inputs arrive after qualification/service needs; full robot manufacturing/ramp remains open |
| DE-CF10 support-before-expansion — bounded implementation | Finite requests, actual support/components, announced allocations, known cohorts and payer cash admit or defer expansion. Reserves can preserve installed service but fail to fund commissioning; optimal fleet strategy and real support reliability remain open |
| DE-CF11 funding-maturity-recovery — bounded implementation | Funded/unfunded/delayed draws, dated interest/maturity, policy-dependent default/cure and actual cash recovery; eligible funded owner sale and same-unit redeployment now execute; legal priority, lease repossession and empirical valuation remain open |
| DE-CF12 funded-reinvestment — bounded implementation | A declared later start is gated by available cash and a reserve; no added service is invented, but autonomous candidate selection/optimization remains open |
| DE-CF13 digital-human-catches-up — bounded implementation | Dated human efficiency changes the robot increment in actual task hours; interaction is counted once. A separate exact courier-window comparator is now available; target competitive advantage and a common real service boundary remain open |
| DE-CF14 no-learning-no-transfer — bounded feedback implementation | Earlier unique eligible episodes fund an explicit candidate hypothesis; paid target/retained trials gate use. No-learning retains cost and no-transfer preserves prior configurations; empirical rates remain unknown |

Switch points should be stated in the parameter's actual unit and bounded by the tested policy. A one-cent synthetic funding boundary is an execution fact for that world, not optimal fundraising or a market offer. Ranking reversals need feasible alternatives at a common service boundary; incomparable alternatives should remain unranked. Every recommendation needs a falsifier that could cause it to change, including a simpler process that works better.

The service-specific automotive comparison is now implemented within its stated synthetic boundary. The next substantive work is target evidence for qualified supply/support, complete accepted service and retained work, with real eligibility, validation and retained-cohort obligations. The [share-transition extension](research/entry_transitions.md) now preserves operating identities and obligations through funded ownership change; it does not establish real valuations, legal transfer rights, asset redeployment or lifecycle returns. Apply the new ownership/cash tests within each real service boundary and retain missing support, qualified-supply, learning and all-actor cost evidence.


#### Utility service challenge, edition 0.19

The [inspection decision](research/utility_inspection_decision.md) now gives the utility pack a replayable service contract. Eleven of twelve remotely accepted points keep all visits; complete coverage still preserves committed crew pay, physical field duties or required presence. Fixed/mixed coverage and provider-funded resources are explicit alternatives. The $120 synthetic avoidable-crew-bill switch applies only to equal complete service and declared window bills, not real lifecycle economics. Proceed with qualification and observation design, wait on labor/rollout economics, and reject a particular substitution that drops a due obligation.

#### Automotive service challenge, edition 0.20

The [workcell comparison](research/automotive_workcell_decision.md) strengthens the conventional alternative with a primary fixed-vision/buffer reference while preserving different plants, parts and generations. In the synthetic worlds, real buffering and validated changeover can remove an apparent humanoid advantage; a competent human also meets the default service. Preparation consumes separate people and material readiness. Supported replacement requires a paid distinct qualified configuration and cannot repair missed deadlines. Proceed with a matched qualification protocol, wait on real complete operating economics, and reject a substitution that drops timing, transport, quality or support obligations. No source rate or lifecycle value is inferred.

#### R&D and qualification evidence gate, edition 0.21

[The learning follow-up](research/learning_qualification.md) now supplies an explicit chain from eligible data through candidate construction, target acceptance, old-cohort regression, integration and supported supply. Apply it to hospital urgency/custody, utility accepted point/review coverage and automotive complete-kit timing. Four proposed counterfactual contracts preserve costs and physical identities; they do not count as completed DE-CF09/10/14 implementations. Existing numerical outcomes and ten bounded challenge families remain unchanged.


#### Funded qualification challenge, edition 0.22

[Sixteen complete feedback worlds](research/learning_execution.md) now execute the prior source-derived contracts in a bounded workcell. Candidate success, earlier eligible data, compatible delivery, physical test slots and installed support remain separate gates. Transfer the qualification questions to hospital and utility service, not the automotive rates. Twelve families are bounded implementations; DE-CF10 remains partial beyond support-priority work on existing units.


#### Commercial evidence gate, edition 0.23

The [source audit](research/commercial_disclosure_boundaries.md) strengthens the prompt-incumbent comparison through operator continuation. All three decision packs inherit acceptance, version, support, rights and dated funding questions; none inherits warehouse costs or future-generation capability. Existing hospital, utility and automotive recommendations remain provisional, and no empirical investment threshold closes.


#### Funded renewals and incumbent response, edition 0.24

[Seventeen complete renewal worlds](research/competitive_renewals.md) add bounded DE-CF05, bringing coverage to thirteen families and 128 worlds. Actual supported capacity, firm prices, paid onboarding, expiry and prior service determine assignments; demand remains conserved. Bid-response timing holds the physical plan fixed, while delayed procurement and the human-led alternative remain separate comparators. First operating cash crossing may reverse. Locks preserve price and assignment but cannot guarantee funded delivery. All three packs inherit questions, not cleaning coefficients. DE-CF10 expanding-fleet support/investment and empirical whole-service economics remain partial. The same campaign continues.


#### Installed support and expansion commitments, edition 0.25

[Sixteen support/expansion worlds](research/support_expansion.md) add bounded DE-CF10 and complete implementation coverage of the fourteen named challenge families through 144 worlds. The shared engine preserves fixed demand, finite requests, actual installed service, rejected manufacture, components, paid staffing and one Treasury. Announced versus actual allocations and actor-specific cash can reverse a policy ranking; a support reserve is not full commissioning finance. Fresh validation satisfies the bounded counterfactual-execution requirement, while optimized fleet investment, real support contracts, rights, asset recovery and empirical full-cost acceptance remain incomplete. All three recommendations remain provisional. The original campaign clock continues.


#### Funded asset recovery, edition 0.28

[Fourteen owner-sale worlds](research/asset_recovery.md) deepen DE-CF11/12 on the existing dated physical/Treasury chain. Fourteen named families now contain 158 complete worlds. Title transfers only with actual eligible ownership, release/configuration permission, buyer funds and a shared shipping slot. The buyer must then qualify and support the same unit. Lender recovery can occur without new accepted service, and restored service can coexist with old unpaid principal. A stronger human buyer gains no additional accepted output. Customer/staff obligations stay at the old company. All three provisional recommendations now include this transfer-and-continuity gate without importing synthetic rates or prices. DE-04 remains partial for real priority, restructuring, valuation and optimized investment.


#### Contract continuity and finance denominators, edition 0.29

[The supplier-document challenge](research/asset_recovery.md#supplier-contract-challenge-edition-029) adds a distinct gate to all three provisional packs: usable operating rights and support after a transaction must be demonstrated separately from title or ultimate ownership. Its manufacturing AMR source is a structural comparator elsewhere. Unreconciled finance illustrations cannot price accepted service. The public form and source arithmetic add no synthetic world, target consent or empirical ROI; fourteen families / 158 worlds and broad partial acceptance remain.


#### Annual commitments and the human alternative, edition 0.30

[Eight resource worlds](research/annual_commitments.md) deepen the competitive and scarcity tests on one monthly physical/Treasury loop. Declared prior use and outside reservations constrain actual support, software qualification and commissioning by calendar year. A robot-service interruption changes the specified response rule and customer awards while funded human capacity preserves total market service; no payroll removal is inferred. All three packs now require dated usable allocations alongside their complete service and payer obligations. Fourteen families / 166 worlds remain bounded synthetic coverage. Outside workloads, annual component-vector conversion and broad joint portfolio execution remain incomplete. The original campaign continues.


#### Compatible components and dated maintenance, edition 0.31

[Eight component worlds](research/component_execution.md) connect a synthetic catalog manufacturing BOM to actual whole-part issues in the existing monthly physical/Treasury loop. Qualified and quarantined inventories, explicit replacement requirements, annual support ceilings and contingent funding remain separate constraints. No annual spare average becomes a repair schedule. Human/conventional capacity still tests the inference from lost robot service to lost total output. All three packs inherit the compatibility, receipt/qualification and funded continuity questions without importing synthetic parts or prices. Fourteen families / 174 worlds remain bounded coverage; target maintenance, supplier financing and joint annual portfolio supply remain incomplete.


#### Inspection readiness challenge, edition 0.32

The [inspection-readiness audit](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md#rollout-retained-work-and-robot-repair-edition-032) adds matched-service, retained-work and configured-support gates to all three packs. Cross-sector source questions transfer; rates, prices, rights and projected benefits do not. All broad requirements remain partial, and fourteen families / 174 worlds remain unchanged.

#### Intelligence and physical-resource integration, edition 0.33

[Thirteen combined architecture worlds](research/architecture_resources.md) advance the monthly/annual/four-architecture bridge. They share one service obligation, actual physical units, compatible parts, annual resource commitments and each architecture's original role accounts. Halving assumed open-model adaptation work permits upstream qualification but physical integration still delays the full fleet until January under the common reservation. The licensed two-unit threshold is exactly 10,500 integration minutes; one minute less delays a unit. Fixed paid humans deliver all 4,000 zones in every world, so neither greater robot exposure nor lower adaptation work proves removed payroll or added complete service. Previously paid manufacture stays sunk after later qualification failure.

Hospital, utility and automotive inherit the test for dated configured qualification, retained work, genuine shared-resource identities and payer funding. No cleaning quantity, hypothetical price or source entitlement transfers into those service units. Fourteen challenge families now contain **187 complete bounded worlds**; the independent cases are not one simultaneous portfolio. DE-03/06 and empirical whole-service acceptance remain partial. Full six-cell workloads, competition/acquisition/recovery/learning composition and actual economic evidence remain open.

#### Hospital economic claim gate, edition 0.34

The [existing hospital evidence package](research/field_support_denominators.md#hospital-economic-claims-edition-034) now challenges source-version, staffing and cost assumptions. Proceed with bounded service/workforce measurement when authorized and operationally feasible; wait on a rollout whose financing requires unverified paid staffing removal; reject a comparison that omits required service, retained work or transition/fallback costs. The utility and automotive packs inherit this measurement question only. Their service units, costs, staffing and preferred embodiments remain independently unresolved. This gate changes neither prior numerical scenarios nor empirical acceptance status.

#### Shared staffing and complete competing work, edition 0.35

[Nine workforce worlds](research/shared_workforce.md) deepen DE-CF01: one paid crew executes cleaning, robot attention/handling and separately required inventory jobs. A prior-service-based notice changes future paid blocks; funded extra capacity resolves some later scarcity. A one-minute shortfall can miss required cleaning, while an unfunded buyer creates no receipts despite delivery. The stronger human-dispatch premise remains much cheaper in the bounded window than the robot staffing-reduction case. Fourteen families now contain **196 worlds**; independent worlds are not one simultaneous portfolio.

All three packs inherit the need for actual shared-resource identity, complete competing obligations, transition/fallback and payer cash. These synthetic cleaning rates, inventory jobs, staffing terms and efficiency premises do not calibrate hospital, utility or automotive decisions. Whole-system empirical costs and broad integrated acceptance remain partial.


#### Workforce, service window and provider evidence, edition 0.36

Two [primary historical sources](research/field_support_denominators.md#employment-operating-windows-and-fallback-edition-036) test the staffing transition behind the deployment packs. Preserve anonymous configuration, observation period, expanded-building counterfactual and actual operating organization. Staff redeployment can be useful without current payroll removal; a manual contingency still needs available qualified people and cash. A provider handling bundled services does not reveal machine title, maintenance authority or profit. Proceed/wait/reject gates now require the receiving duties and fallback to remain jointly feasible. No schedule is promoted to measured robot exposure, and no source headcount/payback calibrates the model.

#### Fallback liquidity and the next integration boundary, edition 0.37

[The eighteen-world workforce package](research/shared_workforce.md#prospective-purchase-reserve-edition-037) now tests a prospective purchase floor, complete competing workloads and counterparty cash. Nine additions deepen DE-CF01; fourteen campaign families contain **205 worlds**. These are bounded comparisons, not an optimized policy or one joint portfolio. Existing six-cell evidence and all legacy scenario outcomes remain preserved.

| Decision pack / retained cell | What this bridge establishes conditionally | Next unresolved decision |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Hospital logistics | Payer liquidity and qualified fallback capacity must be checked separately after redeployment | Actual receiving-duty rosters, urgency/custody coverage and paid response terms |
| Utility inspection | Cash retained for mobile expansion need not supply the residual human route | Matched complete inspection/review work, mandatory visits and funded outage response |
| Automotive presentation | Refused purchase leaves prior production cost and stock at the OEM | Stable-takt conventional alternative, new-generation qualification and recovery of paid stranded work |
| Floor cleaning | One actual synthetic crew, two due services, prior-service notice and optional purchase floor execute together | Connect service/price retention to newco/incumbent and acquisition decisions without a second fleet or Treasury |
| 3PL tote handoff | A service fee or transaction label does not finance every provider role | Operator acceptance, real support/payment terms and retention at equal technology |
| Construction installation | Funds, worker authority and job completeness remain separate gates | Complete setup/installation obligations and digitally improved specialist/human baseline |

The integration queue is now explicit. First reconcile the competitive renewal and entry-transition interfaces with one service and cash history: preserve incumbent/entrant identities, inherited liabilities and cash-funded customer acquisition. Then join eligible recovery/relocation without treating paid title as supported operation. Finally test whether learning eligibility, failed-trial cost, qualified parts and architecture rights move that same business's service date, rather than citing a separate favorable learning world. The current workforce fixture has one active business and must not be relabeled as a competitor model; establish compatible contracts before composing modules. Keep all six cells and the three selected packs visible, and preserve substantial digital-AI ecosystem, physical supply and frontier-model work beyond this local cleaning execution.

#### Company entry under actual renewals, edition 0.38

Ten [joint entry/renewal worlds](research/entry_transitions.md#actual-renewal-and-company-entry-edition-038) preserve one physical/cash history, acquired company liabilities, funded growth and actual prior service. Equity reclassification, first new contract-lot awards, regained work and common-owner reassignment are separate. Fourteen challenge families now contain **215 bounded worlds**, with all previous outcomes preserved. The target's arrears can rise even when the combined market is fully served; acquisition funding is not operating solvency.

For each of the three service packs, verify operating consent and customer renewal/breach terms alongside payer budgets and complete required work. For the other three retained cells, keep equal technology and digitally improved incumbents in the entry comparison. Do not transfer synthetic cleaning contract prices or infer independent competition after common ownership. Next join eligible recovery and qualification to this transition boundary while preserving old liabilities and failed paid work. Shared staff, annual commitments, typed parts and model-rights/learning constraints remain separately executed until their contracts can be made compatible.

#### Funded recovery under actual customer commitments, edition 0.39

The [joint recovery/retention analysis](research/entry_transitions.md#funded-recovery-and-actual-retention-edition-039) advances DE-04/05 through original paid title, outside finance, common-owner legal accounts, debt cure, qualified reuse and locked customer commitments in one execution. Fourteen families now contain228 bounded worlds. Neither creditor recovery nor robot support implies added whole-market service; a paid human buyer or a separately funded target can preserve the obligation differently. Compare complete service before cash and retain outstanding claims and assets.
