Generated from [enterprise_finance.json](model/results/enterprise_finance.json). These are mutually exclusive worlds in the same synthetic floor-service unit, not additive activity or empirically calibrated decision packs. No debt default does not ensure funded support. Cash recovery preserves remaining claims and does not sell assets. Fourteen worlds implement bounded DE-CF11/12 mechanisms; they do not satisfy the campaign requirement for broad counterfactual coverage.

### Focused follow-up: Digital paths, cohorts and the operating calendar

Authoritative analysis: [research/calendar_digital_execution.md](research/calendar_digital_execution.md).

Decision checkpoint 0.15 · synthetic mechanism study · 7 November 2026. [Methods and equations](model/CALENDAR_BRIDGE.md) and [complete worlds](model/results/calendar_bridge.json) specify the assumptions. The [financing tranche](research/financed_execution.md) supplies dated cash/default rules. No benchmark score, advertised rate, real financing offer or customer labor saving is used as a numerical coefficient.

The annual model previously showed how a moving digital-human baseline changed robotics' incremental value, while the monthly model represented actual cohorts and funded execution in a different service unit. Merely displaying their outputs together could not establish that an annual improvement was available to a particular installed robot or financed at a particular date. The new bridge supplies a declared cleaning-zone unit, calendar mapping, cohort policy and cost/resource contract inside the existing monthly loop. It does not append the annual fleet to the monthly stock or pretend the historical floor bundle had a known conversion.

#### Equal service can coexist with very different task time and unchanged payroll

Four ample-capital branches use the same paid staffing and customer-move policy: the human process, digital-human process, robot-assisted process and combined process. Each delivers **4,180 accepted cleaning zones** over the eight-month horizon. The incumbent and entrant together commit and pay **$38,400** of staffing cash in each branch.

Human task time nevertheless changes substantially. The human baseline uses 1,337.6 task hours; the digital-human branch uses 1,136.96. Robot-assisted branches leave much less human task time, while paying the same shifts. The combined branch's remaining task time is about 29.08 hours. None of these changes automatically removes a paid coverage commitment or creates extra customer demand. Released time could have value in a different assignment, avoid future hiring or support growth, but that requires an explicit change in service, demand or staffing policy.

The robot branches also fund actual manufacturing, transport, integration and continuing support. Five paid starts do not become five newly productive deployments: after the existing stock, allocation, support and demand constraints, some finished units remain unused. Opening robots have prior assets and integration outside this cash window. Consequently, the observed-period outlays are neither lifecycle cost nor an optimal investment ranking. The test establishes that a task-hour saving alone does not settle cash savings under the declared operating policy.

The four-way interaction is retained as a diagnostic difference in task hours, not added as another premium. Human digital efficiency and robot-assisted release can overlap. The robot increment on the digital-human alternative is the relevant matched difference; the baseline's prior digital improvement cannot be claimed again as a robot benefit. This continues substantial H's moving-baseline inquiry through actual dated execution, while leaving the stronger conventional coordination comparator unfinished.

#### A calendar improvement is not a free upgrade to every installed robot

The standard window begins in July 2026 and ends in February 2027. The path therefore crosses an annual boundary after six months. Output rolls up into six observed months in 2026 and two in 2027; neither is extrapolated into an annual forecast.

In the improving path, human efficiency, robot coordination and reference inference expense change at January. Installed robots can use the changed capability/coordination level only under a declared cohort policy. Frozen cohorts retain their qualified robot level and subscription-rate vintage. The calendar-upgrade policy books a synthetic $500 payment and two qualification hours for each changed robot. These are assumptions about the service contract; no real model supplier's update charge, rights or qualification process is inferred.

The resource boundary changes execution. Five installed units require about 54.17 support hours per month. Upgrading all five adds ten qualification hours. With a 60-hour shared allocation, only four units can be supported and upgraded in January. The fifth retains its identity and older vintage and can qualify later. With 65 hours, all five fit in the same month. Paid human fallback maintains the same accepted service in these ample-capital worlds, so the scarce-upgrade world uses more human task time rather than inventing extra output or counting the blocked robot as productive.

A stronger robot level need not create an immediate realized gain when demand is already served. The higher-support allocation makes the upgrade feasible; it does not by itself make the upgrade economically valuable. Future demand, resilience or workload diversity could change that conclusion, but none is booked as free value here. Conversely, a price-only subscription change can affect cash without requiring a fictitious performance qualification event. An unchanged robot level does not incur a qualification charge just because the calendar turned.

Initial commissioning has its own time constraint. Three arrivals need 210 integration hours at the assumed 70 hours each. A 200-hour monthly allocation admits two, then the remaining unit can commission in a later month. A shipping invoice already paid stays paid. Neither annual capacity nor later available cash retroactively completes that earlier work.

#### Funding is part of the bridge

The physical support booking, first rent where relevant, software subscription and upgrade qualification settle atomically for a supported unit. If the actor is one cent short on the software portion, the whole contingent booking is cancelled: no physical payment remains, no support hours are consumed and the software vintage does not advance. Fixed staffing and site-subscription obligations have their own payment rules and remain visible.

The low-capital financing world delivers 4,150 zones rather than 4,180 and ends with an actual debt-policy default. Its missing 30 final-period zones are not removed from demand or credited to an imaginary fallback. It preserves the debt and the robot stock. A separately favorable annual capability or unit-cost result would not reveal that dated failure without the common Treasury and service ledger.

Every added digital or qualification payment reconciles to the outside supplier and is included once in enterprise outlay. Annual manufacturing cost applies to actual paid starts, including rejected production; an expected-yield divisor is not charged a second time. The physical operating bundle excludes the separately priced digital addition before the latter is introduced. Whole-month billing and per-invoice rounding are disclosed, while principal and interest retain their distinct financing treatment.

#### Implications and remaining challenge

For hospital logistics, the useful transfer is the distinction between a new policy level, a qualified installed cohort and an actual funded service period. Retained urgent coverage or escort staffing cannot be eliminated merely because task time falls. The cleaning-zone coefficient is not a hospital delivery estimate.

For utility inspection, an improved inspection policy still needs a qualified configuration, access and support at the relevant time. A frozen cohort is a useful adverse case, but it is not evidence that any operator actually keeps an old version or lacks rights. Mandatory retained visits stay outside a fictional automatic labor saving.

For automotive presentation, initial line integration and later policy qualification consume distinct dated resources. A faster nominal policy cannot meet a takt obligation until the relevant unit is commissioned, supported and admitted. Neither the synthetic mobile-manipulator body nor its cleaning rate establishes automotive suitability.

This checkpoint advances the retained-paid-shift and digital-human challenge families and supplies a bounded frozen-cohort/no-transfer test. It does not close endogenous learning, four intelligence architectures or the exact stronger conventional baseline. The next useful work is to map intelligence resources, fees and legal-entity roles into the same dated ledger, then challenge coordination with equal information, authority and resources. Acquisition/hybrid transitions and actual whole-system evidence remain required. Existing empirical unknowns and provisional decision gates stay open.

#### Reproduced calendar and cohort worlds

| Synthetic calendar world | Accepted zones, 8 months | Retained human task hours | Staffing committed, USD | External physical/operating cash outlays, USD | Supported robot-months | Qualified upgrades |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| C00 human baseline | 4,180.00 | 1,337.60 | 38,400.00 | 79,550.00 | 0 | 0 |
| C01 digital human | 4,180.00 | 1,136.96 | 38,400.00 | 80,350.00 | 0 | 0 |
| C10 robot base | 4,180.00 | 38.10 | 38,400.00 | 296,087.57 | 33 | 0 |
| C11 robot digital | 4,180.00 | 29.08 | 38,400.00 | 301,012.57 | 33 | 0 |
| calendar improving | 4,180.00 | 35.32 | 38,400.00 | 302,446.28 | 32 | 5 |
| frozen cohorts | 4,180.00 | 29.08 | 38,400.00 | 301,012.57 | 33 | 0 |
| upgrade hours available | 4,180.00 | 29.08 | 38,400.00 | 303,262.57 | 33 | 5 |
| calendar financing pressure | 4,150.00 | 115.04 | 38,400.00 | 292,406.12 | 28 | 4 |

Generated from [calendar_bridge.json](model/results/calendar_bridge.json). The four C00/C01/C10/C11 branches share a paid-staff/customer-move policy and ample capital. Cash outlays include actual starts and retained inventory; opening assets predate the cash window. They are not lifecycle costs or an optimal investment ranking. Partial years are aggregated without extrapolation; no historical floor-bundle conversion or extra annual fleet is assumed.

### Focused follow-up: Funded acquisition, inherited obligations and hybrid entry

Authoritative analysis: [research/entry_transitions.md](research/entry_transitions.md).

A platform can buy an existing service business without creating a new customer, robot or accepted unit of output. That distinction now executes in the [funded entry-transition model](model/ENTRY_TRANSITIONS.md), using the existing monthly cleaning-zone, cohort, customer and Treasury loop. Fifteen complete worlds add bounded **DE-CF06 acquisition without new demand** and **DE-CF07 hybrid transition**. They are invented contract sensitivities, not empirical company valuations or a recommendation to transact.

#### Purchase price does not capitalize the operating company

The reference target serves 500 recurring synthetic zones per month with two existing leased robots and retained paid staffing. Status quo and a funded share acquisition both deliver 4,000 zones over eight months. They retain exactly the same physical events, units, service ledger and target operating cash. Increasing the hypothetical share price from $100,000 to $150,000 moves another $50,000 from buyer to seller without changing production or consolidated resource outlays. It leaves the buyer less liquid. No terminal equity value is supplied, so neither the cash table nor the output table ranks the investments.

Share ownership changes in month two only after the actual closing group settles. The lessor still owns the robots; the target company remains their custodian and operating counterparty. Its pre-close receivables still pay that company, and an existing $30,000 payable is not erased or reassigned to the seller. In the liability stress case, the funded acquisition closes while overdue obligations halt target service. A positive target bank balance does not establish readiness when a full required invoice cannot settle.

With zero target opening cash, the $100,000 price can still reach the seller while the target delivers zero service. A separate $25,000 working-capital injection at month two restores 3,000 later zones, not the 1,000 missed in the first two months. Consideration and operating capitalization solve different constraints. The injection must itself be financed by the buyer; no target cash, paid customer invoice or hypothetical share value is available to pay the purchase price automatically.

#### Closing is a funded event with retained failed-deal costs

The reference pays $1,000 for prior diligence and $5,000 for conditional transition resources. Buyer opening cash of $106,000 exactly funds those bills plus the $100,000 consideration; one cent less blocks the whole contingent closing. No partial seller payment, transition payment, target injection or ownership change survives a failed candidate settlement. The already incurred diligence remains. Missing assumed transfer consent also blocks closing while retaining diligence cost. These are declared synthetic terms, not statements of legal priority or market deal practice.

A one-month assumed service interruption after successful closing loses 500 zones. The target's $32,000 eight-month paid staffing commitment and physical unit identities remain. Smaller operating cash outlays during an interruption can reflect work that was never supported, not improved efficiency. The model does not invent fallback coverage or recover the missed service later.

#### Hybrid adds an operating leg rather than duplicating the base

The four route comparisons each begin with $200,000 of buyer cash. Organic build commits $60,000 to a separate company, obtains robots through actual funded supply/installation/support and schedules customer moves. It captures 70 baseline zones and assigns 150 unmet/new zones that were already in the market ledger. It delivers 4,750 total zones over the window. The seller-owned incumbent loses only the explicitly reassigned customers; its existing staffing and assets remain.

Hybrid pays for the target and separately funds the new company. The acquired 500-zone baseline stays assigned to the target; only 150 existing unmet/new zones move to the growth company. The standard hybrid delivers 4,717 zones. Its narrower growth-company sales leave a late liquidity shortfall even while cash remains at the parent. Moving another $5,000 of the same opening parent capital into that company restores 4,750 zones without adding robots or demand. This is a cash-allocation sensitivity within a fixed policy, not an optimal capitalization rule.

The independently committed growth leg can proceed even when the share closing fails. That adverse world explicitly remains an organic growth position with a failed acquisition, rather than being relabeled a successful hybrid platform. Acquisition itself earns no automatic productivity, staffing or data-learning bonus. The reference also retains fixed prompt/delayed incumbent responses: a funded return of 70 baseline customers changes assignment and collections, not aggregate demand. Competing onboarding costs and cash pressure can change total delivered service. These cases do not calculate acquisition payback, prove a durable entry advantage or optimize the incumbent's response.

#### Implication for the decision packs and thesis T3

For hospital, utility and automotive entry, separate three questions: whether the existing company owns or controls the relevant service relationships; whether its operating obligations and technical rights survive the proposed transaction; and whether actual funds can support the desired new physical process. The cleaning-zone cases provide conservation and failure mechanisms only. They do not import their prices, durations, costs, customer-transfer ability or approvals into another service.

Thesis T3 is strengthened: equal technology access and aggregate capital leave customer assignment, legal accounts, working capital and service continuity unresolved. Acquisition can buy control of existing activity; growth still requires additional funded capacity and customers within a conserved market. The strongest counterargument remains that a real platform could improve processes or capital allocation materially. Evidence of those improvements must be measured against inherited obligations and the incumbent's feasible response, not supplied by an acquisition label.

The next work shifts to the unresolved service-specific alternatives and qualification evidence, starting with utility residual visits/fixed sensing and the automotive workcell comparator. Real all-actor costs, retained paid work, qualified supply, operating rights, support and endogenous learning remain incomplete. Asset purchase/redeployment, legal restructuring, valuation and broad market equilibrium are outside this bounded share-transfer implementation. The active 168-hour campaign clock is unchanged.

#### Actual renewal and company entry, edition 0.38

The initial entry model preserved legal entities and funded closing, but customer movements were scheduled scenario inputs. The [joint execution](model/results/renewing_entry.json) now subjects status quo, standalone build, share acquisition and hybrid formation to the existing funded renewal rule inside one physical and Treasury run. Ten worlds deepen DE-CF06/07 and the renewal challenges; fourteen families contain 215 bounded worlds. All earlier numerical cases remain unchanged.

A lot represents a recurring service contract with an identified operator, locked price and expiry. Awards require currently funded capacity and paid onboarding/switching work; later renewal may respond to actual prior service. The equity transaction occurs in phase two, before physical work and phase-five renewal. It changes ultimate ownership, while target cash, asset title, receivables, inherited bills and existing contract terms remain with the same legal company. The purchase uses the parent's available cash after any separately committed growth funding and prior diligence. It does not borrow the target's bank balance.

**Buying contracts is not winning them through service.** The acquisition-only world reclassifies 500 zones of recurring contract volume at closing, gains no new contract lots and delivers the same 4,000 zones as status quo. Share consideration is $100,000 to the seller, and diligence/transition resources are $6,000. That changes ownership and cash, not production. Terminal equity value and returns remain unknown.

The standalone build first wins 100 zones of recurring lot volume, but ends with no assigned lots after the declared incumbent response. Equal access to supported robots does not preserve the entrant's initial price advantage under these offer rules. The hybrid world first wins 100 zones of recurring lot volume through the separate operating entrant. At closing, 400 zones of the target's existing contract volume are reclassified to the platform. A later 100-zone transfer between commonly owned operating firms is internal reassignment, not another external customer win. Both firms continue separately declared offer mandates after common ownership. This explicit organizational premise is not independent competition, optimal group pricing or a claimed real market outcome.

A one-month target interruption in the hybrid case reduces that month's delivery from 500 to 200 zones. Later service returns to 500, but the 300 missed zones remain lost. With three-month locked contracts and no breach exit, the same interruption leaves only 100 zones delivered in that month and 400 permanently missed. Both settings have seven complete months. Gross later awards from unassigned contracts include recovered work; new-platform contract-lot IDs count first awards only and exclude earlier platform ownership or equity reclassification. Neither measure counts unique people or inferred customer acquisition.

**Complete market service can coexist with an unfunded acquired company.** An inherited $30,000 target bill remains due to the original counterparty and is eventually paid. In the uninjected case, other target operating obligations then accumulate to $15,100 due and unpaid by the final month, while the entrant takes more internal work and the overall market still receives all 4,000 zones. A separately funded $30,000 target equity injection avoids those closing arrears in the matched case, leaving the parent only $4,000. The acquisition price itself never supplied that operating money. Named-bill settlement is therefore insufficient evidence that the company is fully funded.

The parent also faces a binding closing boundary. With $60,000 committed to growth, $1,000 diligence, $100,000 consideration and $5,000 transition resources, $166,000 of parent opening funds closes; $165,999.99 does not. Failed closing retains diligence and the separately funded growth leg, pays no partial consideration/transition/equity group and changes no target ownership. In the saved $160,000 case, growth continues, the acquisition fails and the parent closes with $99,000. No closing retry or automatic cash sweep is invented.

The stronger human/competitive-response case keeps the same paid staff and software charge, assumes higher digital-human efficiency and no new robots, and uses the declared incumbent response offer. It delivers all 4,000 zones while the entrant wins no new lots. Its bounded external operating/transaction cash is $71,700, versus $253,408.38 for standalone robotic build and $259,408.38 for the base hybrid. These remain conditional window cash comparisons, with different retained assets and no terminal valuation, empirical uplift or lifecycle ROI.

For hospital, utility and automotive packs, acquisition and operating-contract eligibility are separate gates. Verify actual control/consent, inherited obligations, funding after transaction costs, service interruption, renewal or breach rights and the competent incumbent alternative before interpreting acquired revenue as entry advantage. The numerical cleaning prices, contracts, staff and disruption assumptions do not transfer. The joint model still lacks the shared crew, annual/typed-part constraints, four-architecture roles, asset recovery and endogenous learning from other modules; it does not establish a simultaneously feasible six-cell portfolio.

#### Funded recovery and actual retention, edition 0.39

The [combined recovery execution](model/results/recovering_entry.json) joins company entry, real scheduled payments, robot ownership, debt default, relocation, qualification and actual customer renewal in one physical/Treasury history. It adds thirteen bounded challenges inside the existing families, bringing their total to228. The common-owner firms retain separate declared offer mandates and legal bank accounts. This is neither optimal restructuring nor an empirical estimate of acquisition/recovery value.

**The robot has a paid history.** The standard case opens with no installed or finished robot. The provider spends $36,000 manufacturing one identified unit. An outside lender actually advances $60,000 to the target; the target later pays the provider $60,000 for the robot, which ships in month1 and is commissioned in month2. The parent separately buys the target's shares in month2. A subsequent asset sale names that same unit, a distinct operating-company buyer, actual title, current default, explicit release/configuration rights, $30,000 consideration, $1,000 relocation and two months to arrival. Neither share ownership nor a loan claim grants a right to sell a lessor's robot. The lease comparison has different actual payment flows and no target debt default here; it is a title-refusal control, not a matched default experiment.

**Cash cure can restore service before physical reuse.** With no sale, the target defaults in month4 and the eight-month market receives2,400 of4,000 zones, with $20,732.58 principal still owed. With the funded $30,000 sale, the target remains in default in the sale month because debt servicing already occurred. Next month's scheduled settlement pays its remaining debt and restores its human operation. The whole market returns to500 zones in month5; the relocated robot first qualifies and receives support in month6. The window totals3,600 zones: the400 missed in month4 are never restored. Sale price is an internal transfer, while principal payment actually returns funds to the outside lender.

The destination may never qualify the robot, or it may arrive after the observation window. Both cases retain the paid sale and relocation, leave a buyer-owned stranded unit, repay the target debt and still deliver the same3,600 total zones through the recovered human operation. Therefore the whole-market service recovery in this fixture cannot be attributed to successful robot reuse. Actual operator allocation and expenditures differ: all-payer resource/transaction cash is $137,315.16 with successful qualification and $127,582.58 with no qualification. Those are conditional window outlays with different retained assets, not lifecycle cost or returns.

A lower $20,000 sale price permits physical reuse in month6 but leaves the target in default with $5,782.58 of principal outstanding. Only2,850 zones are delivered. Thus successful robot relocation does not itself cure the seller or preserve the complete service obligation. With locked three-month customer terms and no breach exit, the $30,000 transfer case loses500 zones in its default month rather than400; later cash and service recovery leave that shortfall intact.

**The purchase boundary is not an operating-funding boundary.** $46,050 of initial growth-company equity leaves exactly $31,000 available at the month4 offer, enough for purchase plus initial relocation. One cent less refuses both contingent payments and leaves title unchanged. The exact-funded purchase nonetheless empties the buyer's operating funds, leaves the robot uncommissioned at the horizon and delivers only3,400 zones. This is the same distinction highlighted by the earlier fallback-reserve investigation: funds committed to an asset can remove the cash needed for service. The separate workforce/resource execution has not been silently joined here.

A $30,000 parent-to-target equity injection at the earlier share closing prevents target default, so the recovery policy refuses an unnecessary owner sale. All4,000 zones are served; the parent retains only $4,000. The operating companies, parent and financier face different cash positions from the asset-sale route. This declared prior action is not an optimized funding policy, an automatic group cash pool or evidence of a real financing offer.

The competent human-buyer pair pays the entrant the same $4,000 monthly staff budget as the incumbent and recomputes capacity from the existing wage/task/calendar mapping. With or without the later robot purchase it serves all4,000 zones. Buying the robot raises all-payer resource/transaction outlay from $146,632.58 to $158,565.16 without adding accepted service, while returning more actual cash to the lender and reducing its remaining principal claim. Creditor recovery and service productivity are different outcomes. The digital-human mapping and fixed firm offers remain synthetic; no observed staffing removal or digital uplift is inferred.

The all-payer figures above add outside customer switching resources to the existing fixed operator/provider/parent/seller boundary. They count manufacture, paid staffing/software and transaction/relocation/qualification resources once. Internal hardware and later asset prices, equity and loan principal remain separately traced transfers; outstanding operating bills, interest, credit claims and physical assets are retained, not converted to profit or terminal value.

For hospital logistics, utility inspection and automotive presentation, verify ownership and configuration/support rights separately from company control; reconstruct actual original asset funding and every payer; and test retained service while sale, debt cure, relocation and requalification occur at different times. Software/model access, customer acceptance, residual physical duties and provider support belong in those gates. The same method challenges cleaning, tote handoff and construction; no cleaning price, staffing ratio or deadline transfers to those services. Shared crew, annual resources, typed parts, four intelligence architectures, endogenous learning and a simultaneously feasible six-cell portfolio remain separately executed or incomplete. Actual matched full-service costs, labor transitions, rights and residual values remain empirical gaps.

#### Qualified crews constrain actual customer wins, edition 0.41

[The shared-crew/renewal extension](research/shared_workforce.md#paid-crews-and-actual-customer-awards-edition-041) now places funded offers, retained outside duties, staffing notice and actual customer awards inside this entry execution. Common ownership keeps separate qualified crews and operating accounts. A better staffed rival can preserve the cleaning market while inventory obligations of the original company fail. Buying a company therefore requires both customer continuity and a feasible roster for inherited duties. The stronger human/no-robot comparison and every payer remain explicit; this is synthetic mechanism evidence, not a staffing or acquisition recommendation.

#### Forward coverage after a staffing commitment, edition 0.44

The [qualified-staffing extension](research/shared_workforce.md#qualified-support-parts-and-binding-staffing-edition-044) composes shared provider support and compatible parts with the existing acquired/entrant companies, distinct crews and actual awards. A resource shortage can break service after binding notice; a second robot does not create another provider pool. Common ownership still supplies neither pooled crews nor automatically transferable cash or rights. The acquisition/build decision must carry inherited duties and a forward qualified/funded service plan. Real contracts and values remain unknown.

### Focused follow-up: Operating rollout, contract value and provider funding

Authoritative analysis: [research/commercial_disclosure_boundaries.md](research/commercial_disclosure_boundaries.md).

Edition 0.23 extends the existing F/G customer package through FG-E019–FG-E023 and FG-AUDIT-01. It changes the evidence supporting deployment gates, without changing synthetic coefficients. The inference is that operating continuation, customer value and provider survival require three different demonstrations. None can substitute for the others.

#### What the new sources establish

[GXO's 26 October announcement](https://gxo.com/news_article/gxo-and-agility-robotics-expand-partnership-to-accelerate-deployment-of-humanoids-in-u-s-operations/) describes continued Flowery Branch operation and planned additional sites. Its site tote count and Agility's broader operating-hour count have different populations. Their quotient would not measure a site's rate. Additional deployments begin on Digit 4, with later Digit 5 migration planned. This strengthens the existing prompt-incumbent counterexample in FG-CMP-01; it does not establish completed migration or matched payroll savings.

The [6 October investor presentation](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/2074973/000121390026107259/ea030784201ex99-1.htm), visually checked at slides 45, 57 and 66, separates customer illustrations, generation-specific BOM and provider product economics. Its BOM-recovery definition of provider payback omits costs that still need funding. The [13 October prospectus](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/2074973/000121390026109058/ea0297114-07.htm) supplies explicit planning components and historical company statements. They support a reproducible boundary audit, not an executed price quote or measured site return. The generated report and dashboard show the arithmetic from those saved records.

The [5 November closing update](https://investors.agilityrobotics.com/news-releases/news-release-details/agility-becomes-first-us-publicly-listed-pure-play-humanoid) changes financing status from expected to completed. Its aggregate includes earlier funding and is stated before transaction expenses. Subtracting the earlier component prevents one obvious double count but does not reconstruct the closing bank balance. Historical operating cash use remains informative about the earlier business; it is not a forecast of future burn or a post-close liquidity verdict.

The [22 October compute announcement](https://investors.agilityrobotics.com/news-releases/news-release-details/agility-robotics-expands-nvidia-collaboration-it-prepares-digit) concerns the planned general-availability Digit 5 configuration and evaluation before final certification. It identifies a dependency for qualification, not a measured improvement in accepted service. It must not be retroactively assigned to the operating Digit 4 cohort.

#### Three gates, with different failure modes

**Operating gate.** A buyer needs unique accepted handoffs on time, matched demand and exposure, a specified configuration, and all retained work. Aggregate counts can show that a deployment persisted while remaining insufficient to price a unit of service. A new location also requires its own layout, systems integration, staff procedure and safety acceptance. Expansion therefore increases the importance of a repeatable qualification process; it does not eliminate it.

**Commercial gate.** The relevant contract must allocate acceptance, payment start, service interruption, upgrade costs, termination and residual equipment responsibility. A paid evaluation program can be valuable engineering work without becoming a recurring productive deployment. Its fee, a per-robot deployment charge and a service subscription represent different obligations. No program charge should be silently assigned to the earlier GXO contract. The source's five-year illustration also cannot turn a conditional three-year order into five years of assured collections.

**Provider funding gate.** Hardware construction, deployment work, service delivery, software development and corporate operations require cash on different dates. A subscription can be attractive over a hypothetical life yet fail to finance its initial hardware or a support surge. Conversely, a company's development-stage losses do not by themselves prove that one mature site has unfavorable incremental economics. A fair assessment keeps both levels visible and asks what share of shared development/support is avoidable, recurrent or required for the next qualified cohort.

The reproducible payment-component totals deliberately omit unpriced customer work and financing. The provider product-cost subtotal deliberately excludes corporate costs, consistent with the source boundary. They are not consolidated resource totals: the customer fee is a transfer to the provider, while provider labor and hardware are uses of resources. Add the underlying resources once when consolidating; keep the fee in each actor's cash account. Neither a product margin nor a whole-company loss divided by a missing robot denominator resolves the full-service cost.

#### Substantial H: software is an operating and qualification obligation

This case links digital work to deployment through three distinct layers: onboard policy and safety computation, fleet/site orchestration and diagnostics, and R&D that produces a candidate update. The prospectus's Arc discussion provides context for the operating layer, including local sensory processing and diagnostic transmission. It does not establish that every observation is exported as reusable training data or that customer data rights permit cross-site training.

The decision question is whether an update improves the constrained accepted service after qualification and ongoing support. In the [funded learning worlds](research/learning_execution.md), hypothetical candidates must earn their place through paid tests and retained-cohort checks. The present source audit adds a real reason to maintain that separation: commercial generations and compute configurations are changing while an existing cohort continues to operate. The synthetic worlds do not forecast Agility's update success, migration time or costs.

A buyer should be able to specify the supported version, update approval and rollback, the operating envelope during network loss, incident responsibility and the data needed to reconcile a failed task. These are proposed contract/qualification questions, not findings that the product lacks those capabilities. A provider should separately budget ongoing software and support from the research program that may unlock new tasks. Combining them into an inference-token price would conceal the engineering and service obligations that determine scale.

#### Consequences for the existing deployment and entry decisions

| Decision | Proceed gate | Wait or reject trigger |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Existing tote workflow | Continue a bounded site evaluation once acceptance, retained work, exposure and funded support are observable. | Wait on expansion valuation when hours, schedules or contract terms cannot be matched; reject a cost-per-tote calculation using fleet hours from another population. |
| Hospital logistics | Apply the same service, interruption and funded-support questions to the hospital's complete request and handoff workflow. | Do not transfer warehouse rates, generation readiness, labor assumptions or prices to hospital service. |
| Utility inspection | Price complete accepted measurements, residual visits and response obligations for the chosen configuration. | A mobile-platform financing headline cannot remove physical duties or replace inspection qualification. |
| Automotive workcells | Qualify the actual generation, end effector, presentation and retained task mix before scaling. | Wait when a future compute/safety configuration is required for the proposed workflow; retain the qualified conventional comparator. |

For a standalone operating entrant, supplier funding is helpful context but does not provide the entrant's working capital, customer demand or implementation authority. An acquisition can bring those relationships while also importing obligations. A hybrid can pool specialist integration and service capacity, but its fees and incident authority need an explicit allocation. GXO's continuation means prompt incumbent adoption is a live competitive possibility; the entrant cannot claim a durable technology-access advantage from a public robot offering alone.

These findings reinforce staged deployment and actor-specific funding rather than a market-wide humanoid ranking. The three principal campaign recommendations remain provisional. No new empirical whole-system-cost requirement is closed.

#### Reproduction and stopping rule

`commercial_disclosure_summary` in [research_calculations.py](model/research_calculations.py) reads typed quantities in [customer_evidence.json](data/customer_evidence.json). The normal scenario exporter refreshes [observed_research_summary.json](model/results/observed_research_summary.json). Exact equations and source identifiers accompany each calculation. Null customer ROI/payback, paid labor removal, site rate, net closing cash and runway remain null. Historical periods are not annualized.

Reopen this source branch when a public executed service agreement, matched site operating/staffing record, qualified Digit 5 release or post-close financial statement changes a gate. Repeated announcements are not new independent cohorts. Further public searching without such a trigger has low decision value; advance the remaining cross-cell execution challenges instead. No outreach or commercial transaction was undertaken. See the [sole-writer review](reviews/commercial_disclosures_review.md).

### Focused follow-up: Funded renewals and the entry cash window

Authoritative analysis: [research/competitive_renewals.md](research/competitive_renewals.md).

Edition 0.24, continuation of the same Deployment and Entry Decision campaign. The accepted input is edition 0.23 at `133903316c3710fbfb288934baa05722a4f8cc03`. All results below are deliberately synthetic cleaning-service executions. They neither estimate a target customer's contracts nor calibrate robots from the recent commercial disclosures.

Equal access to robots leaves three distinct questions: can the entrant win a recurring service, can it keep delivering it, and do actual collections recover its recorded operating outlays? The [renewal extension](model/COMPETITIVE_RENEWALS.md) now derives assignments from funded capacity, locked prices, expiry and prior service. Earlier scheduled customer moves remain preserved in their original cases. This bounded addition advances **DE-CF05**, without claiming an equilibrium or a general investment ranking.

#### A price-response window is not a cash-recovery window

The common reference has 500 recurring zones per month, ten identified fifty-zone contracts, two robots requested by each business, paid staff and the same financed supply. The incumbent's default $29 bid becomes $25 when qualified supported robots are available; the entrant bids $27. Switching work, onboarding, formation, collections, support and fixed obligations remain. The four primary timing worlds have identical physical plans, installed unit identities, qualified cohorts and support allocations. Assigned demand and actual human/robot use may differ. They change only the incumbent response rule or its earliest month.

| Twelve-month world | Entrant accepted zones | First cash break-even | Entrant operating cash after recorded bills at close |
|---|---:|---|---:|
| Prompt supported-robot response, first month 2 | 200 | Not observed | −$87,884.51 |
| Response first in month 7 | 2,400 | Not observed | −$34,684.51 |
| Response first in month 10 | 3,900 | Month 8; maintained through month 11 | $4,315.49 |
| No incumbent response | 4,900 | Month 8; maintained through month 11 | $16,815.49 |

All four deliver 6,000 market zones in total; customer transfers never add market activity. The date convention is zero based. These are outcomes of declared prices and commitments, not a predicted competitive margin or a universal response deadline. The nonresponsive incumbent still delivers residual work; it is not removed from the market.

The no-response world observed only through month seven delivers 2,900 entrant zones but ends at **−$5,054.19**, with uncollected invoices. Extending actual execution to month eleven yields the positive value above. Keeping twelve months but delaying entrant receipts from one to three months preserves 4,900 accepted entrant zones and changes terminal operating cash after bills to **−$10,184.51**. Booked revenue and accepted output cannot finance an earlier obligation before settlement. No annualization or additional observed period is invented.

Delayed procurement is a separate world because it changes shared unit allocation and the entrant's own commissioning timing. With incumbent procurement delayed to month seven, response occurs in month eight. The entrant first crosses zero in month eight, then ends at **−$21,425.80**. A first cash crossing is not durable recovery. The initial incumbent reference similarly crosses in month one, falls below zero again and only remains nonnegative from month five. Report both dates rather than choosing the favorable one.

#### Customer protection and capacity protection are different

A six-month contract prevents unilateral repricing. The entrant keeps its initial 100 recurring zones through month five, delivering 600 entrant zones overall, versus 200 under monthly renewals. Yet it ends at **−$77,484.51**. The same lock also prevents it from competing for the incumbent's later-expiring lots when new capacity arrives. Term length is not an automatic one-way advantage for entry.

One month's price difference on a fifty-zone lot is exactly $100. At that switching-cost tie the incumbent retains the customer; at $99.99 the entrant can win its two initially fundable lots. The one-cent witness tests a declared contract rule, not the empirical precision of a buyer preference. Onboarding must also be affordable. An unfunded onboarding bid pays neither fictitious onboarding nor switching resources and leaves the customer with the next funded eligible provider. Separate tests make buyer funding fail after recipient onboarding would otherwise have been affordable; atomic admission prevents a stranded resource payment.

With $9,000 entrant opening cash and six-month terms, the entrant funds its initial awards but misses service in month one. The permitted prior-service exit allows reassignment in month two: market service totals **5,900**. Removing that exit keeps unserved contracts locked until expiry and totals **5,500**. The 400-zone difference is restored future service; neither policy recovers the first 100 missed zones. Both preserve the failed entrant's fixed obligations and end with the same **−$37,600** operating cash after recorded bills. A customer lock cannot cure an underfunded operator.

A human-led incumbent that forgoes its robot order and responds in month two still delivers the complete 6,000 market zones with its funded staff. The entrant again delivers 200 zones and ends at **−$90,225.80**. This is a separate physical/procurement comparator, not the pure timing contrast. Its purpose is to challenge the assumption that buying robots is necessary to defend service. It is not an optimized staffing or shutdown strategy, and no digital uplift is estimated from a benchmark.

#### Implications for deployment, entry and the three packs

For an organic cleaning entrant, proceed to target measurement only with a defined accepted service, an executable onboarding/capacity plan, durable customer access and dated collections that survive a plausible incumbent offer. Wait where a temporary price gap or robot procurement lead is the only advantage. Reject an asserted cash-recovery claim that substitutes booked sales, financing inflows or locked but undelivered work for actual funded service and collections. These are evaluation gates, not a recommendation to make a financial transaction.

A customer-side platform choosing build, acquire or hybrid must compare the actual retention and authority it receives. [The acquisition cases](research/entry_transitions.md) preserve inherited obligations and separate purchase price from working equity. The new renewal cases demonstrate a mechanism that may change the value of those customer relationships, but no combined valuation, acquisition premium or automatic synergy is calculated. Provider/operator/integrator/financier obligations remain separate. Cheap equipment and an apparently attractive customer contract can coexist with operating cash failure.

For hospital logistics, a commercial renewal cannot establish clinical continuity, handoff acceptance or the available fallback after a failed supplier. For utility inspection, continuity concerns accepted coverage, retained visits and review obligations rather than this model's cleaning zones. For automotive workcells, changing the buyer or provider must preserve qualified configuration, takt, handling and retained support. Transfer the renewal, breach, working-capital and exit questions to these packs; do not transfer synthetic prices, demand or response months. Their existing proceed/wait/reject recommendations and empirical cost gaps remain.

This is substantial H through the conventional operational counterargument: a simple transparent renewal rule, paid human capacity and an improving incumbent can defeat an undifferentiated technology-access thesis. No LLM execution benefit, data exclusivity or automatic capability transfer is awarded. Existing intelligence-role fees, learning/qualification and digital-human evidence retain their separate demonstrated scopes.

#### Falsifiers and next work

The entry interpretation changes with measured durable contracts, superior whole-service production, genuinely underserved demand, a funded staffing redesign, slower feasible incumbent response or stronger customer value beyond the chosen price rule. It also changes when realistic termination penalties, qualification lead times, failure guarantees, financing and shutdown/redeployment options alter obligations. None is estimated here. Stable lot order and current-capacity admission are declared conservative policy choices, not optimal behavior.

The [seventeen complete worlds](model/results/competitive_renewals.json) include qualification delay, thin capital, support scarcity and exact switching boundaries. Bounded coverage becomes thirteen families and 128 worlds; **DE-CF10 remains partial**. Next test finite installed-support and expansion commitments under a shared cash/engineering/supply schedule, with lost service and rejected starts preserved. Full expanding-fleet optimization, real commercial terms, asset recovery and empirical whole-system costs remain open. The active campaign deadline remains **2026-11-14T20:12:27Z**.

#### Reproduced competitive renewal worlds

| Synthetic renewal world | Entrant accepted zones | First cash crossing, month | Maintained through horizon from month | Terminal operating cash after recorded bills, USD |
| --- | ---: | --- | --- | ---: |
| prompt response | 200.00 | Not observed | Not observed | -87,884.51 |
| no response | 4,900.00 | 8 | 8 | 16,815.49 |
| response month seven | 2,400.00 | Not observed | Not observed | -34,684.51 |
| response month ten | 3,900.00 | 8 | 8 | 4,315.49 |
| human led response | 200.00 | Not observed | Not observed | -90,225.80 |
| short observation window | 2,900.00 | Not observed | Not observed | -5,054.19 |
| delayed procurement | 3,000.00 | 8 | Not observed | -21,425.80 |
| late entrant qualification | 0.00 | Not observed | Not observed | -80,378.06 |
| thin entrant cash | 200.00 | Not observed | Not observed | -35,000.00 |

Generated from [competitive_renewals.json](model/results/competitive_renewals.json). Months start at zero. Funding-adjusted bank change excludes equity and net loan principal, then reserves recorded unpaid operating bills. This is not residual asset value, distributable cash or lifetime ROI. Current funded capacity cannot guarantee all future locked service.

### Focused follow-up: Installed support and finite expansion commitments

Authoritative analysis: [research/support_expansion.md](research/support_expansion.md).

Edition 0.25, continuation from accepted edition 0.24 at `01e1ceab09ef5b083a94e1ab618a6b013dba371b`. The [support/expansion policy](model/SUPPORT_EXPANSION.md) adds sixteen synthetic worlds to the existing monthly physical and Treasury execution. It tests **DE-CF10** without changing old engine coefficients or importing a source announcement as calibrated capacity.

A funded manufacturing order can consume cash needed to support installed robots before the new unit is useful. Conversely, refusing to order until all future resources exist today can unnecessarily delay service. The correct comparison preserves the announced plan, what actually arrives, all actor cash and every failed unit or service period. No one policy is declared universally superior.

#### Current capacity and announced capacity imply different commitments

The fixed market requires 8,000 cleaning zones across eight months. Two opening robots and retained paid human capacity initially deliver less than the monthly requirement. One additional robot is requested; repeated candidate dates are opportunities to meet that finite request, not additional demand. The reference support budget grows from 25 to 60 hours in month four. Dates are zero based.

| Synthetic policy/resource world | Start month | New commissioning month | Accepted zones, eight months | Opening-cohort supported robot-months |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|
| Funded only, actual increase occurs | 0 | 4 | 7,401.90 | 16 |
| Announced capacity plus cash reserve, increase occurs | 2 | 4 | 7,401.90 | 16 |
| Current capacity plus cash reserve, increase occurs | 4 | 6 | 7,125.43 | 16 |
| Announced increase fails to arrive | 2 | Not commissioned | 6,894.10 | 16 |
| Ample support from the beginning | 0 | 2 | 7,678.38 | 16 |

The announced policy orders early enough for the available support window, using the stated manufacturing and commissioning leads. Funded-only ordering creates earlier WIP/transit but the same accepted service and included resource cash in this particular comparison. No unpriced carrying-cost advantage is invented. Current-capacity caution loses **276.48** accepted zones relative to the announced policy. A longer manufacturing lead moves the necessary order earlier while retaining the same month-four commissioning outcome.

When the promised allocation fails, the funded unit remains in transit awaiting commissioning. The pre-change decisions and all earlier physical/cash states match the successful-announcement world. The model does not consult the future failure when approving the earlier order. Even the ample-support case does not satisfy the complete 8,000-zone service target because the opening capacity and initial ramp remain. Supported robot-months and complete customer service are different measures.

#### A cash reserve protects existing service but does not finance every next step

With $40,000 opening provider cash, three-month service-fee collections and ample physical support hours, funded-only admission starts the new unit immediately. It loses two opening-cohort support months and delivers **6,149.33** zones; the new unit remains uncommissioned at the end. The two-month recurring-support reserve blocks the expansion and preserves all sixteen opening support months, delivering **6,894.10** zones. The protected **744.76** zones are actual future service, not backfilled misses. Both retain the fixed human staffing commitments and the same customer workload.

The exact initial admission boundary is **$42,730.32** provider cash. One cent less rejects the first order and shifts admission to month five. Yet crossing the threshold does not guarantee the expected month-two commissioning: the exact-boundary unit cannot pay commissioning until month five. Its two-month reserve covers a named recurring-support budget, not every future bill. The one-cent-below unit commissions in month seven. Their accepted service differs, but this is not proof that the reserve is optimal or sufficient for a real company.

A separate funded $5,000 provider loan restores one new commissioning in month three, preserves opening support and delivers **7,540.14** zones. It is fully repaid in month seven, leaving only **$32.91** provider cash at close and substantial uncollected service fees. Loan principal is not sales or extra consolidated resources. The model demonstrates dated funding and repayment, not a financing offer, safety margin or investor return.

#### Cash and planned hours cannot replace qualified resources or a paying operator

A deliberately rejected first manufactured unit retains its full $36,000 manufacture cost and components. A later individually identified replacement restores the reference service; two funded starts produce one commissioned new robot. The growth request is still one. Acceptance is observed when the existing physical loop reaches its ready date; the earlier admission policy does not exploit the supplied future acceptance flag.

With only seven opening qualified components, maintenance and starts compete for the same finite stock. The case delivers **2,797.90** zones and only five opening-cohort supported robot-months. Neither a monetary reserve nor an announced engineering allocation manufactures a component. A later actual support reduction similarly lowers service even after successful commissioning. Frozen-cohort replay tests preserve each known configuration's support/software cost instead of repricing all installed units as the current release.

When the operator has no cash, an ample provider balance does not pay the operator's committed staff and site obligations. Delivered service is zero; the two legal accounts do not pool cash automatically. With zero growth requested, monthly candidate dates fund no starts and create no inventory. These cases reject two misleading shortcuts: treating aggregate capital as freely transferable, and treating a planned pipeline as qualified supported capacity.

#### Decision consequences across the research program

A robotics provider should qualify the entire path from funded start to sustained customer service. A manufacturer margin, backlog, financing announcement or a purchased module is insufficient. The [commercial disclosure audit](research/commercial_disclosure_boundaries.md) supplies real-world questions about generation, support and actor cash; its prices and aggregate financials do not calibrate these synthetic reserve results. The [renewal work](research/competitive_renewals.md) separately tests customer retention and operating collections. Neither combines into an unobserved product ROI.

For the hospital pack, a new fleet cannot take support from urgent installed workflows without explicit fallback and accepted clinical service. For utility inspection, support priority must preserve required coverage, reviewer capacity and residual visits. For automotive, expansion requires components, qualified configuration, changeover/integration and continuous supported takt. Transfer these commitment and continuity questions, not the cleaning zones, per-unit hours, prices or thresholds. All three empirical recommendations remain provisional.

Substantial H remains connected through cohort-specific software obligations, human capacity, conventional planning and explicit forecasts. The simple policies use current state and an announced calendar; no LLM advantage, model-score transfer or data premium is assumed. The prior [learning/qualification worlds](research/learning_execution.md) preserve finite engineering and failed candidate work. This extension adds the distinct manufacture/installed-support/funding sequence, with shared identities and one Treasury.

Proceed to target measurement when contractual service, actor cash, component supply, qualification, support and contingency resources can be reconciled on one dated plan. Wait where a support increase or collection is merely announced. Reject a claim of fully financed deployment when it substitutes a recurring-support reserve for commissioning, debt and remaining obligations. These are research gates, not authorization for purchases or commercial commitments.

The bounded counterfactual catalog now spans fourteen named challenge families and 144 worlds. The result does not close empirical full-cost requirements, real rights, asset recovery, optimal fleet investment or cross-market transfer. The same active campaign continues toward **2026-11-14T20:12:27Z**. Next challenge the provisional recommendations against measured accepted service and complete retained work; add sources only when they can change a decision gate, and retain explicit unknowns where they cannot.

#### Reproduced installed-support and expansion worlds

| Synthetic support/expansion world | Accepted zones | Funded starts | New commissioned | Opening-cohort supported robot-months | Provider closing cash, USD |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| announced capacity reserve | 7,401.90 | 1 | 1 | 16 | 168,374.20 |
| funded only | 7,401.90 | 1 | 1 | 16 | 168,374.20 |
| current capacity reserve | 7,125.43 | 1 | 1 | 16 | 167,056.78 |
| announced increase fails | 6,894.10 | 1 | 0 | 16 | 171,439.36 |
| ample support | 7,678.38 | 1 | 1 | 16 | 169,691.62 |
| thin provider funded only | 6,149.33 | 1 | 0 | 14 | 4,121.94 |

Provider fees transfer within the operating boundary; loans, outstanding claims and terminal assets remain separate.
