### Focused follow-up: Hospital support, repair and replacement obligations

Authoritative analysis: [research/hospital_support_contracts.md](research/hospital_support_contracts.md).

Edition 0.26 · 8 November 2026 · continuation from accepted `adb45e480c74bc3c2cc8ea1a10f1edff8ba41328`. Primary records and explicit unknowns extend the existing [customer package](data/customer_evidence.json), audit **FG-AUDIT-02**. The [hospital recommendation](research/deployment_entry_decisions.md#hospital-logistics--qualify-the-entire-obligation) now requires a current configured order, repair/exit rights and funded fallback capable of the same accepted service. This is a research gate, not an initiated purchase or field trial.

Earlier hospital studies identify handling, urgency selection, accompaniment and unresolved staffing denominators. Procurement evidence adds a distinct constraint: installation, software access, support eligibility and repair remedies may leave essential delivery work with the operator. Neither evidence class replaces the other. A component price cannot answer whether a due medication reaches its recipient during repair.

#### The retrieved US schedule and its limits

The [GSA-hosted attachment](https://www.gsaadvantage.gov/ref_text/36F79724D0104/36F79724D0104_online.htm) names contract 36F79724D0104 and TUG T-2.5. Its contract period is May 2024–April 2029, but the price revision is undetermined; the September 2012 cover footer is not selected as an effective date. The seven-page PDF was downloaded, fingerprinted and visually inspected. It is a schedule artifact, not an executed target-site order. [FG-E024–025]

Listed prices include $51,300 for the T-2.5 bundle; $3,600 annual or $300 monthly eligible maintenance; $22,500 existing-hospital installation for up to four units; $4,450 each additional installation; $8,500 mapping; and $6,850 an additional elevator-manufacturer interface. Software/controller/base-station inclusions have site-address boundaries. Maintenance eligibility requires new installation or continuous support. Delivery wording begins installation rather than guaranteeing commissioned service. [FG-E024]

The warranty attachment allows a reasonable correction period of at least 60 days after notice, with conditional relief from affected maintenance fees. It also contains modification, access and operating conditions, exclusions and supplier-negligence indemnity. A separate return policy concerns misordered accessories/parts. [FG-E025]

These observations change how a proposal should be evaluated:

- **Price the actual configuration.** Do not charge a group installation line once per robot or turn a site inclusion into an unlimited portfolio license. An additional interface is distinct from a controller. The record does not establish which optional items a hospital needs or whether quoted services overlap. The dashboard shows separate lines and units without inventing an installed subtotal.
- **Preserve support eligibility.** Annual and monthly maintenance are alternative periods. Their coexistence does not establish identical payment dates, reinstatement rights after a lapse, or applicability to another generation. A bought asset, supported asset and qualified operating configuration remain separate states.
- **Fund the service through repair.** A correction-period lower bound is neither observed downtime nor a maximum restoration deadline. Maintenance-fee relief does not deliver a request. Fallback needs funded staff, access, capacity and urgency coverage; provider maintenance does not itself supply them.
- **Verify modification and exit rights.** A software inclusion does not establish third-party repair, model access, data export, retraining, site migration or transfer on acquisition. These are questions for the actual order; legal enforceability is not adjudicated here.

#### Compact catalog metadata is incomplete

The [VA catalog](https://www.vendorportal.ecms.va.gov/NAC/Search/Details/36F79724D0104) displays the same contract and installation-start wording. Its warranty field says “60 Days” and its minimum order is one unit. The attachment instead describes maintenance-term warranty/remedy conditions and a $100 minimum. These representations remain unreconciled; the website-version footer is not a record-specific revision date. [FG-E026]

The compact warranty field cannot establish a maximum repair commitment. Nor can the researcher select a favorable order-minimum interpretation or assert current availability on all displayed terms. A current configured order and its incorporated terms are the next evidence requirement. The catalog identifies economically important ambiguities without resolving them.

#### A buyer award makes support dependence concrete

The [NHS Greater Glasgow and Clyde notice](https://www.sell2wales.gov.wales/search/show/search_view.aspx?ID=JUN521106&catID=) records an £800,000 ex-VAT AGV maintenance award to Aerocom (UK) Limited, with one tender. The buyer attributes the available maintenance route to its installed closed-loop Otsaw Swisslog system. The recorded conclusion is 13 March 2025, dispatch 17 June, and mirror publication 23 June. Cancelled March notices are not additional awards. [FG-E027]

This is a disclosed buyer constraint, not demonstrated excessive pricing, supplier margin, actual disbursement or achieved reliability. Robot count, full scope, payment timing and operating exposure are absent. The award is not annualized, divided per robot or converted into a US price. The retrieved award does not establish a terminal period; earlier prospective wording is not silently used to fill it.

The inference for entry is narrow: access to similar robot technology does not establish access to an installed hospital's interfaces or maintenance route. Replacement needs qualification, transition, parallel service and process authority. An installed system can constrain switching without being the best future design. A portable, independently supportable alternative with a credible transition could weaken that constraint. The award quantifies neither direction.

#### How this changes the decision

For hospital logistics, **proceed with analytical qualification only when repair and exit terms join funded service continuity**. Wait where configuration, support access, restoration commitments or retained labor remain unknown. Reject a specific case that substitutes fee relief for accepted delivery, removes still-required urgent coverage, or assumes costless replacement. The feasible human/digital courier stays in the comparison through failures and transition, not only successful robot journeys.

For utility inspection, apply the restoration question to required measurement-and-disposition windows. For automotive, apply it to takt, line recovery and generation requalification. Neither pack inherits hospital prices, remedy periods or the NHS constraint. Substantial H remains present through site software, integration authority and unverified data/model rights. An open-interface or cloud-service label does not establish those rights.

The [renewal worlds](research/competitive_renewals.md) distinguish retained awards from delivered service; [support/expansion worlds](research/support_expansion.md) distinguish recurring reserves from other funded obligations. Procurement evidence makes those questions concrete without calibrating the synthetic quantities. All fourteen counterfactual families and 144 worlds remain numerically unchanged.

A current executed order, incorporated support terms, accepted request IDs, retained staff work, actual invoices/collections and funded fallback would change the decision. A qualified alternative support route could weaken the switching constraint. Complete accepted service with avoidable paid commitments could strengthen deployment. Current evidence establishes neither empirical ROI nor target cost per delivery.

#### Retrieval and stopping boundary

FG-E024/025 record the source PDF's SHA-256. OCR guided navigation; all seven pages were visually checked. The NHS award was read on an official government mirror after direct PCS retrieval returned HTTP 502. A Find a Tender route returned HTTP 403 and was not bypassed. SAM pages exposed no readable body through the retrieval route; aggregator descriptions of US awards were discovery leads only. No underlying signed order, paid access, correspondence or commercial action was obtained. The Qilu full-methods gap remains unchanged.

This bounded source tranche stops here. Reopen when an executed term, matched service/support record or qualified alternative can change a recommendation. Repeated listings and cumulative activity alone are insufficient. The active campaign continues under its original clock and sole-writer restriction.

### Focused follow-up: Funded asset recovery and restored service

Authoritative analysis: [research/asset_recovery.md](research/asset_recovery.md).

Edition 0.28, continuation from accepted `33579d11c03d7906d6924ef8d7b7dbbe590d1cd4`. [Fourteen funded recovery worlds](model/results/asset_recovery.json) extend the existing physical, calendar, company and Treasury loop. The [methods](model/ASSET_RECOVERY.md) specify synthetic prices and rights, actual settlements and one unchanged robot identity. This is a bounded execution mechanism, not an observed distressed-asset transaction or a valuation estimate.

An installed robot can have productive capacity while its operator cannot meet obligations. A new buyer may have cash but lack a qualified site. A lender can recover money without restored service; conversely, a robot can return to productive service while an old loan remains impaired. Treating these as one recovery percentage hides both the transaction and its operating consequences.

#### Follow the same unit through title, cash and service

The reference customer owns one opening robot and defaults on a funded loan. After that month's scheduled debt settlement, a distinct incumbent buys the unit for a declared $20,000 and pays $1,000 for initial relocation. The old operator's current support is canceled; its customers, staff commitments, remaining debt and legal identity stay behind. The unit is unavailable during months one and two, then separately qualifies and commissions at the buyer in month three. Accepted service remains limited by actual demand, paid human capacity and supported physical capacity.

| Synthetic world | Sale | First supported buyer month | Buyer expected accepted zones, eight months | Remaining seller principal, USD | Final physical state |
|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---|
| Funded reference | Completed | 3 | 3,269.10 | 0.00 | Installed |
| No asset sale | None | None | 2,352.00 | 10,141.29 | Installed at seller |
| Buyer qualification unavailable | Completed | None | 2,352.00 | 0.00 | Awaiting qualification |
| No commissioning slots | Completed | None | 2,352.00 | 0.00 | Awaiting commissioning |
| Low $3,000 sale price | Completed | 3 | 3,269.10 | 8,091.29 | Installed |
| Stronger paid human buyer, purchase | Completed | 3 | 4,000.00 | 0.00 | Installed |
| Stronger paid human buyer, no purchase | None | None | 4,000.00 | 10,141.29 | Installed at seller |

These are expected synthetic cleaning zones, not observed completed jobs or cross-market calibration. The total due workload is 7,200 zones. The reference delivers 4,371.10 across both companies. The stronger buyer already covers all 4,000 zones assigned to it; purchasing and supporting the robot adds no accepted buyer output and removes no paid staffing. It is therefore insufficient to justify the purchase by pointing to available robotic capacity. The model does not infer a benefit from an unpriced option or future customer growth.

The no-sale and ineligible worlds retain physical property and obligations. A lessee cannot sell its independent lessor's asset. Missing release or configuration-transfer permission prevents the sale even with ample buyer cash. With no shipping slot, the offer fails; a completed relocation cannot displace a second new unit into the same slot. These are explicit model permissions and resource checks, not claims about enforceable real-world rights.

#### A successful sale can leave a stranded buyer

Purchase and initial relocation settle together or neither does. At the exact synthetic funding boundary, a one-cent deficit prevents title transfer and both payments. Passing the boundary does not prepay all future commissioning, software or support. This fixture's later collections fund month-three commissioning even when the purchase exhausts current cash; that result cannot be assumed for a different collection calendar.

If the buyer configuration never qualifies, the funded sale still stands, the unit remains unavailable and buyer service equals the no-purchase human baseline. The seller can nevertheless settle its loan from actual receipts. An arrival after the observed horizon has the same lack of restored service during the window. No resale proceeds, end-of-window valuation or reversal is invented to repair that unfavorable outcome.

Conversely, the low-price sale permits the same physical restoration while leaving the old seller in default. Principal recovery, lender loss, restored buyer service and the old company's customer obligations need separate reports. In the reference, the lender had already received $9,858.71 before sale proceeds arrived; remaining principal is $10,141.29 at sale-month close and is settled the following month. The entire original loan is not incremental recovery from the asset.

#### Use recovery as a gate in the existing decisions

Hospital continuity needs a funded fallback during removal, transport, site acceptance and support qualification. A sale contract or replacement entitlement alone does not satisfy due deliveries. The [hospital contract evidence](research/hospital_support_contracts.md) establishes why repair, supported software and replacement terms must be inspected; it does not supply a resale price or authorize transfer of a target configuration.

Utility inspection additionally needs current routes, permissions, sensor/reviewer qualification and required coverage during transition. Automotive reuse requires the buyer's integration, safe workcell configuration, takt and commissioning capacity. All three packs inherit the questions, not these cleaning rates, prices or rights assumptions. None gains an empirical ROI or final recommendation from this extension.

The provider/operator/integrator/financier boundary also survives the sale. Consideration transfers cash between buyer and seller; relocation and later support consume separately funded resources. Customer ownership, robot ownership, custody, software rights and lender claims remain separate. Substantial H enters through software cohorts, conventional planning, the stronger human baseline and explicit transfer eligibility; no model release automatically transfers a qualified service stack.

Proceed with a measured recovery case only when the actual owner, permitted release/configuration, capable buyer, dated funds, relocation, site acceptance and retained obligations reconcile. Wait when the buyer's operational qualification or fallback remains unresolved. Reject a recovery claim that sells another party's asset, treats purchase as accepted service, pools legal accounts or cancels debt/customer commitments without an explicit supported transaction. These are research decision gates, not authorization for commercial actions.

DE-04 remains partial: the declared owner sale now executes, while secured priorities, lease repossession, restructuring, valuation and optimized investment remain outside scope. Fourteen bounded families contain 158 worlds; source counts and empirical requirements do not improve merely because the mechanism exists. The same active campaign continues to **2026-11-14T20:12:27Z**. The next integration should connect an unresolved annual fleet/resource or competitive decision, or add target evidence capable of changing a recommendation, rather than multiply favorable recovery variants.


#### Supplier contract challenge, edition 0.29

The synthetic sale and share-acquisition cases retain declared permissions; they do not establish usable target contracts. FG-E028 supplies separate locators for use/resale, ownership change, assignment and retained claims in a public supplier form. Read the written-variation context too. A document review must distinguish a discretionary right from an automatic outcome, and public wording from an applicable executed agreement. [Teradyne Robotics v2.9](https://a.storyblok.com/f/169662/x/1a0bf144e3/teradyne-robotics-standard-delivery-terms-ver-2-9-october-2025.pdf), linked from [MiR's delivery-terms page](https://mobile-industrial-robots.com/general-terms-of-delivery).

This changes the acquisition/recovery question without asserting a transaction outcome. In the model, shares can change ultimate ownership while legal accounts and liabilities stay in place. That accounting identity does not prove operational continuity. Likewise, acquiring the physical unit does not answer which software, site qualification, supplier commitments and support account the new operator can use. These are separate diligences before comparing build, acquire or reuse. The named source is a manufacturing AMR comparator; its conditions are not imported into the hospital or utility target as facts.

The [MiR support page](https://mobile-industrial-robots.com/service-support/mir-service-activation) separates documentation, service-agreement support and subscribed telephone help. This sharpens the measurement request: inspect the relevant serial/buyer entitlement, scope and restoration commitment. Access to a manual is not evidence of a funded repair resource or restored due service. No serial lookup, login, contact or application was attempted.

The [public finance page](https://mobile-industrial-robots.com/service-support/finance) also needs reconciliation. Its hourly headlines differ from dividing its monthly figures by its stated planned hours. The reproducible table below preserves each currency presentation and both source values; it does not choose a corrected quote. The 48-month multiplication is only an undiscounted component total. No conversion rate, missing fee, residual, actual draw or purchase option is invented. Planned clock hours cannot become accepted deliveries, robot availability or paid labor removed.

The five gates in FG-AUDIT-03 therefore ask for: actual title and permitted disposition; company-control and assignment review; target variations and retained claims; funded support/qualification/fallback; and a reconciled payment/service denominator. Six explicit unknowns remain. Proceed only to a target measurement case when those records reconcile. Wait where applicability, consent or operational coverage remains uncertain. Reject a purported economic comparison that converts a transaction label or low advertised hourly amount directly into dependable service. These are research gates, not a determination of enforceability or a commercial instruction.

No synthetic world, operating coefficient or empirical full-cost criterion is added or closed. Fourteen bounded families / 158 worlds remain, under the same in-progress campaign clock. The next substantive model gap is joining annual fleet/component/resource constraints and competing customer commitments; the next empirical gap is a complete target operating and contractual record.

#### Entry, renewals and paid original title, edition 0.39

[The combined recovery analysis](research/entry_transitions.md#funded-recovery-and-actual-retention-edition-039) now starts from a unit manufactured and purchased inside the observation window, retains company acquisition and actual customer renewals, and separates cash cure from physical reuse. A funded intra-platform owner sale can restore the target's human service before the robot reaches the buyer; identical market service can persist when destination qualification never occurs. Exact purchase funding can leave operations unfunded. These results deepen the existing recovery contract without replacing its original fourteen cases or inferring rights to leased assets. Every earlier numerical result remains unchanged.

### Focused follow-up: Annual commitments and competitive service

Authoritative analysis: [research/annual_commitments.md](research/annual_commitments.md).

Edition 0.30 adds an enforceable annual support/integration allocation to the existing monthly competitive cleaning case. It addresses a gap in the earlier bridge: proving that twelve monthly ceilings fit inside an annual reference pool does not establish that the pool remains available after other commitments. This is a synthetic mechanism and a decision test, not an observed annual staffing plan or calibrated estimate of robot returns.

The [complete executable contract](model/ANNUAL_COMMITMENTS.md) holds physical manufacture, unit identities, payer accounts, paid staffing and customer demand in the existing loop. A resource reservation changes actual admissible support or commissioning. It does not create another market's output or another resource bill. Fourteen named challenge families now include eight additional resource worlds, deepening the competitive and scarcity mechanisms rather than adding a new empirical claim.

#### The important reversal is in attribution

In the unrestricted control, 14 supported robot-months occur during the first six observed months, July–December 2026. They consume exactly 9,100 support minutes. Reserving all but 3,900 minutes permits September's two bookings and October's four, then blocks every November and December robot booking. January uses its own new-year allocation and restores all four units. It does not restore the eight omitted robot-months.

Under the declared robot-triggered incumbent pricing rule, that interruption changes November and December's incumbent offer from $25 to $29 per accepted zone. The entrant gains 100 recurring zones in each of those months. Its total accepted service rises from 200 to 400 zones over the twelve-month window. Buyer switching resources rise from $200 to $400. This is a conditional consequence of the explicit response rule, not evidence that an incumbent would actually withdraw its price response when robots stop.

**Total accepted market service remains 6,000 zones in both cases.** Already funded human capacity covers the work. The entrant's funding-adjusted operating cash after recorded bills remains negative: −$87,884.51 in the unrestricted case and −$80,319.35 with the support interruption. Neither amount is a complete lifetime return: opening assets, residual value and future unissued obligations have their existing explicit boundaries. Released task hours still do not identify payroll removed.

The human-led response counterfactual makes that distinction stronger. The incumbent buys no robots and follows a declared calendar response. It keeps its $25 offer during a month with no supported robot in the entire market, preserves 6,000 delivered zones and limits entrant service to 200 zones. Procurement and policy intentionally differ from the equal-technology control. A robot-dependent competitive moat cannot be inferred when a feasible paid human operation can preserve service and the same price response.

#### Exact resource and calendar boundaries

An allocation of exactly 9,100 first-year support minutes admits all 14 bookings. At 9,099 minutes, the final 650-minute booking is refused and 649 minutes remain unused. The model admits complete paid unit-month bookings; it does not quietly give a robot 649/650 of its required support. Total market output can still stay unchanged through the human alternative. Thus a resource threshold, a robot-output threshold and a customer-service threshold are different claims.

Reserving all but 8,400 first-year integration minutes admits two new units. The other two retain their physical identities and wait for January commissioning, while the first pair continues supported operation. Two 70-hour commissioning bookings use the full available integration allocation. The remaining annual support pool cannot substitute for missing integration capacity. Similarly, ample annual availability cannot waive a 20-hour monthly support ceiling.

The opening-balance control splits the same unavailable support amount between 300,000 minutes of assumed pre-window use and a smaller outside reservation. It preserves all physical, cash and commercial execution exactly. This tests the accounting boundary: reclassifying unavailable capacity must not create new costs, work or benefits. Prior use is not reconstructed history; its historical funding and output remain outside the observation window.

All reservations are synthetic. Their large share of the reference pool is a chosen stress condition, not a finding about a real employer's utilization or supplier commitments. Each year reconciles prior use, outside reservations, actual bookings and unbooked balance to the same reference pool. January never lends capacity backward. Software qualification competes in the support pool, and a one-cent software payment shortfall rolls back both contingent physical payment and annual booking.

#### Consequences for the three deployment packs

For **hospital logistics**, require a dated support and integration commitment for the actual cohort and site alongside the complete delivery and fallback obligation. A supplier's annual staffing total cannot establish the hospital's reserved capacity. If competent funded porters preserve the complete service, compare robot task changes and actual avoidable paid commitments separately.

For **utility inspection**, bind access, review, qualification and residual physical visits to the same calendar. An annual survey capacity number does not guarantee the specialist window or remove a required visit. The cleaning minutes and prices do not transfer; only the allocation and service-accounting question does.

For **automotive presentation**, reserve commissioning and old-generation support before relying on a ramp date. A physically received unit can wait for an integration allocation while existing work remains funded elsewhere. Identify whether the stronger conventional workcell or human baseline already preserves accepted kits. The separate component-vector and plant scheduling contracts remain unresolved.

Proceed to a scoped target evaluation when the chosen service, dated usable capacity, paid fallback, unit configuration and each payer's obligations reconcile. Wait when an aggregate annual budget hides competing commitments or the required calendar is unknown. Reject a claimed service or payroll benefit that depends on unsupported robot-hours, backward borrowing of future capacity, imported outside-market output or an untested human baseline. These are evaluation gates, not purchasing or investment instructions.

#### What remains unresolved

The annual six-cell model and monthly physical loop use different component representations. This tranche deliberately establishes no conversion between annual actuation/compute/memory vectors and the monthly generic module. Simultaneously executing other markets would require explicit system-compatible bills of materials, qualified receipt dates, shared queues and actual payer obligations. Reservations alone cannot prove joint portfolio feasibility.

The wrapper also does not optimize allocation, investment, exit or bidding, and does not make separate acquisition/recovery/expansion scenarios jointly feasible by adding their results. A next consequential integration must choose one of these missing contracts and test a decision reversal on conserved stocks and cash. More favorable scenario variants would not close that gap. Actual annual resource commitments, response behavior, service outcomes, rights and full costs remain empirical acceptance needs.

Outside reservations are not executed service or extra cash expenses. Paid human capacity preserves total service in these cases; robot-months, customer awards and payroll removal are separate outcomes.

### Focused follow-up: Compatible components and dated maintenance

Authoritative analysis: [research/component_execution.md](research/component_execution.md).

The earlier annual model names actuator and onboard-board pools, while the monthly model uses abstract modules. Those quantities cannot be added or converted without a contract. Edition 0.31 supplies an explicit replacement contract for one matched cleaning system: whole compatible manufacturing parts, separately received and qualified inventory, and named discrete replacement requirements on the same paid physical loop. The prior generic mode remains available with unchanged behavior.

This is a synthetic manufacturing/maintenance mechanism, not a product BOM, observed failure rate or measured supply forecast. The annual catalog's actuator/board identities specify compatibility. Actual receipt dates, qualification dates and replacement requirements are separately declared. Full six-cell supply allocation, supplier working capital and the target's actual maintenance process remain unexecuted.

#### What the complete cases change

The qualified control begins with no typed stock. In month zero, 100 compatible H actuators and four boards arrive and qualify. Four funded starts consume 96 actuators and all four boards. The remaining four actuators satisfy one assumed replacement requirement per unit in month four. Actual units, Treasury, competition and annual-resource outcomes match the earlier ample generic-material control. This is a controlled change in the stock contract, not an estimate that the two inventories have equal economic value.

An announcement with no actual receipt creates no usable or quarantined inventory. With physically received but unqualified boards, 100 actuators can be usable while four boards remain held and manufacture still cannot proceed. With 100 Q actuators and four boards, the H system cannot manufacture at all. Summing these stocks as undifferentiated “components” would erase the binding incompatibility.

If board qualification occurs in month one after the sole month-zero manufacturing attempt, no robot is manufactured automatically. The inputs then contain usable stock but no later order. An explicitly rescheduled month-one start creates four units, moves the first supported incumbent response from month two to month three, and reduces first-year supported exposure from 14 to 10 robot-months. A promise, receipt, qualification and authorized funded production are four different milestones.

The spare-delay case delivers four replacement actuators in month four's phase 4. All four installed robots already missed their phase-3 service bookings that month. The parts remain protected and are issued when service succeeds in month five. No replacement or annual support booking is counted in the failed month. First-year exposure falls from 14 to 10 robot-months, and the declared response rule temporarily changes the incumbent's offer. Service is not backfilled by the later shipment.

Rejecting two of the four paid starts consumes the same 96 manufacturing actuators, four boards and $144,000 of bundled manufacturing cash. The two rejected unit identities remain in the physical ledger. Only the two operating units complete replacement requirements, leaving two qualified spare actuators unused. Applying expected yield as another cost multiplier or returning the rejected parts automatically would violate this execution contract.

#### The human alternative limits the economic inference

All eight standard cases still deliver 6,000 accepted market zones through the retained paid human operation and any supported robots. A component shortage therefore changes robot capacity and the specified competitive response, but does not establish lost total service in these cases.

The entrant serves 200 zones in the qualified control, 300 with explicit later manufacture or the spare delay, and 1,200 when no robot manufacture occurs. These differences follow the declared robot-triggered incumbent bid policy; they are not estimated customer preferences. The earlier human-led response remains a direct falsifier of an assumed robot-dependent pricing advantage. The component result does not establish that a real incumbent must abandon a price response when its robot cannot operate.

Entrant operating cash after recorded bills remains negative in every standard case. Avoided robot rent/support or an unexecuted purchase can reduce cash outflow without proving a better long-run business. Missing residual value, future obligations, actual staffing flexibility and full target costs remain separate. No released task-time estimate becomes paid labor removed.

#### Decision consequences

**Hospital logistics:** verify compatible, qualified replacements and the provider's actual support entitlement against the delivery obligation. A part shipped later in the day or month does not establish that service was covered before arrival. Preserve funded porters and other fallback; compare their complete service before claiming robot availability adds output or removes payroll. This cleaning timing contract is a question for the hospital, not a measured hospital repair schedule.

**Utility inspection:** identify the configured sensor/compute/actuation stack, usable replacements, qualification and access/review windows. An announced supply quantity or compatible mechanical platform does not prove the installed inspection configuration works. Residual physical visits, review and fixed/conventional alternatives remain in the service boundary. Do not import cleaning parts or prices.

**Automotive presentation:** distinguish whole manufacturing kits, qualified assemblies, site commissioning and generation-specific supported replacements. A rejected funded build retains resources even if no accepted kit is produced. A different actuator or board configuration is not interchangeable by count. Compare the existing qualified conventional workcell, retain its service obligations and require a dated replacement plan before relying on a ramp.

Proceed to a scoped evaluation when the exact configuration, qualified receipt dates, whole-start requirements, installed-service replacements, usable support rights and each payer's obligations reconcile. Wait when qualification or replacement timing remains unknown. Reject an asserted deployment benefit that counts announcements as stock, substitutes incompatible parts, retroactively credits late repair, or ignores the stronger human/conventional alternative. These remain evaluation gates, not purchase instructions.

#### Evidence and integration still required

The replacement vector and its inclusion within the fixed support labor/cash bundle are assumptions. The next empirical challenge is a target configuration's actual service, replacement and commercial record: failed versus completed interventions, qualification lead time, technician work, supplier funding, warranty/rights and accepted service during outages. A public primary record that changes one of those assumptions is more valuable than another favorable synthetic variant. Do not repeat exhausted restricted-source searches or infer missing prices and repair obligations from a vendor capability claim.

The annual aggregate spare flow is not yet a discrete failure process, and the declared component receipts do not execute the other annual markets or supplier financing. The model therefore remains partial on joint portfolio feasibility and empirical whole-service economics. Fourteen challenge families now include 174 bounded worlds; their results must not be added as one company plan.


#### Inspection readiness challenge, edition 0.32

The [primary-source readiness challenge](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md#rollout-retained-work-and-robot-repair-edition-032) leaves the assumed support/parts bundle and supplier consignment unvalidated. Customer integration and review require their own measured work boundary. Plant-repair benefits cannot calibrate robot replacement needs. All eight component worlds retain their original numerical results.

## 5. Digital-AI ecosystem, benchmarks and direct contributions

Authoritative chapter: [research/digital_ecosystem.md](research/digital_ecosystem.md).

First substantive research wave · evidence retrieved 25 October 2026 · input revision `campaign launch snapshot (see ignored sync_receipt.json)`

This is an intermediate Workstream H finding set, not the completed seven-day campaign. The paired [evidence file](data/digital_evidence.json) contains 17 evidence records, five benchmark cards, eight analogy tests, three direct-contribution maps, seven relationship records, and explicit unresolved questions. The [digital-infrastructure chapter](research/digital_infrastructure.md) and [HI evidence](data/digital_infrastructure_evidence.json) extend its upstream chain. Shared sources do not constitute independent corroboration. Requested runtime configuration is GPT-6 Astra with Ultra reasoning; this child can see supported tool configuration names but cannot independently verify its actual selected model or reasoning setting.

### What changes the robotics judgment

Digital AI changes both the candidate robotic system and the incumbent against which it must compete. A robot operator that beats yesterday's human process can still lose to a human operation with better scheduling, documentation, purchasing, exception handling, or engineering support. These digital improvements can also complement robots. The correct comparison therefore needs four matched operating configurations, with an explicit interaction and full implementation costs.

The strongest source evidence in this wave does not support a universal digital productivity coefficient. A large customer-support deployment increased resolved issues per worker-hour, particularly for less experienced workers. A later study of experienced software developers found its current uplift estimate undermined by participant selection, task selection and concurrent-agent time accounting. These are informative differences in production systems, rather than competing estimates of one economy-wide effect. Likewise, the current peer-reviewed agent-coordination study supports conditional architecture choices; it does not establish that adding agents always increases production.

Direct digital contributions to physical systems are real but occur at distinct interfaces. AutoRT physically used foundation models to select and organize data collection. DrEureka used GPT-4 to generate training configurations subsequently tested on hardware. Astranomy evaluated language-model control and oversight in simulation. Open-RMF documents conventional traffic and building interfaces. SAP announces enterprise agents. Combining those sources into a diagram does **not** establish that a working LLM-to-ERP-to-fleet production stack has been deployed or measured.

Three immediate decisions follow. First, select markets using accepted output, timing, integration and support requirements rather than benchmark success alone. Second, keep the customer process owner, deployment integrator, robot controller, model provider and capital provider distinct even when one entity fills several roles. Third, measure learning and R&D gains through validated improvements and critical-path reduction, not raw episodes, generated code or announced customer access.

### The connected ecosystem dossier

The chain is physical inputs and capital → model research, training, post-training and evaluation → inference and cloud distribution → agent/tool runtimes → integration and change management → operating customers → accepted customer output. Each arrow has a different unit and evidentiary status. Training FLOPs are not usable inference capacity; capital commitments are not commissioned capacity; model access is not permission to write business records; a portfolio relationship is not a deployment; an accepted work product is not eliminated payroll.

The separate HI chapter covers power, qualified compute/memory, capacity and feedback to construction, maintenance and infrastructure. This H chapter covers how capability travels through that chain into work. Its upstream development evidence is deliberately bounded: [Hoffmann et al.'s compute-optimal training paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2203.15556v1) studies allocation between parameters and training tokens under a specified training-compute budget. The domain is text-model training loss and downstream evaluation. Its fitted allocation exponents are neither robot reliability exponents nor coefficients for adoption, manufacturing cost or firm output. Deployment lifetime inference costs and physical experience collection introduce objectives that differ from this fixed-training-budget optimization. Post-training, data licensing and research-talent markets remain incomplete in this wave.

The [FTC's January 2025 partnership report summary](https://www.ftc.gov/news-events/news/press-releases/2025/01/ftc-issues-staff-report-ai-partnerships-investments-study) identifies equity/revenue-sharing, cloud-spend commitments, distribution, information access and some control/exclusivity rights in selected Microsoft–OpenAI, Amazon–Anthropic and Alphabet–Anthropic relationships. Its information boundary is September 2024 plus public material through January 2025. It supports a mechanism in which finance can be bundled with compute purchases and switching costs; it does not establish the October 2026 terms of every relationship or realized competitive harm. The implication for robotics is to maintain separate financial, compute, data, operational-control and customer-access edges. An investment cannot be counted again as independent cloud revenue and additional free deployment capital in a consolidated scenario.

Two current deployment-company disclosures illustrate distinct organizational mechanisms:

| Arrangement | What the primary disclosure supports | What remains unproven |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [OpenAI Deployment Company, 11 May 2026](https://openai.com/index/openai-launches-the-deployment-company/) | An OpenAI-majority-owned and controlled deployment business, more than $4 billion of announced initial investment, partner channels, and an agreement to acquire Tomoro. The stated acquisition would add approximately 150 forward-deployed engineers and specialists. | Closing was conditional in this source. This wave has not verified completion, cash deployment, customer productivity, acquisition returns or retained margins. More than 2,000 partner-sponsored companies are potential channels, not observed installations. |
| [Ode, 15 July 2026](https://www.ode.com/press/anthropic-blackstone-and-hellman-friedman-introduce-ode-with-anthropic-an-enterprise-ai-services-firm) | An issuer-described standalone services company linking Anthropic, Blackstone, Hellman & Friedman and other investors, built around Fractional AI, which the issuer says was acquired in May 2026. | Detailed ownership percentages, exclusivity, model portability, customer-data rights and operating returns are not established by the announcement. |

These arrangements support studying implementation as an economic activity. They do not yet prove that an integrator earns durable rents, that ownership causes faster adoption, or that a customer-side physical newco can finance its ramp. A services company helping existing businesses adopt AI differs from a new operating company selling manufactured products, inspections, deliveries or cleaning. The physical entrant needs customer qualification, facilities, assets, working capital and retained contribution; a lab relationship supplies only some possible complements. A model improvement can strengthen an integrator by expanding useful applications, or weaken it by making previously scarce work easier for customers or platforms to perform. Both mechanisms remain open.

The [Census CES 26-25 full paper](https://www2.census.gov/library/working-papers/2026/adrm/ces/CES-WP-26-25.pdf), dated April 2026, supplies a better deployment denominator than announcements. For the November 2025–January 2026 reference period, 18% of firms reported AI use in a business function; the employment-weighted figure was 32%. Among functional adopters, 57% used AI in three or fewer functions. These are broad-AI measures, not LLM-only use or physical automation. The revised core question took effect on 17 November 2025, so a discontinuity from earlier wording is not clean adoption growth. Firm use refers to two weeks, worker-task use to six months, and firms report worker behavior. The study distinguishes adoption, breadth, task use and investment; it does not identify causal productivity from their correlations with commercial performance.

For robotics this favors an adoption record with separate capability, process integration, operating authority, workforce use, economic attractiveness and delivered output. Low adoption need not mean technological incapability, but high benchmark capability does not establish the size of an economically viable deployment overhang. That quantity remains unknown.

### Two contrasting operating dossiers

#### Business-software customer support: assistance inside a repeated production process

[Generative AI at Work](https://academic.oup.com/qje/article/140/2/889/7990658), published in the QJE on 4 February 2025, examines 5,172 customer-support agents at a software firm and its subcontractors. The rollout primarily occurred in fall 2020 and winter 2021. The tool used GPT-3-based real-time suggestions; agents remained responsible for conversations and could edit or ignore suggestions. The relevant product is a resolved customer issue, not generated text.

Access increased resolved issues per agent-hour by 15% on average. This combined changes in handling time, parallel chat handling and resolution. Benefits were heterogeneous: less experienced/lower-skilled workers gained more, while highly skilled workers showed little productivity improvement and some quality deterioration. Evidence during outages is consistent with some persistent worker learning, but those outages were rare and estimates noisier. The paper's staggered-adoption/event-study design handles observed worker/time/tenure factors and examines alternative estimators; interpretation still requires parallel-trend and adoption assumptions. It is not simply a randomized trial of the full observed rollout.

The mechanism is concrete: knowledge and communication practices are delivered at the point of decision inside an existing workflow, with a human retaining final authority. This suggests a research question for physical end markets: can digital assistance improve dispatch, maintenance diagnosis, operator training or customer-service work around a physical process? It does not supply a 15% uplift for assembly, floor cleaning, nursing, inspection or robot supervision. Even in the source population, more resolved issues per hour does not identify removed payroll, wage changes or equilibrium employment. At fixed work and quality, time can be released; its realized value depends on staffing, demand and redeployment.

For adoption strategy, the relevant complement is not just model quality. It includes historical examples, an interface fitted to live work, human judgment, feedback and training capacity. It also warns that copying the average effect across different worker cohorts can overstate expert benefits. A digitally improved incumbent may become more competitive before installing any robot.

#### Software development: changed task selection makes uplift difficult to identify

[METR's 24 February 2026 update](https://metr.org/blog/2026-02-24-uplift-update/) explicitly rejects treating its new experiment as a reliable current productivity estimate. The original February–June 2025 study found that AI-allowed tasks took 19% longer, with a reported interval of +2% to +39%. The later experiment began in August 2025 and included 57 developers across 143 repositories and more than 800 tasks. Among ten returning developers, the raw task-time estimate was −18% with an interval from −38% to +9%; among new developers it was −4%, interval −15% to +9%. Negative figures mean less time, not negative productivity.

The crucial finding is methodological. Developers increasingly declined participation because they did not want to work without AI. Participants withheld tasks they expected AI to accelerate. Pay also fell from $150 to $50 per hour between studies, creating another selection mechanism. Some work changed in scope or quality; some developers worked on another task while an agent ran, making task-time attribution difficult. Randomizing submitted tasks does not remove selection into submission. The authors considered the data weak evidence about the magnitude of current speedup.

This does not establish that AI slows developers in October 2026, nor that large current gains are proven. It establishes that a dated average task-time result can lose validity as tools, work choice and organization change. For robotics R&D, the unit should be validated engineering output at a fixed objective, with active human time, elapsed critical-path time, model/training compute, failed proposals and physical testing recorded separately. Faster code generation is not equivalent to a shorter hardware qualification campaign. Concurrent agents may improve total throughput while complicating per-task measurement; that is a reason to improve the accounting, not to assume a gain.

Together these dossiers motivate heterogeneous digital effects, an explicit human baseline, and outcome-specific measurement. They cannot be pooled into one “AI productivity” parameter.

### Benchmark cards and a consequential version correction

The linked JSON retains configuration, task population, budget, assistance, costs, acceptance and missing fields. An unknown budget is explicitly unknown rather than silently comparable.

| Evaluation | What it measures | Main boundary for economic use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [GDPval, September 2025](https://openai.com/index/gdpval/) | Professional deliverables across 44 knowledge-work occupations and nine industries; 1,320 full-set tasks, 220 public gold tasks; blind expert preference comparisons. | One-shot tasks arrive with context and reference files. The advertised roughly 100× time/cost comparison is inference/API billing, excluding human oversight, iteration and integration. It cannot establish whole-job or physical-work automation. |
| [TheAgentCompany v3](https://arxiv.org/html/2412.14161v3) | 175 tasks involving browser, terminal, company systems and simulated coworkers; Gemini 2.5 Pro/OpenHands 0.28.1 achieves 30.3% full success and 39.3% partial-credit score. | A simulated firm, not an operating firm. The reported $4.20 average API cost per instance excludes organizational costs. Exact run ceilings and wall-clock distributions need further extraction. The result is a dated configuration, not a 2026 frontier ceiling. |
| [Agent coordination, Nature Machine Intelligence, July 2026](https://www.nature.com/articles/s42256-026-01268-y) | 260 configurations across six benchmarks, five architectures and three model families with matched per-system compute ceilings and tool access. | Within-domain architectural guidance, not a universal law of teams or physical fleets. Added SWE-bench and Terminal-Bench cells have only 20 instances each. |
| [Astranomy](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/srl/projects/astranomy/) | Direct, persistent-agent and orchestrated Astra policies on 120 RoboLab tasks, five fixed-seed episodes each, with simulated DROID. | Simulation pauses during inference; no walltime or retry limit. Medium reasoning and action-step limits are part of the configuration. Success does not measure real-time productive throughput. |
| [DrEureka v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2406.01967v1) | GPT-4-generated rewards/domain randomization, RL training and real-world quadruped/manipulation tests. | Existing simulation infrastructure, multiple candidates and hardware validation are required. Best candidate performance is not one-shot engineering success or a total R&D cost saving. |

**The July coordination paper materially revises the January Google blog.** The earlier blog describes 180 configurations, four benchmarks and cross-validated R² = 0.513. The peer-reviewed article expands to 260 configurations and six benchmarks, with R² = 0.373, or 0.413 with a task-grounded capability measure. It explicitly labels its approximately 45% single-agent capability threshold a practical selection rule rather than a universal scaling principle. Most descriptive patterns do not survive cluster-robust inference; six benchmark clusters limit precision. The model selects the best architecture in 87% of held-out **within-domain** configurations. A separate validation predicts the sign of multi-agent gain in 94% of 16 model-by-benchmark configurations.

The direction remains useful: structured financial reasoning showed large benefits from coordination, sequential planning deteriorated, and verification architecture affected error propagation. The reported 17.2× versus 4.4× amplification comparisons are benchmark trace measures, not robot accident rates. They motivate comparing a strong single agent, conventional solver and alternative coordination structures with equal information and budgets. They do not set a numerical fleet uplift or a 45% robot-autonomy threshold.

Astranomy makes the economic distinction especially visible. Its page reports Agentic Policy success of 74.8% and 13.37 seconds average inference latency per call. Continued full-page retrieval resolved the initial headline ambiguity: Section 4.2 and Table A11 state that cost values cover **successful episodes only**, priced using October 1, 2026 API rates. The Agentic mean is $36.40 over 447 usage-covered successes out of 449 successful episodes. This is not total expenditure on successful and failed attempts divided by delivered successes. Failure costs, resets and physical support would still be needed for commercial cost per accepted output.

Section 4.1 reports that inference accounts for 97.8% of Agentic estimated episode duration, 97.0% for Direct EEF and 94.9% for Orchestrated Policy. Estimated duration adds rollout time and inference time while the original simulation pauses during inference. It is not an observed real-world operating cycle or proof that the same success would persist while the scene changes. Section 7 explicitly says statistical significance of configuration differences has not been established and excludes contact-rich insertion/assembly and highly dynamic tasks. These qualifications constrain both adoption and architecture claims. A stronger simulated score can coexist with a poor time-constrained production proposition.

### Three direct-contribution maps

#### Robot reasoning and control

AutoRT's interface is image/scene understanding → language task proposal → affordance and constitution filtering → chosen scripted, learned or teleoperated policy → observed episode outcome. The physical collection spans four office buildings and seven months. It used 53 robots in total and more than 20 simultaneously; those are different denominators. One human supervised three to five mobile manipulators, and sometimes reset the environment.

Table 1 is important counterevidence to reading “77,000 episodes” as productive autonomous output: 73,293 scripted-policy trials had 21% success, 3,060 teleoperated trials 82%, and 936 RT-2 trials 4.7%. The authors attribute low RT-2 performance to differences between collection environments and its training/inference conditions. Their stated objective is diverse data collection, with a preliminary downstream-learning evaluation. Successful data generation, successful task execution, accepted customer output and profitable operation are four different outcomes.

For the six shared market cells, a proposed digital planner needs explicit state, command authority, validation and fallback. A low-level controller remains separate. The measurable contribution might be improved task selection, fewer interventions, faster recovery or better held-out performance. Each requires a target test; it cannot be inferred from model naming. Astranomy supplies a second, simulated control configuration and highlights why inference cost and timing must be separate from physical-policy quality.

#### Fleet, site, and enterprise operations

[Open-RMF interfacing](https://openrmf.readthedocs.io/en/latest/interfacing/index.html) documents fleet state, destination, pause/resume and path messages, plus door state and requests. A fleet that only reports state gives an orchestrator different options from one accepting paths or pause commands. Coordination therefore depends on negotiated interfaces and control rights, not just planning intelligence. The retrieved documentation is a mutable `latest` page, not a pinned deployment release.

The proposed full interface is ERP/WMS/MES orders, inventory and priorities → validated allocation or schedule → fleet manager → robot/building execution → acknowledged state and business-record reconciliation. The process owner defines priorities; the fleet manager controls movement; human staff resolve exceptions. Stale information, an unacknowledged instruction or duplicate transaction can break the chain. Timeouts, idempotency, reconciliation and escalation are proposed design requirements here, not claimed observations of a deployed LLM stack.

[SAP's May 2026 announcement](https://news.sap.com/2026/05/more-autonomous-supply-chain/) supplies intended planning, manufacturing, logistics, asset/service and ERP roles, with phased general availability through 2026. It supports identifying interfaces to investigate. It does not establish which named agent is available at a selected customer or a measured robotics productivity contribution.

The target-side boundary is stronger with [ChristianaCare's operator-authored account](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC13048758/). Its grant-funded 2022–25 pilot used three cobots across more than 80 patient areas, reported more than 48,600 deliveries and tested EHR/elevator integrations. Operational value concentrated in nonurgent medication and equipment flows. Complex, time-sensitive nursing workflows were not meaningfully augmented. No LLM effect is isolated. For `CELL-M10-hospital-logistics-US-2025`, this argues for separate urgent and nonurgent cohorts and an explicit request/dispatch/infrastructure interface, rather than treating all hospital physical support as one eligible pool. A large delivery count is insufficient to infer nurse payroll savings.

#### Robotics research and development

DrEureka takes simulator code, an objective and safety instructions, generates candidate rewards, builds a reward-aware physics prior, proposes domain-randomization settings, trains policies and tests transfer. It used GPT-4, sampled 16 domain-randomization configurations and trained three seeds per configuration. The authors evaluated candidate policies in the real world. The comparisons show useful technical improvements in bounded locomotion and manipulation settings; they do not remove training or hardware-test needs.

The paper reports a three-hour runtime for its bounded domain-randomization comparison versus ten hours for CEM and twenty for BayRn. That is a method comparison, not a measured reduction in total humanoid engineering labor or development cost. It also explicitly lacks a systematic mechanism to select the most promising policy before deployment. Some ablations generated jerky, unsafe behavior that triggered controller protection. Candidate generation has value only when verification and release constraints are included.

The model consequence is to let an R&D intervention change a validated release's cost or delay only through an explicit critical path. Coding, reward search, training and physical validation can constrain different projects. If hardware testing remains binding, faster candidate generation may improve exploration but not calendar time. METR's measurement concerns reinforce recording total accepted engineering output and concurrent resource use rather than adding an unexplained “research acceleration” multiplier.

### Analogy ledger and permitted use

Eight full cards in the JSON preserve source evidence, target evidence, necessary conditions, material differences, predictions, falsifiers and alternative explanations. None authorizes copying a numerical digital coefficient into robotics.

| Test | Current judgment | Consequence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| H-AN01: training scale → delivered physical productivity | Source-domain relationship supported; numerical target transfer unsupported. | Model compute, coverage, validation and operating outcomes separately. |
| H-AN02: cheap software replication → fast fleet rollout | Naive transfer rejected. Digital infrastructure itself is physical; robots additionally need qualified units, sites and support. | Keep manufacturing, commissioning and service constraints after capability gates. |
| H-AN03: model availability → integrator rents | Complementary activity is observed; durable rents unproven. | Test repeat deployment cost, customer control, platform bundling and portability. |
| H-AN04: deployment access → data moat | Conditional, with explicit rights and transfer validation. | Raw fleet size or customer count cannot create a separate data-value premium. |
| H-AN05: benchmark performance → autonomous job/payroll replacement | Direct conversion rejected. | Restore workflow boundaries, intervention, timing, quality and demand. |
| H-AN06: more agents → better fleets | Universal claim rejected; architecture-specific hypothesis retained. | Permit positive, zero and negative coordination effects; compare simpler controllers. |
| H-AN07: lab-linked deployco → financeable physical newco | Organizational comparator only. | Separate service integrator, customer operator and financier; fund enterprise ramp. |
| H-AN08: AI improves source workers → improved physical incumbent | Source effects heterogeneous; target coefficient unknown. | Keep digital-only human baseline in every joint scenario. |

An informative falsifier is not simply a failed technology demonstration. For the learning analogy it is deployment expansion that adds little accessible, relevant experience or fails to improve held-out target performance. For integrator value it is an OEM/platform bundle or modular interface that removes the scarce service while retaining customer outcomes. For the newco analogy it is an incumbent adopting the same available technology before the entrant recovers formation and ramp costs.

### Contract for the executable joint scenario

Use the authoritative CPU engine, not a second calculation implementation in this chapter. At matched output, quality, demand and period, let costs be:

| | Existing digital operations | Specified additional digital intervention |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Human-led process | C00 | C01 |
| Specified robot-assisted process | C10 | C11 |

The robot's incremental saving under the improved digital baseline is `C01 − C11`; total joint saving is `C00 − C11`. The cost interaction is `C11 − C10 − C01 + C00`, with a negative value indicating complementary cost reduction. Output and service-quality contrasts must be displayed separately. A cell that cannot operate without required embedded robot software is not a valid “robot without software” counterfactual; hold that mandatory software fixed across the robot row and vary only the additional intervention.

Digital assistance to humans, robot planning latency, inference expense, integration amortization, intervention labor and coordination effects need separate controls. Evidence observations remain fixed when those scenario controls change. Shared scheduling or data improvements appear once. Demand expansion and altered service scope require a separate scenario. No source in this wave calibrates the six physical cells' digital-only gain, joint interaction or whole-enterprise labor saving; those parameters remain explicit assumptions or nulls.

The main sensitivities should include a successful digital-only incumbent, little physical transfer, negative coordination overhead, a binding supervision/integration constraint, and an R&D improvement that fails to shorten physical validation. These are informative tests of the strategic mechanism rather than forecasts.

### Coverage and exact next priorities

H1 is connected to the lead's infrastructure dossier; this wave does not complete research-talent, data-rights or post-training markets. H2 distinguishes training performance, configured workflow capability, economics and adoption, but does not estimate a quantitative overhang. H3 has two contrasting sourced operating dossiers and survey context. H4 has five benchmark cards with explicitly incomplete budgets. H5 has current coordination evidence and three direct maps. H6/H7 have dated partnership and deployment-company arrangements, but current contract terms, operating returns and open-model competition remain incomplete. No complete ecosystem census or calibrated physical productivity estimate is claimed.

1. **Restore operating timing to the control comparison.** The full methods now establish conditional-on-success timing/cost and missing usage for two successful Agentic episodes. Next obtain failed-attempt costs and independent walltime-bounded physical control evidence. Stop when a versioned all-attempt cost boundary and target comparison exist, or preserve the precise missing target evidence.
2. **Measure the moving human comparator in a shared physical cell.** Seek operator or causal evidence for hospital logistics, manufacturing planning or utility maintenance that separately measures supporting digital work and physical outcomes. Stop with a matched output/quality/cost estimate, or retain a null target parameter with the attempted route.
3. **Verify deployment-company operating substance.** Check Tomoro closing, Ode's actual customer outcomes and contractual model/data rights. Seek one full deployment-cost/outcome case; announcements alone do not complete this question.
4. **Make coordination comparisons reproducible.** Extract the current Nature paper's exact configuration/budget tables and code references, preserving its revision from the January summary. Design the target test around shared state, authority, verification and equal resources.
5. **Measure validated robotics R&D acceleration.** Recover candidate-selection, compute, human-review and physical-test costs for DrEureka and contrasting work. The completion criterion is fixed-objective validated engineering output and critical-path cost/time, not more generated candidates.

These priorities connect directly to market sequencing, deployment architecture, learning investment, provider boundaries and same-technology incumbent/newco comparisons. They require public research first; unavailable field measurements remain explicit gaps rather than invented calibration.

### Direct R&D contribution and qualification, edition 0.21

[The R&D follow-up](research/learning_qualification.md) links video-generation and adaptation methods to the physical validation and supported-configuration obligations needed for deployment. A useful digital research tool can reduce one stage while another determines readiness. The record preserves source conflicts, inherited data, partial credit and target-only forgetting limits; it does not infer a universal acceleration, transfer penalty or robotics productivity coefficient. Technical self-assessment is also separate from available units, replacement parts and contracted support.


### Funded qualification follow-up, edition 0.22

The [executable learning follow-up](research/learning_execution.md) adds unique eligible operating data, a paid candidate, target/retained-cohort trials, delivered compatible runtime modules and finite support. It preserves source/target boundaries and all prior numerical scenarios. Candidate performance remains a disclosed hypothesis; real costs, rights, qualification distributions and supported scale remain unresolved.


### Digital obligations inside a commercial robot service

The [Agility disclosure follow-up](research/commercial_disclosure_boundaries.md) separates onboard policy/safety compute, Arc orchestration/diagnostics and R&D. Planned compute changes require qualification; data flow does not establish training rights or successful transfer. Continuing software/support and development cash are separate obligations.

### Linked labor outcomes and the staffing decision, edition 0.40

The [new labor-outcome audit](research/digital_labor_outcomes.md) adds a historical Danish survey/register comparison to the existing H package. It preserves conditional expected allocation, administrative labor contrasts and changing occupational mobility as different outcomes. The publication revision does not extend the observation window. Its practical use is to strengthen the target work-and-staffing measurement contract, not to supply a universal digital or physical labor effect.

Across customer support, software development, conventional dispatch and hospital AGVs, the quantity measured determines the claim that can be made. Released task time can support a capacity hypothesis; actual paid savings require a feasible whole-service staffing transition. The source/target analogy and fleet/site/enterprise contribution map now make that bridge explicit. Existing numerical paths and source estimates remain unchanged.

### Focused follow-up: Digital work reallocation and actual labor outcomes

Authoritative analysis: [research/digital_labor_outcomes.md](research/digital_labor_outcomes.md).

Edition 0.40 · 8 November 2026 · Substantial H, with customer and provider implications. The same [decision campaign](DEPLOYMENT_ENTRY_CAMPAIGN.md) remains in progress through 14 November. This is a public-source audit and measurement design, not a new simulation or a target staffing estimate.

#### Decision: preserve the possible benefit and identify its form

A digitally improved human organization is a serious alternative to installing robots. Its benefit can appear as more accepted work, better quality, shorter queues, greater resilience or different tasks before a wage bill changes. An operating newco might sell that capacity; an incumbent might use it to clear a backlog. Neither can finance payroll savings that have not been connected to a feasible staffing decision. The relevant investment claim must specify which outcome pays, when, and to whom.

The [existing digital evidence package](data/digital_evidence.json) now connects a linked survey/register study to the historical customer-support study, the developer selection problem, conventional hospital dispatch and documented AGV staffing changes. These sources measure different outcomes. Together they sharpen the required observations without supplying a common percentage to insert into the model.

#### What the new source actually measures

Humlum and Vestergaard's [public author paper](https://www.andershumlum.com/s/chatbots_260313.pdf), revised **13 March 2026**, studies eleven exposed Danish occupations. Surveys were conducted in late 2023/2024; administrative outcomes end in **December 2024**. Table B.1 records 115,000 invitations, 25,241 complete responses and 24,796 linked responses. Table 1 separately reports 14,790 ever-users and 10,006 never-users. These counts do not supply Table E.3's unprinted conditional sample size. [H-DK-2026-DESIGN]

Table E.3 reports savings for 75.6% of ever-users. Among respondents reporting savings, 84.7% select other-task allocation and 29.4% select more of the same tasks. The questionnaire asks what respondents **expect** to do. Choices overlap. Its 3% work-time estimate also depends on category/frequency coding and an assumed eight-hour day. These are neither observed redeployment nor accepted-service gains. [H-DK-2026-TASKS]

Labor analyses compare adopter/non-adopter changes under stated trend assumptions; pooled estimates add adopter-specific trends. Authors describe a 2% pooled earnings bound and a distinct 3% dynamic bound. Occupational mobility changes despite near-zero average labor contrasts. Selection, timing, response and common-effect/spillover limits remain. No physical payroll coefficient follows. [H-DK-2026-LABOR]

##### Audit qualifications that change the decision

The record preserves each estimand, population, date, locator and missing value. Figures 3, 5 and 8 and Tables 1, B.1 and E.3 were visually inspected alongside methods and questionnaire text. The author PDF's SHA256 is `dc6f69de66b49d875a41bfa4bcc35621b9c022310c3184cefeceb790c884c892`. Three claim records describe this one paper; they are not independent corroboration. [The author index](https://www.andershumlum.com/research) identifies the revision and previous title. Published aggregates were inspected; confidential microdata were neither requested nor re-estimated.

The practical consequence of the questionnaire qualification is to keep an intention outside the realized-work ledger. A respondent can expect to perform other tasks while the organization lacks demand, authority, equipment or a suitable time window. Conversely, valuable quality improvement may occur with unchanged recorded hours. A null labor contrast therefore cannot be substituted for zero productivity, and a reported saving cannot be substituted for a paid staffing reduction. The positive occupational-mobility result also prevents a blanket conclusion that work organization did not change.

A study's identification controls belong in its evidence card. They cannot be carried into a new investment case as a guarantee of causality. A target rollout still needs its own comparison: which demand, personnel, qualifications and process changes coincided with it, and how would the same service have been delivered otherwise? Common effects on competitors or non-users matter to a market-entry thesis even when a relative comparison differences them out. The latest paper date does not extend the observed outcome window.

#### Use the strongest available comparison at each level

| Evidence already accepted | Outcome it helps investigate | Additional observation needed for the investment claim |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Historical customer support](research/digital_ecosystem.md), H-QJE-2025 | Accepted resolutions per agent-hour and worker heterogeneity | Matched quality/demand, a feasible staffing or additional-sales decision, and all implementation costs |
| Developer study, H-METR-2026 | Submitted-task time under changing tool use and selection | Concurrent work, task selection, validated engineering output and critical-path time |
| [Conventional patient dispatch](research/conventional_coordination.md), H-PROXIMITY-2025 | A competent digital-human coordination alternative | Complete work including cancellations/outside-window demand, active labor and paid staffing |
| [Historical AGV workforce audit](research/field_support_denominators.md#employment-operating-windows-and-fallback-edition-036), FSD-E010/E011 | Redeployment, retained duties, operating windows and manual contingency | Current target rosters, actual payroll/contractor invoices and complete service through failures |
| New linked digital labor audit | Separate reports/expectations from administrative labor outcomes | Actual time disposition, quality-matched whole service and the target organizational transition |

These comparisons are complementary. A task study can identify a plausible capacity improvement, while an administrative study tests whether a different margin changed. Neither makes the other redundant. Avoid averaging incompatible measures into an assumed automation effect. Preserve strong human/conventional operation in each physical case and measure new review, integration and exception work, including work imposed on people who do not directly use the technology.

#### Six physical-service measurement contracts

The following are proposed observations for existing cells, not completed field studies or requests to contact operators.

| Cell | Join the released task to retained service and actual labor |
| --- | --- |
| Hospital logistics | Follow dispatch, pickup, receipt, nursing/porter duties, supervision and urgent manual contingency. Retain canceled and outside-window requests. A faster routine delivery may leave the same staff necessary for the rest of the service. |
| Utility inspection | Link remote review, residual visits and revisit decisions to a qualified field roster. Shorter observation time need not remove travel or a required site visit. |
| Automotive presentation | Observe variant completeness, buffers, line deadlines, replenishment, quality and changeovers alongside workcell staffing. Local pick-time gains can leave the line constraint unchanged. |
| Floor service | Reconcile floor work, nonfloor duties and response obligations in the shared crew calendar. Notice, overtime, agency labor and cash collection dates determine which staffing action can execute. |
| Tote logistics | Link moves to downstream handoff acceptance and fulfilled orders, including receiving labor and exception queues. Additional movement alone is not the customer service. |
| Construction installation | Retain trade qualifications, setup, sequencing, inspections and travel. Released minutes must fit a feasible crew and site schedule before becoming saleable capacity or avoided cost. |

Each contract separates active task time, elapsed service time, paid time, accepted output and cash. Preserve failures and repeated attempts, then reconcile unique accepted work to demand. Record the worker or contractor who performs new duties and the legal payer. A lower customer wage bill accompanied by greater provider support is a change in cost allocation until consolidated resources are measured.

#### Consequences for the three packs and company formation

The hospital, utility and automotive [decision packs](research/deployment_entry_decisions.md) now include an explicit labor-outcome gate. They retain their service-specific alternatives and unresolved economic metrics. The source does not supply a robot price, staffing ratio, site qualification or deployment deadline.

For an **incumbent**, a useful first claim can be capacity or service resilience while the paid schedule remains fixed. Count overtime or a hire as avoided only against documented workload and a credible staffing alternative. Do not retroactively describe unchanged staffing as a failed investment if the approved benefit was quality or backlog clearance; evaluate the benefit actually claimed.

For an **acquisition or hybrid platform**, inherited payroll, support obligations and working capital remain liabilities of identified entities. Acquiring operating authority may enable a different roster, but authority does not establish that the roster can meet all duties. Any transition reserve must cover retained service and new work through notice, qualification and collection delays. The [joint entry/recovery results](research/entry_transitions.md#funded-recovery-and-actual-retention-edition-039) already show why creditor cash, human-service restoration and productive robot reuse require separate tests.
