For a **standalone operating newco**, there is no historical payroll to remove. The comparison is the cost and reliability of the actual feasible crew and technology mix for contracts it can win. Digital assistance may help output or expertise acquisition; demand, qualification, funding and customer retention still have to support the capacity. Treating an incumbent survey's expected savings as a newco margin would skip those steps.

For a **provider, integrator or financier**, record who supplies training, review, integration, exception response and fallback. A service fee and the provider resources it finances belong to different accounting boundaries. Underwriting against a customer's expected time saving leaves open both the customer's cash conversion and the provider's support burden.

#### Ranking reversals and next evidence

Three plausible observations would change a recommendation:

1. The same paid crew meets more accepted demand and preserves all prior obligations. That can support a capacity case even with no payroll reduction, provided realized contribution covers the additional inputs.
2. A proposed smaller crew misses retained work or requires contractor/overtime replacement. The apparent payroll saving shrinks or the lower-cost claim fails the service condition.
3. New oversight and integration work moves across entities or occupations. A customer cash benefit can coexist with weak provider economics or higher consolidated resource use.

These are hypotheses and measurement gates, not findings attributed to the Danish sample. The [shared-workforce](research/shared_workforce.md) and conventional-coordination fixtures make some constraints executable; they do not estimate how frequently these outcomes occur in the field. All existing numerical assumptions and scenario results remain unchanged in this source tranche.

The next empirical requirement is a matched target record joining complete service, actual allocation of work and dated paid obligations across the transition. Existing unanswered questions on whole-service cost, rights, financing and broader resource integration remain partial.

### Focused follow-up: Benchmark reproducibility and transaction authority

Authoritative analysis: [research/coordination_reproducibility.md](research/coordination_reproducibility.md).

Version 0.1.1 · 25 October 2026 · Next-wave H/E research supplement. The observed launch repository revision is recorded in ignored `sync_receipt.json`. This extends [the digital ecosystem dossier](research/digital_ecosystem.md); it does not replace its first-checkpoint evidence or claim completion of the continuing campaign. Machine-readable records and the reconstructed task-cost table are in [coordination_reproducibility.json](data/coordination_reproducibility.json).

Three findings change what can be reproduced or inferred. TheAgentCompany publishes enough task-level results to establish that its reported average includes unsuccessful tasks, but the effective monetary limit is unresolved. Astranomy documents substantial configuration detail and a pinned task-list source, while its policy harness and complete failed-episode costs remain unavailable in the inspected public material. Open-RMF supplies concrete conventional integration mechanisms, including validation, request IDs and completion callbacks; these mechanisms have narrower semantics than an enterprise transaction or proof of physical completion.

All numerical reconstruction below concerns the source benchmark. None supplies a physical throughput, labor-substitution, cost-learning or adoption coefficient. Reading code establishes a documented/implemented interface at a source revision, not a deployed installation, tested interoperability or measured benefit from an LLM.

#### TheAgentCompany: recoverable failure costs, incomplete run provenance

The [v3 paper](https://arxiv.org/html/2412.14161v3), dated 10 September 2025, reports Gemini 2.5 Pro with OpenHands 0.28.1 at 30.3% full completion, 39.3% partial-credit score, 27.2 model calls and about $4.20 per instance. The run is much older than the paper version: the experiment directory is dated 10 May 2025, two sampled trajectories carry that date, and its initial repository commit was 11 May. It is a dated configured-system result, not a current frontier ceiling. The paper fixes simulated coworkers and LLM evaluators to Claude-3-5-Sonnet-20241022; those environment calls are distinct from the agent being evaluated. [HCR-E001–005]

The [archived run report](https://github.com/TheAgentCompany/experiments/blob/8cc10953909b88b2aa856b05134c344b505e5fcd/evaluation/1.0.0/20250510_OpenHands-0.28.1-gemini-2.5-pro/README.md) exposes all 175 task rows and identifies 53 perfect completions. Its “Details” section declares **OpenHands CodeAct PyPI release 0.28.1 with cherry-pick PR 7679**, using `gemini-2.5-pro-preview-05-06`. The two raw-trace checks below corroborate that declared model identity. Its more precise mean is $4.23. The README bytes are identical at the initial result commit and the inspected repository head, SHA-256 `dd989928d67a2b9b42a4e0872b520958821ef718e0b22183b88224b69841ad71`. Reconstructing the displayed, cent-rounded costs gives:

| Source-benchmark accounting boundary | Reconstructed amount | Interpretation |
| --- | ---: | --- |
| All 175 evaluated task rows | $740.34 | Agent API-cost estimate across the recorded suite, including non-perfect results. |
| The 53 perfectly completed tasks | $80.85 | Successful-subset cost; it is not the cost of attempting the full suite. |
| The 122 non-perfect tasks | $659.49 | About 89.1% of the displayed total. Some earn partial credit; this is not proof that their work has zero value. |
| Full-suite cost divided by 53 perfect completions | About $13.97 | An accepted-output-normalized benchmark ratio, conditional on this heterogeneous task mix and full-completion criterion. |

These are arithmetic reconstructions, not new experiments, actual invoices, retry-until-success estimates or business margins. Each row is rounded to cents; the aggregate can differ from the unrounded sum by up to $0.875 if each row uses ordinary nearest-cent rounding. The tasks differ in value and difficulty. Dividing the total by 53 does not turn them into interchangeable business services.

The pinned [summary implementation](https://github.com/TheAgentCompany/TheAgentCompany/blob/98b68ef82a47690c316f42fddb05baafaab56851/evaluation/summarise_results.py#L91) explains the denominator: it extracts distinct model-response usage records from agent trajectories, calculates token costs assuming no prompt caching, and divides total trajectory cost by trajectory count, without filtering for success. The Gemini pricing branch names `gemini-2.5-pro-preview-05-06` and dates its price reference to 8 May 2025. This method does not price the separate NPC/evaluator runs, environment hosting, setup, recovery labor or omitted infrastructure-failure attempts. The runner documentation explicitly says errors may skip tasks and evaluation can be resumed. The 175 completed rows cover the benchmark population; they do not prove that no earlier setup or runtime attempts were discarded. [HCR-E004]

A bounded spot check independently decompressed two published trajectories, without executing agent code. A successful `pm-create-plane-issue` trace contains eight distinct calls to `gemini-2.5-pro-preview-05-06`; the published pricing formula reconstructs $0.21672, matching the report's $0.22. A zero-score `sde-close-all-prs` trace contains 100 calls and reconstructs $95.91578, matching $95.92. Their first-to-last recorded-event spans are approximately 40.48 seconds and 1,285.55 seconds respectively. These are trace spans, not complete service cycle times: initialization, grading and infrastructure operations lie outside the sampled boundary. Two purposively selected traces cannot estimate average or tail latency for the suite. [HCR-E005]

The [9 May harness revision](https://github.com/TheAgentCompany/TheAgentCompany/blob/3a1777e23eec4c075ab095b5a814634b843db031/evaluation/run_eval.py#L31) pins OpenHands 0.28.1 in `pyproject.toml`, sets `max_iterations=100` and `max_budget_per_task=4`, disables prompt extensions/history truncation and uses a no-op condenser. Its 300-second sandbox timeout, 900-second initialization timeout and 600-second grading timeout are not a single task-wide wall-clock deadline. The script's task-image tag is `1.0.0`; no image digest manifest or exact executed configuration accompanies the inspected report. [HCR-E003]

The declared patch matters to reproducibility: [OpenHands PR 7679, “Fix cached tokens”](https://github.com/OpenHands/OpenHands/pull/7679), was merged on 3 April 2025 at 01:11:19 UTC, with merge commit `d7f651a06c231fac1448c0c4a3ffd92c8be682c6`. Its stated purpose is to handle `None` cached-token values. The report identifies the cherry-pick; it does not identify the resulting local executed tree or establish that the patch explains any budget discrepancy. A bare OpenHands 0.28.1 label omits this declared modification. [HCR-E014]

The configured budget of four cannot be promoted to a verified four-dollar evaluation ceiling. Archived no-cache repricing substantially exceeds it, including the sampled $95.92 trace. Different runtime cost accounting, ineffective model pricing, local overrides or a different executed configuration could explain this; the inspected evidence does not select among them. The historical source revision is a reproducible candidate configuration close to the run date, not proof of exact executed code. A future replication needs the actual patched config/tree, price-accounting behavior, dependency/image digests, restart policy and all-attempt logs. Simply running current `main` would also change OpenHands: the repository history records an upgrade to 0.42.0 in July 2025.

#### Astranomy: a task-source pin is not a policy-harness pin

The existing H dossier already resolves the consequential cost and clock qualification. [Astranomy §4.2/Table A11](https://research.nvidia.com/labs/srl/projects/astranomy/) prices **successful episodes only**. Agentic token usage covers 447 of 449 successes, from 600 attempts. Its $36.40 mean therefore cannot be compared directly with TheAgentCompany's all-instance mean, nor converted into all-attempt cost per accepted output by dividing by the reported success rate. Failed episodes may have different call counts and token histories, and two successful episodes also lack usage. [HCR-E006; existing H-ASTRANOMY]

This audit adds the version boundary. The page's raw HTML links its task previews to [RoboLab commit `ad45d4f…`](https://github.com/NVlabs/RoboLab/tree/ad45d4f974725d020f82c2b0d77d78533aeba2b3), committed 12 September 2026. That is a genuine immutable task-source reference. The hero's **Code** link instead points to `gitlab-master.nvidia.com/xuningy/robolab/-/tree/main/policies/ai_native`; attempted retrieval from this session failed with HTTP tunnel 403. No public immutable policy-harness commit or exact Codex/served-model build was found in the inspected report and linked public sources. The link failure does not establish what access others may have. The report itself acknowledges limited visibility into serving infrastructure and internal processing. [HCR-E007–008]

The mutable report was retrieved as 1,265,231 HTML bytes with SHA-256 `087ea1e189899e73b742922f4be463eee0e60204b919f8e89e53618e503271d4`. This fingerprints what was inspected; it is not a publisher version or a stored full archive. An exact publication timestamp remains unverified. The protocol and final-configuration table make the following settings inspectable:

| Boundary | Reported configuration | Reproducibility implication |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Population and sampling | 120 tasks, five episodes each, seeds 0–4; ablations on a selected 30-task subset | Retain task and seed, including failures; do not pool ablation populations with the full benchmark. |
| Shared input and budget | Astra, medium reasoning, three 384 × 216 views; 8,192 completion tokens per call, including reasoning and tool/action output | A per-call token ceiling is not an episode cost ceiling. Exact served-model and harness builds remain unresolved. |
| Direct temporal configuration | 3–15-action chunks on 90 tasks, historical 1–15 chunks on 30; three observations at t−14, t−7 and t | The pooled result is not one completely uniform action-horizon condition. Preserve this cohort distinction in replication. |
| Agentic configuration | Persistent full text/visual history; planning and motion feedback retained; optional tools disabled; one target held for 1–5 steps | Persistent history helps explain token growth; optional Python/image tools are not part of the reported default merely because an ablation enables them. |
| Orchestrated configuration | Cosmos3-Nano-Policy proposals, Astra review/edit/takeover, 15-step review horizon; optional native tools disabled | A second controller adds its own timing and cost boundary; general model calls alone are not the full system bill. |
| Clock and stopping | 15 Hz simulated control; simulation paused for inference; no walltime or retry limit; task-specific action-step limit | No observed response while the world keeps changing, and no physical deadline-constrained success estimate. |

The linked public RoboLab code supplies relevant conventional machinery: `eval/episode.py` obtains `max_steps` from `env.max_episode_length`, while `core/environments/config.py` takes `episode_length_s` from the task class. For example, the pinned `FoodPackingByColorTask` sets 120 seconds of simulated episode length. That does **not** establish the exact configuration or maximum real elapsed time of Astranomy's corresponding run, since its policy-side overrides are unverified and inference pauses the simulation. The required next evidence is an executed configuration manifest, not a conversion from simulated seconds to worker productivity. [HCR-E007]

Timing is also conditional on successful episodes. The report's 13.37 seconds per Agentic call and 97.8% inference share use estimated duration formed from rollout duration plus inference time; network delay is included in Astra inference. The demonstration with delays inserted is an estimate under sequential inference and real-time action execution, not a measured physical trial. The report expressly withholds statistical significance for configuration differences. Source-side capability remains informative; real-time deployment, full cost and target-side generalization remain open.

I additionally inspected the public cost-chart, inference-timing, full-results and failure-viewer script references. Those inspected assets did not expose a complete all-attempt token ledger. This is a bounded negative retrieval finding, not proof that such data do not exist. Prompt displays, example videos and a task-source pin are useful counterevidence to a claim of no disclosure; they do not close the missing executable-harness and accounting boundaries.

#### Open-RMF: implemented coordination with layered authority

The first H dossier cites mutable introductory documentation. This audit pins three **separate components**, without asserting they form a tested compatible deployment:

| Component | Inspected release and immutable revision | Source date |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `rmf_ros2` | 2.14.0, `7f7b247a618c22f76bf01ff0fb393f72317f6d9a` | Commit 25 September 2026 |
| `rmf_api_msgs` | 0.7.0, `2f25a8590ae9aace6f56125b752a88b688c5634e` | Commit 18 September 2026 |
| `rmf-web` | 0.3.0, `7aa265337936a5bd9a920b3fa2360a43244a015c` | Release published 5 August 2025 |

The [task-request schema](https://github.com/open-rmf/rmf_api_msgs/blob/2f25a8590ae9aace6f56125b752a88b688c5634e/rmf_api_msgs/schemas/task_request.json) requires a task category and description; the description must match a schema supported by a fleet. Optional start time, priority, labels, requester and fleet restrictions communicate scheduling intent. A requester string is not itself proof of business authorization. The task-state schema separately represents queued, underway, completed, failed and canceled states, and distinguishes dispatch status from task progress. [HCR-E009]

In the pinned [dispatcher implementation](https://github.com/open-rmf/rmf_ros2/blob/7f7b247a618c22f76bf01ff0fb393f72317f6d9a/rmf_task_ros2/src/rmf_task_ros2/Dispatcher.cpp#L446), a valid dispatch request creates a task ID, pushes a bid notice, and responds `success: true` with task state. That response establishes entry into the dispatch process, **not** successful assignment or physical execution. The dispatcher caches responses by request ID in bounded process memory, with `_max_size = 50`; the implementation is not a durable enterprise idempotency ledger. It republishes lingering dispatch commands, expiring them after ten seconds in this revision. After a task is dispatched, the dispatcher explicitly treats it as the fleet adapter's responsibility. [HCR-E010]

Fleet admission and actuation are separate. [`FleetUpdateHandle`](https://github.com/open-rmf/rmf_ros2/blob/7f7b247a618c22f76bf01ff0fb393f72317f6d9a/rmf_fleet_adapter/include/rmf_fleet_adapter/agv/FleetUpdateHandle.hpp) exposes request-consideration callbacks after feasibility checks. Defaults depend on the API layer: its delivery and cleaning callbacks require enablement, while `EasyFullControl` documents an accept-all default that can be restricted. No universal deployment permission policy follows from those defaults. [`EasyFullControl::RobotCallbacks`](https://github.com/open-rmf/rmf_ros2/blob/7f7b247a618c22f76bf01ff0fb393f72317f6d9a/rmf_fleet_adapter/include/rmf_fleet_adapter/agv/EasyFullControl.hpp#L337) delegates navigation, stop and action execution to the integrator. The integrator calls `CommandExecution::finished()` when a navigation/docking command successfully finishes and supplies activity-linked state updates. This is a concrete feedback responsibility; a generated plan cannot substitute for robot feedback. [HCR-E011]

Cancellation also has a bounded meaning. [`TaskManager::_handle_cancel_request`](https://github.com/open-rmf/rmf_ros2/blob/7f7b247a618c22f76bf01ff0fb393f72317f6d9a/rmf_fleet_adapter/src/rmf_fleet_adapter/TaskManager.cpp#L3036) validates the cancel schema and sends a success response when `cancel_task` accepts the operation. For an active task, that method calls the active task's cancel function and returns; it does not wait for an observed physical stop or reversal of business effects. Cancellation acceptance must therefore be distinguished from final robot state and any inventory/order reconciliation. If `cancel_task` returns false, the inspected handler sends no success response; it does not emit an explicit negative cancellation reply there. A caller may instead encounter a response timeout, whose cause requires reconciliation. This audit does not characterize a safety-rated emergency-stop system. [HCR-E012]

The pinned [web task route](https://github.com/open-rmf/rmf-web/blob/7aa265337936a5bd9a920b3fa2360a43244a015c/packages/api-server/api_server/routes/tasks/tasks.py#L219) validates the response and stores task state/request records after successful dispatch. The application attaches a user dependency to task routes; actual authentication configuration and business permissions require installation-specific inspection. Its [`RmfService.call`](https://github.com/open-rmf/rmf-web/blob/7aa265337936a5bd9a920b3fa2360a43244a015c/packages/api-server/api_server/rmf_io/rmf_service.py#L73) creates a fresh UUID on every call, publishes over ROS, and defaults to a five-second response timeout. The source describes a pub/sub pseudo-service with possibly multiple or out-of-order responses. A timeout is not evidence that the request had no effect; retrying the HTTP operation creates a new ROS request ID in this implementation. Dispatcher response caching therefore cannot, by itself, deduplicate repeated business requests. This is a code-derived failure-mode hypothesis, not an observed incident. [HCR-E013]

The resulting integration boundary is explicit:

| Stage | Observed source mechanism | Remaining target responsibility |
| --- | --- | --- |
| ERP/WMS/MES business intent | No end-to-end business connector verified in this audit | Own order validity, stock/reservation state, task-to-business-ID mapping and who may commit changes. |
| Model proposal | No LLM uplift established by these RMF sources | Validate against service requirements and allowed commands; compare with a deterministic alternative. |
| Task API admission | Typed category/description and response/state schemas | Preserve request identity across retries and distinguish acceptance from assignment/completion. |
| Fleet assignment/admission | Bidding, feasibility and fleet consideration callbacks | Configure permitted work and confirm adapter capability at the target site. |
| Robot execution | Integrator navigation/stop/action callbacks and progress/completion feedback | Keep robot state authoritative; handle stale feedback, interrupted work and partial physical effects. |
| Cancellation/reconciliation | Cancel request/response plus evolving task state | Observe terminal state, reconcile physical goods and business records, then release/reassign work. |

This applies as an architecture question to the existing tote-logistics, hospital-logistics and floor-cleaning cells. It supplies no evidence that these exact component versions are used in GXO, a hospital or ISS, nor that humanoid or LLM orchestration improves those workflows.

#### Executable implications and bounded next steps

The common economic engine should retain independently selectable inference/service cost, failed-attempt work, elapsed-time loss, integration cost and accepted-output probability. These findings do not calibrate their values. A source-side replay can test accounting and protocol invariants before any physical deployment study:

1. **Cost denominator:** reconstruct all 175 archived TAC rows and keep perfect/non-perfect counts. Join all model calls, NPC/evaluator usage and infrastructure restarts before labeling an amount total cost per accepted output. The saved JSON table reproduces the displayed-cost calculation offline.
2. **Clock:** require both action-clock and elapsed-clock fields, all-attempt distributions and target service deadlines. Reject a transfer from paused-simulation success to a continuously changing physical environment without target evidence.
3. **Transaction state:** exercise timeout-after-acceptance, duplicate business request, restart, late response and cancel-after-partial-execution cases against one pinned compatible RMF installation. Success means reconciled terminal state and no duplicate business effect, not merely HTTP success. These are proposed validation cases, not tests executed here.

The narrow audit stops after establishing the source pins, reconstructing the available denominator, sampling contrasting raw traces and tracing request/response/execution responsibilities. It did not spend API credits, install/run the benchmarks, execute ROS, operate robots, contact maintainers or obtain paid data. The 175 archived trajectories total about 308 MB compressed; only two were decompressed in this wave. A complete cost/latency replay and an installation-specific transaction test are deliberately separate work, not claimed validation.

The next priorities are: obtain an exact TAC run manifest and explain its effective monetary ceiling; obtain Astranomy's full failed/successful usage ledger and immutable policy configuration; then select one compatible RMF release set and a target operator's business-state contract. Stop a public-source branch when the required artifact is absent or inaccessible after the documented direct-source checks, preserving the null. Reopen it when a new artifact or target-site source becomes available. No outreach or physical experiment is authorized by this document.

Verification used Exa primary-page retrieval, direct public GitHub/raw-source inspection, local standard-library parsing, source hashing and arithmetic checks. A separate reviewer reproduced all 175 saved rows and totals, both sampled trace hashes, model/call counts, repricing and event spans, and checked the RMF control-flow claims. Review corrections added the declared cherry-pick and the no-response cancellation qualification; no benchmark or robot was executed. The requested model was GPT-6 Astra with Ultra reasoning; the serving model/reasoning configuration is not exposed for independent verification. Claims above rest on inspected sources, not a claimed serving identity.

#### Bounded conventional scheduling continuation

The [new comparison](research/conventional_coordination.md) replaces a deliberately weak comparator for one explicit synthetic service-window question. It exhaustively plans the observed queue, can wait and replan, and uses common funded resource/deadline/identity admission for conventional and synthetic delayed proposals. Twelve worlds and independent enumeration expose mandatory-service misses, overhead, failed work and stale proposals. This is complementary to the earlier transaction fixture: no installed RMF system, distributed durable authority, actual LLM uplift or real physical throughput is claimed.

### Focused follow-up: Annual digital scenarios and the moving adoption comparison

Authoritative analysis: [research/annual_digital_scenarios.md](research/annual_digital_scenarios.md).

Status: implemented synthetic mechanism, model 0.2.0. The annual-path design is now executable through the common model, local API, dashboard and exports. This is a new local implementation of the accepted design; the unpublished cloud edits described in the supplied handoff were unavailable and are not claimed as recovered or verified.

The decision question is whether a robot investment stays attractive as its human-led alternative also improves. A lower combined production cost is insufficient: the customer compares that cost and the commercial payments with the best available alternative, while delivery still depends on support, components, capital and installation. The model now makes that comparison at every year rather than holding the digital baseline fixed across the horizon.

#### Three changes with different meanings

The resolver accepts independently specified annual levels for human time reduction, signed robot coordination and the robot's additional digital-service expense. These replace their scalar settings in that year's economic ranking, site eligibility, manufacturing-demand signal and operating output. Omitted channels repeat the scalar. Capability and funded learning remain separate mechanisms. Full-horizon validation rejects missing periods, nonfinite values, booleans and unknown fields before fleet calculations; there is no smoothing or extrapolation.

Human efficiency reduces the hours required by the human comparator and the human work retained after robot service. Coordination changes accepted robot capacity before the site-demand cap; it can worsen. Robot digital-service expense changes resource costs and the model's assumed cost-plus payments. It is an annual service bundle, not a per-token inference price. Neither a cheaper service nor favorable coordination directly improves the capability index, hardware qualification or learning transfer.

These distinctions follow the [accepted design](research/digital_trajectory_design.md) and its [independent design review](reviews/digital_trajectory_design_review.md). The digital dossier's H-QJE-2025 and H-METR-2026 evidence motivates heterogeneous human baselines; H-COORDINATION-2026 and the [benchmark/coordination analysis](research/coordination_reproducibility.md) motivate signed coordination and a strong conventional comparison. The [learning-data analysis](research/learning_data_economics.md) separates collection from validated improvement. These sources constrain the interpretation. **None supplies the levels or slopes of the synthetic paths.**

#### What the six counterfactuals establish

The generated [annual comparison](model/results/annual_digital_comparison.json) contains six five-year paths with learning disabled: a constant reference, improving human efficiency, cheaper robot service, worsening coordination, a joint path, and the same improving human process with conventional robot control and no additional coordination/service bundle. Every result retains its complete inputs, annual comparisons, fleet ledger, source hashes and limitations.

In the tote-handoff illustration, an improving human baseline lowers both the human-only and combined resource costs. It can nevertheless close new robot admission because the human alternative improves more. The existing robot stock remains on the ledger. Its output can fall under the inherited current-eligibility cap; that is a limitation of the continuation rule, not evidence that an operator would optimally idle or retire the robot. The report's generated table shows the exact costs and gate outcomes without independently recalculating them.

A cheaper robot-service bundle can restore positive customer savings under the assumed contract. The resource saving and the allocation of that saving are different quantities: the cost-plus rule passes a price reduction to the customer and reduces the provider's dollar markup. The exact toy witness reduces resource expense by $1,500, raises customer cash gain by $1,725 and reduces provider recurring margin by $225. The net resource gain remains $1,500. This does not establish actual pricing, margin or bargaining behavior.

The conventional combined case is the relevant counterfactual for additional orchestration. It retains the same digitally improved human process, configured robot, maintenance, required control and site software. It removes only the added coordination effect and robot digital-service bundle. C10 alone is insufficient because it also removes the human digital improvement. The extra bundle may help or harm; the six scenarios do not identify a real scheduler's performance.

#### What changes for the research program

1. **Deployment sequencing:** screen adoption against the contemporaneous human-led alternative. A favorable robot-cost trend alone cannot justify faster penetration. Inspect the customer contract and payback gate as well as consolidated resource cost.
2. **Value capture:** separate changes in resource use from payments among customer, model supplier, OEM and operator. Workstream K's model-architecture comparison must price adaptation, inference, supervision and rights explicitly before attributing margin to a superior policy.
3. **Capability and learning:** keep service prices, policy capability and deployment-mediated exposure separate. A model company reporting lower inference expense supplies no automatic robot-capability coefficient. A larger simulated installed base supplies no empirical learning effect.

The annual path now addresses the evolving-comparator mechanism in W1-006. W1-TRAJECTORY remains partial: hardware retrofit diffusion, contract vintages, failure/recovery queues and engineering critical paths still need their own mechanisms and evidence. The separate [physical-requirements crosswalk](research/physical_requirements_crosswalk.md) now provides observed requirement and classification boundaries, without calibrating these paths. K dossiers remain subsequent research work.

#### Boundaries retained and verification

Annual admission and lifetime margins extrapolate current conditions over the assumed life; they are not forward-looking valuations of the entire path. All compatible installed cohorts receive current software and price conditions without upgrade expense or rollout delay. Supported available robot-hours remain the learning-exposure variable, even when economic eligibility suppresses accepted service. Annual capital budgets are nonrevolving allowances. The separate monthly entrant, treasury, monthly physical fixture and initial-year commercial tables are not one jointly feasible company model.

The [implementation review](reviews/annual_trajectory_review.md) records sole-agent verification rather than a new independent review. The frozen model 0.1.1 comparison covers every existing numerical field across eight presets and both onboard configurations. Adverse cases test overlap, harmful coordination, cost-only changes, stock retention after admission reversal, zero capability, invalid late periods and shared resource accounting under nonconstant paths. API/CLI replay and the browser's selected-year table, trace and export/reload use the same result object. No GPU, robot, external service or empirical calibration was executed.

**Next question:** which measured physical requirements and support obligations would change one of these adoption comparisons enough to alter the preferred deployment sequence or model-provider architecture?

### Focused follow-up: Competent conventional coordination and mandatory service

Authoritative analysis: [research/conventional_coordination.md](research/conventional_coordination.md).

The new service-window comparator strengthens the hospital decision pack and substantial Workstream H without treating an agent label as productive capability. A conventional solver can wait, batch eligible decisions, reserve scarce handoff time and replan after new arrivals. It deserves those abilities before an agent proposal is credited with an advantage. The bounded exact solver now has them under the [explicit request/resource contract](model/CONVENTIONAL_COORDINATION.md). This is a synthetic mechanism study; it does not estimate hospital performance or run an LLM.

#### Matched obligation before aggregate throughput

The reference has six due requests in twelve arbitrary five-minute ticks. One request requires a human courier and must finish within its short declared window. Routine work can use human transport or a robot mode with paid human loading and handoff. The complete request population, deadlines, resource calendars, funded executor and acceptance rules are common across policy comparisons. All twelve worlds retain the same $120 paid staffing commitment, including worlds that deliver nothing. Prices, durations, urgency and quality are assumptions rather than observations or recommendations for clinical practice.

The mixed conventional policy accepts four requests, including the mandatory request, for $151.30 of paid window resources. The human-only policy accepts three, also meeting the mandatory obligation, for $129.80. The additional $21.50 and one additional completion are an internally matched synthetic window contrast; they exclude asset lifecycle economics and cannot establish a robot investment return. Neither policy removes the paid shift.

Removing the courier reserve still permits four total completions and costs $150.80, but the mandatory request is missed. The $0.50 apparent saving cannot rescue a policy that violates the stipulated service contract. This is bounded **DE-CF02 urgent-human-fallback**: acceptance requires the due-request ledger and the mandatory-service gate, not a selected successful-robot count. The reserve is an ex-ante staffing policy and may leave capacity idle; it is not free capacity or an estimate of an optimal real roster.

#### Equal information, then explicit delay and overhead

At time zero the exact solver sees only the released routine queue. It chooses to wait until tick one before starting the first robot request because loading and handoff share the courier calendar. New requests become visible at their release tick, after which the policy replans. Independent enumeration verifies the finite objective on forty seeded small worlds. The optimality claim is local to each observed queue; unknown future arrivals, long-term capital decisions and real travel dynamics are outside it.

Changing only the controller label, with identical solver, delay and costs, leaves service, admissions, events and cash unchanged. A synthetic one-tick proposal delay accepts only three requests and misses the mandatory one. Keeping an immediate conventional urgent path restores four completions and the mandatory gate. Its thirteen total proposal calls fit the explicitly prepaid capacity assumption for twelve decision slots; no measured runtime or free real-world compute is inferred. Increasing planning-capacity cost by $1 per slot adds $12 to window cost without improving output.

These cases establish a testable hurdle for future agent proposals. A target system would need an observed information or decision-quality benefit large enough to cover proposal latency, verification, integration and recovery against this competent conventional alternative. No LLM or multi-agent uplift has been measured. The same solver with artificial delay is not a performance model of a named lab, architecture or product. A slower policy can sometimes benefit from later information in other unseen-event worlds, so the observed disadvantage is not a general theorem about latency.

#### Failed work, scarce funds and execution authority

A robot outage at tick three interrupts the first robot attempt. The model retains the spent attempt resources and partial-work disclosure, pays a $5 recovery bill and replans remaining work. Four attempts yield three accepted requests; total paid resources are $156.30, or $52.10 per accepted request within this restricted boundary. Failed work stays in the numerator and out of accepted output. Return time and authoritative stop are explicit assumptions, not an installed recovery guarantee.

A $150 opening-cash case accepts three requests while retaining the mandatory gate. With only $120, the already booked staff shift uses the funds; later required capacity cannot be booked, so execution is blocked. Paid idle staffing remains a real cash outcome. A separate test shows that unfunded recovery keeps the robot unavailable even when later work would otherwise be physically schedulable.

Adversarial fixed proposals test authority separately from planning quality. Duplicate business IDs, malformed identities and unobserved work are rejected; a delayed proposal that encounters a newly observed outage cannot execute from its old plan. Tentative planning reservations never become permission. A failed multi-action cash admission commits no subset. The complete input, observation, proposal, admission, attempt and payment traces permit auditing these distinctions.

#### Consequence for the three decision packs and thesis T4

For hospital logistics, keep the current provisional recommendation: qualify a bounded service and an improved human comparator, and wait on labor-substitution or scale claims until the real all-request service and staffing ledger supports them. The new model gives a concrete rejection mechanism: equal aggregate completions can conceal an unacceptable mandatory-service miss. It does not resolve custody, accompaniment, elevator access, infection control, wages, real urgency definitions or installed interoperability.

For utility inspection and automotive work, carry over the method of protecting the full obligation and shared resources, not these request durations or dollar figures. A mandatory residual visit or staffed quality check requires its own service-specific contract. Those counterfactual families remain open; a hospital-shaped toy queue is not their validation.

Thesis T4 now has a competent bounded scheduling comparator in addition to transaction-fault reasoning. The strongest remaining objection is that a real installation may already provide good scheduling and durable authority. The next evidence must measure what additional model-driven decisions improve, with physical accepted service and all recovery work in view. This checkpoint advances DE-06 and bounded DE-CF02, while acquisition/hybrid transitions, lifecycle costs, supply, endogenous learning, actual R&D qualification and broad campaign acceptance remain incomplete. The 168-hour campaign clock continues unchanged.

#### Empirical challenge: a human dispatcher with better information

**Edition 0.27, 8 November 2026.** H-PROXIMITY-2025 and H-COMP-01 extend the existing [digital evidence package](data/digital_evidence.json). The earlier exact queue remains a synthetic experiment with unchanged inputs and results. This follow-up changes the empirical comparison required for the hospital decision; it does not estimate an LLM or robot advantage.

[Sun and colleagues’ study](https://pmc.ncbi.nlm.nih.gov/articles/PMC12277699/) evaluates a conventional proximity rule in an existing hospital EMR. Its 136,414 analyzed completed patient requests concern peak weekday hours in 2021–23. Emergency, perioperative and critically ill transport handled by other teams are excluded. Raw mean elapsed time was 40.6 minutes before and 41.0 after; adjusted observational associations favor the intervention. Table 2 gives a 0.950 elapsed-time ratio, with confidence bounds differing from the narrative, and a 0.840 threshold-exceedance risk ratio. Two inpatient-floor groups had increased queue-jumping. Clinical outcomes and actual payroll removal are not established. The authors’ 8.3 person-hours interpretation aggregates estimated request-time reductions; arrival-to-patient timestamps do not support a full component decomposition. The main text refers cancellation analysis to a supplement that remains unread. Exact source locators and boundaries are retained in H-PROXIMITY-2025.

##### Elapsed request time and staff work have different units

A request's elapsed completion time includes waiting before a transporter is assigned. Multiple requests can wait concurrently while the same worker serves another patient. Summing their elapsed times counts request-minutes, not a unique allocation of staff minutes. Even assigned-to-completed intervals need a staff-activity boundary, including multi-person work and overlapping responsibilities, before becoming labor consumption. This is a dimensional objection to a labor interpretation, not a claim that shorter waits lack value.

Keep three economic consequences separate. A shorter wait can improve patient service. Less staff travel can release capacity if measured. Removing a paid commitment requires a feasible roster change while retaining required service. The first does not prove the second or third. An adjusted observational association supplies neither an implemented staffing decision nor the counterfactual wage bill. Lower observed active staffing during a later period is not proof that the intervention caused a reduction.

Do not substitute an inferred saving from rounded table means for the authors’ hour interpretation. Do not silently reconcile the narrative and Table 2 confidence bounds. Different subgroup-model results are not automatically duplicate estimates of one specification. Local qualification is justified as a research question while the source magnitude remains unsuitable for a target coefficient.

##### A stronger baseline changes what an entrant must demonstrate

Give the human-led alternative feasible dispatch improvements, reliable request identity, policy authority and measured implementation effort. Compare it with a robot-assisted workflow using the same complete request population, deadlines, custody and staffing obligations. A model-driven layer needs an incremental information or decision benefit after conventional rules, verification, latency and exception costs. An EMR connection is not itself that benefit.

The [hospital pack](research/deployment_entry_decisions.md#hospital-logistics--qualify-the-entire-obligation) now requires this comparator alongside funded repair fallback. Its proceed gate remains analytical qualification. Wait where complete due-request and staff-activity ledgers are unavailable. Reject an economic case that treats aggregate waiting-time reductions as removable paid hours or excludes requests made worse by its policy. A favorable average can coexist with an unacceptable mandatory-cohort outcome; queue position alone also does not establish patient harm. Each needs its own acceptance rule.

The proposed measurement protocol retains submitted, completed, cancelled, rejected and overdue identities; records staff activity, concurrency and paid schedules; and separately observes setup, layout maintenance, support and failure costs. Compare workload and time conditions, disclosing selection and confounding. No study is initiated here. Patient transport is not medication custody. Utility and automotive inherit the comparison method without hospital percentages or staffing assumptions.

The main HTML was retrieved through an ordinary public request and fingerprinted. Main methods, all three result tables, discussion and limitations were read. Linked paper and supplementary downloads returned HTML challenge pages rather than PDFs; no challenge was solved or bypassed. Supplement cancellation and sensitivity details remain unverified. Restricted patient/operational data were not obtained; regressions were not independently reproduced. This record strengthens substantial H while leaving empirical full-service economics and the campaign incomplete.

#### Reproduced observed-queue coordination worlds

| Synthetic service-window world | Accepted requests | Mandatory accepted | Failed attempts | Paid window resources, USD | Paid staff, USD |
| --- | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| mixed exact | 4 | 1 / 1 | 0 | 151.30 | 120.00 |
| human only exact | 3 | 1 / 1 | 0 | 129.80 | 120.00 |
| no urgent reserve | 4 | 0 / 1 | 0 | 150.80 | 120.00 |
| observed robot outage | 3 | 1 / 1 | 1 | 156.30 | 120.00 |
| matched proposal alias | 4 | 1 / 1 | 0 | 151.30 | 120.00 |
| delayed proposal | 3 | 0 / 1 | 0 | 150.30 | 120.00 |
| delayed with urgent fast path | 4 | 1 / 1 | 0 | 151.30 | 120.00 |
| extra proposal overhead | 4 | 1 / 1 | 0 | 163.30 | 120.00 |
| limited execution cash | 3 | 1 / 1 | 0 | 149.80 | 120.00 |
| unfunded window | 0 | 0 / 1 | 0 | 120.00 | 120.00 |
| duplicate and malformed guard | 1 | 1 / 1 | 0 | 147.80 | 120.00 |
| delayed stale outage guard | 0 | 0 / 1 | 0 | 151.80 | 120.00 |

Generated from [conventional_coordination.json](model/results/conventional_coordination.json). Exactness covers the currently observed bounded queue. Mandatory service remains a separate gate; all paid attempt, recovery and capacity costs remain. These synthetic window bills exclude lifecycle economics, and no LLM performance is measured.

### Focused follow-up: R&D progress, qualification and supported capacity

Authoritative analysis: [research/learning_qualification.md](research/learning_qualification.md).

Edition 0.21, 8 November 2026. This extends substantial Workstream H's direct contribution to robotics and joins I/K's configuration, supply and support questions. [The existing learning evidence package](data/learning_data_economics.json) owns LEC-005..008 and the proposed measurement contracts. No model, simulator or robot was run, and no deployment coefficient changes.

The strategic question is which R&D intervention changes accepted physical service, on which configuration, after paying for the complete path. A useful generated trajectory, a higher video score, an integrated policy server and an available supported robot are different outputs. Improvements at an early stage can matter greatly; the next stage must be demonstrated rather than assumed. This is especially consequential when a company replaces hardware, sells a general policy across bodies, or allocates scarce engineering between installed customers and new deployment.

#### What changes the existing learning-data conclusion

[DreamGen's CoRL proceedings paper](https://proceedings.mlr.press/v305/jang25a.html) supplies stronger evidence than a synthetic-data illustration: it reports physical policy experiments as well as simulation scaling. Its embodiment grounding, action labeling and downstream evaluation provide a concrete route from a video model to robot behavior. However, target low-data examples are not the complete inherited training investment. The GR1 pipeline uses a substantial pick-and-place seed; the Franka pipeline draws on DROID. Initial-frame preparation and target evaluation also remain work. LEC-005 records the individual stages and source-internal discrepancies.

The physical scores require particular care. Several task rubrics award partial credit, and the stated evaluation uses ten rollouts per checkpoint. A score increase is therefore not a measured change in binary factory acceptance or a confidence estimate for unattended reliability. Table 5 also includes an individual policy/task regression despite favorable aggregate results. The paper's main text, tables and appendix disagree on several counts, subsets and means. We preserve those differences rather than select a convenient exact uplift. Table 5 was checked visually against the downloaded proceedings PDF; the source fingerprint is recorded.

Its separate RoboCasa resource statement reports 54 hours on 1,500 L40 GPUs for 240,000 generated trajectories. Multiplying elapsed time by the stated allocation gives **81,000 allocated GPU-hours if the full allocation spans that interval**. This is arithmetic under the stated allocation, not measured GPU utilization, a monetary bill, or the cost of usable physical demonstrations. It excludes the rest of the pipeline and does not belong to the physical GR1 trial denominator. It cannot be multiplied by an assumed cloud price and presented as an observed R&D budget.

The economic implication is an additional possible substitution: compute can produce useful training material for selected targets. Its value depends on inherited data, filtering, action fidelity, training and physical acceptance. It does not eliminate the need to learn the target embodiment or imply that a business can obtain arbitrary validated skills by scaling generated-video count. This extends the earlier Cobalt/DexWild analysis without replacing their different populations or adding their reported improvements together.

#### Transfer needs both a target test and a retained-cohort test

[XEWorld v1](https://arxiv.org/html/2608.05799v1) supplies a useful counter-test. It holds robot embodiments out in controlled simulated scenes and separates video appearance, morphology and projected motion metrics. Target-only adaptation improves selected target metrics while degrading a seen-robot video metric under the stated no-replay protocol. These are learned-world-model diagnostics, not physical control-policy or commercial service results. Its fixed split and leave-one-embodiment-out results must remain separate. Image-plane tracking and excluded unreliable contact diagnostics also limit a claim about physical dynamics. LEC-006 preserves these boundaries.

The inference is narrower than “transfer fails.” A candidate can improve one target without preserving an installed cohort, and a convenient aggregate can hide that change. A qualification program should retain the old configuration's acceptance suite while testing the new target. If the deployment uses separate adapters or checkpoints, their compute, storage, monitoring and maintenance are real operating choices; the observed target-only forgetting result does not establish that such mitigations fail. Equally, asserting an adapter exists does not prove the mitigation works.

For the automotive pack, this means testing the same accepted component sets, timing and quality after a policy or hardware change. For inspection, a clearer generated image or improved visual metric must still yield accepted measurements for the required points, with review and residual duties preserved. For hospital logistics, a newer planner/controller must preserve urgency, identity and custody, including the retained fallback obligation. No metric is carried from a world-model benchmark into these service coefficients.

#### Integration tooling is useful evidence of work to do

NVIDIA's current [G1 reference course](https://docs.nvidia.com/learning/physical-ai/gr00t-e2e-workflow/latest/index.html) describes simulation and real-robot paths that are independent in that course version. The physical workflow names a specific robot, hands, onboard platform and controlled task setting. The [prerequisites](https://docs.nvidia.com/learning/physical-ai/gr00t-e2e-workflow/latest/getting-started/prerequisites.html) distinguish post-training GPU memory from rendering-compatible simulation hardware and onboard resources. The [simulation evaluation](https://docs.nvidia.com/learning/physical-ai/gr00t-e2e-workflow/latest/simulation-workflow/sim-evaluation.html) also pins a compatible checkout. LEC-007 records the retrieved pages and fingerprints; their publication dates are not supplied.

This makes the dependency graph more concrete. A reusable course can lower integration search and setup effort without establishing that one checkpoint crossed simulation to hardware. A successful installation on a different version, hand, camera layout or network is another configuration to qualify. Training capacity, simulation rendering and onboard inference have different compatibility and memory requirements. Pooling them into one generic GPU-hour or memory inventory can make an apparently funded plan physically impossible. Conversely, treating every deployment as requiring the entire research stack would overstate its marginal burden; distinguish reusable infrastructure from per-target adaptation and validation.

The [pinned GR00T-Ready release](https://github.com/nvidia-isaac/isaac-gr00t-ready/tree/8a92bbb2389e1b218a614611c8d40cd4caf342b2) provides a second boundary. Its release 0.3 self-assessment collects evaluator-supplied results; completion does not grant Verified status. The code review confirms scoring of recorded item/gate results and separate tier inclusion. Commercial availability, capacity/ramp and replacement-parts commitments are distinct framework requirements, not quantities established by downloading the toolkit. LEC-008 pins the inspected files. We neither evaluated a robot nor applied for a vendor status.

The framework is useful as a source of missing-artifact questions. It is not evidence that a particular OEM has passed them, a customer has contracted for support, or a general industrial/legal standard is satisfied. A technical compatibility score, vendor relationship and dated supported supply commitment should remain separate records. The latter needs a named configuration, quantity, delivery/qualification timing, replacement responsibility and service obligation. A roadmap statement cannot fill those fields.

#### Measure a complete R&D decision

Begin with the incumbent configuration and a fixed target service. Preserve the same demand, input state, quality and deadlines across candidate R&D programs. Specify what would count as improvement: more eligible tasks, less recovery work, faster validated changeover, lower resource demand or better retained performance. A new model name or a larger dataset is not itself the outcome.

| Stage | Required observation or resource boundary | What may be reused, and what must be paid again |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Eligible experience | Unique episodes, task/configuration, quality, failures and permitted reuse | Legitimately reusable data need not be recollected; duplicated records do not become independent experience. |
| Candidate construction | Generation attempts, action labels, filtering, training and human preparation | Shared pretrained assets can be sunk or licensed inputs; each actual adaptation run and its restrictions remain. |
| Target qualification | Held-out physical acceptance, timing, recovery and relevant adverse conditions | A source benchmark can inform test design; it does not supply the target result. |
| Retained-cohort regression | Old task/configuration outcomes after the proposed update | One target's gain cannot automatically be assigned to every installed unit. Separate checkpoints may carry separate support work. |
| Runtime and integration | Compatible sensors, action interfaces, calibration, power/thermal behavior and installed versions | Successful software bring-up is an intermediate milestone; sustained service still needs its own evidence. |
| Supported delivery | Available compatible units/parts, commissioning, rights, support capacity and cash dates | Orders and inventory remain distinct from ready supported capacity. Prior commitments constrain new starts. |

Count shared work once and incremental uses when they occur. Reusing a dataset for two valid purposes need not double its collection cost, but it can incur two training/validation programs. Do not also add a fictional data-asset premium on top of the resulting service gain. Real data licensing revenue, if observed, is a separate actor transaction with rights and costs, not an automatic social resource benefit.

The funding comparison needs every paid attempt, including rejected generation and failed qualification. The calendar comparison needs dependencies and contested physical resources. Parallel generation may shorten one stage while a fixed validation slot, missing compatible part or support commitment determines the date at which service can begin. Training throughput cannot by itself shorten that critical path. Trials conducted on an operating workcell can also displace production; a dedicated trial unit instead requires separate capacity and cash. These are alternatives to price explicitly, not reasons to reject research.

#### Four challenges prepared for executable testing

The existing campaign already has bounded workcell, inspection, coordination, finance and cohort mechanisms. The following contracts specify what remains to implement; they do **not** create new completed challenge families in this edition.

**No learning:** retain eligible experience and actual R&D expenditure while granting no validated capability increment. This distinguishes a failed investment from a policy that never attempted R&D. It prevents an expenditure or dataset count from automatically manufacturing an improvement.

**No transfer:** allow a candidate's accepted gain only on its validated target configuration. Installed cohorts keep their prior capability unless separately adapted and qualified. Transfer rights and technical compatibility are separate gates. Legitimate shared data is not charged twice merely because transfer fails; additional adaptation and validation are charged when performed.

**Support before expansion:** allocate one finite engineering and physical-validation calendar across installed support and candidate work. Compare explicit policies using the same resource pool. If candidate work displaces a support obligation, retain the missed service and committed staffing instead of silently creating substitute people. A different priority policy can alter timing without creating resources.

**Qualified supply delay:** preserve a financed order, its cash and physical identity when compatible delivery or qualification occurs too late. No order count becomes an operational unit before readiness; no later delivery backfills missed due service. This challenge needs an explicit delivery/commissioning/qualification boundary rather than a generic scalar shortage.

The next implementation should join these contracts to existing physical and Treasury mechanisms, with one service unit and no duplicated fleet. First test the strongest conventional improvement, such as better buffering or measured retooling, against the learning proposal. Then ask whether a funded R&D change yields enough additional accepted service, soon enough, to justify its opportunity cost. The current sources establish useful methods and important limits; they do not yet determine that economic choice.


#### Funded qualification follow-up, edition 0.22

The [executable learning follow-up](research/learning_execution.md) adds unique eligible operating data, a paid candidate, target/retained-cohort trials, delivered compatible runtime modules and finite support. It preserves source/target boundaries and all prior numerical scenarios. Candidate performance remains a disclosed hypothesis; real costs, rights, qualification distributions and supported scale remain unresolved.

### Focused follow-up: Funded learning, physical qualification and supported service

Authoritative analysis: [research/learning_execution.md](research/learning_execution.md).

Edition 0.22. This follows [the R&D qualification evidence audit](research/learning_qualification.md) with an executable path from operating experience to a candidate, physical acceptance and supported service. The sources distinguish useful generated data, policy scores, world-model diagnostics, integration and supported supply. The new calculation tests the economic consequences of preserving those distinctions. It does not estimate the learning rate or cost of any named model or robot.

The experiment uses the existing automotive workcell. Two persistent opening units owe the same three-part kits, quality and deadlines. One unit is the target of a proposed change; the other represents a retained configuration that might benefit from a separately qualified transfer. Three cycles contain 72 due customer-service kits. Actual picks, buffering, handoffs, changeovers and funding use the unchanged executor. Finite engineering, earlier eligible data, compatible module delivery and physical trials determine when a hypothesized candidate can operate. Between windows, buffered components remain identified inventory and recipe changes require a separately funded, capacity-limited reset crew. No fresh empty workcell or free setup is manufactured at a cycle boundary. The [methods](model/LEARNING_EXECUTION.md) and [complete replay artifact](model/results/learning_execution.json) expose every input and limitation.

#### A candidate can be useful without making data volume valuable by itself

The reference collects 22 eligible production-attempt episodes in its first cycle. It then pays for training and separate adaptation/qualification on the target and retained units. The proposed changeover improvement passes the declared fixture, and service reaches **56 of 72 kits on time** across the three cycles. Both units remain the same physical assets. The outcome is a conditional mechanism: a specified improvement reaches service only after its prerequisites. It is not evidence that 22 real episodes can train a useful controller.

The no-learning case collects data and pays for a candidate whose rates do not improve. Its failed physical trial adds cost while timely service stays at **24 of 72**, equal to the no-research operating baseline. Synthetic outlay is $5,280 versus $4,580 for no research under the same collection policy. This is why a budget, model run or larger dataset should not automatically create a capability increment in a business forecast. It is also why a failed research attempt and never attempting research cannot share the same cost ledger.

Tripling observation records leaves unique eligible identities, training inputs, resource allocation, cash and service unchanged. Training rights or valid sensing can instead reduce eligibility to zero even though collection was paid. Later observations do not retroactively alter the frozen training set. The reference collects fewer total episodes than some inferior-service cases because qualification uses engineering that would otherwise be available for collection. Comparing raw dataset size would therefore rank these decisions poorly. More useful service and more recorded data need not move together under a shared resource constraint.

The model preserves unsuccessful handoff attempts as distinct possible observations while counting each accepted service kit once. That separation matters empirically: operating hours, attempted manipulations, unique episodes, valid reusable data and accepted output answer different questions. None can replace the others without a measured conversion and an eligibility rule.

#### Transfer is a second decision, with a retained-customer obligation

When transfer is disallowed, only the target receives the qualified change and timely service reaches **40 of 72 kits**. The retained unit continues with its original configuration. A separate case gives the retained candidate a worse hypothetical changeover. Its paid trial fails; the old configuration again remains active. It delivers the same 40 timely kits as no transfer but incurs an additional failed test and adaptation burden. The experiment does not destroy a working cohort merely to demonstrate forgetting.

This implements the narrower inference from the source audit. A target gain can justify target deployment without proving fleet-wide improvement. Separate configurations, adapters and acceptance tests can preserve installed service, at additional cost. The XEWorld diagnostic does not supply the magnitude of the hypothetical regression here; nor does a successful synthetic fixture establish that a mitigation works in a real fleet. The decision requires actual target and retained-cohort results under the intended runtime, rights and service boundary.

#### Priority changes service even when eventual qualification is identical

At a shared allocation of **20 engineering hours per cycle**, support-first yields **48 timely kits**. Candidate-first yields **32**, although both eventually qualify two units. Candidate work uses the whole middle-cycle allocation under the latter policy, leaving both production units unsupported for that cycle. Their staffing obligations remain. The late service is not restored when support returns.

Support-first is not universally optimal. A service contract, urgency, a valuable new capability or a different demand profile could change the preferred allocation. The result demonstrates a concrete missing term in a research-only plan: an installed customer's support claim competes for the same people. Comparing only the final candidate count would miss the service loss. The reference allocator is intentionally simple and transparent; adaptive qualification resumes in later cycles, but no optimal continuous-time workforce schedule or expanded fleet is claimed.

This is also a resource distinction. Prioritizing engineering support does not reserve future payroll cash. A one-cent funding boundary in the tests changes whether training starts, and a funded training action can leave a later staff invoice unpaid. Financial feasibility must be checked at the dates obligations fall due, not inferred from sufficient total engineering hours or a positive eventual technical result.

#### Orders, physical slots and qualified capacity are different states

A funded one-cycle runtime-module delay lowers timely service to **32 kits** within this horizon. A module arriving after the horizon, or arriving incompatible, leaves the original 24-kit timely outcome. Payment remains in cash and the physical robot count remains two. The claim is bounded to compatible configuration inputs: it is not a complete OEM production, financing or spare-parts model.

Likewise, a **155-tick reserved validation slot** cannot admit the 156-tick test contract. Training, the target module and adaptation can already have been paid, yet qualification and its service gain remain unavailable. At 156 ticks the declared test can run. This switch is a property of the fixture's reservation requirement, not a measured test-duration threshold. It exposes why faster model generation may not shorten time to usable service when another physical stage controls admission.

Qualification itself consumes work. Its accepted test kits are excluded from customer demand and training. Disjoint job/component identities prevent direct reuse, but the task pattern is shared with production. These deterministic tests do not establish statistical generalization, unattended reliability or safety. A real deployment decision would need independent adverse conditions, sufficient repeated trials and measured distributions of recovery and support.

#### Keep the strong conventional option in the decision

An already-qualified conventional fast-retool configuration meets **all 72 deadlines** with the same two units and fixed staffing commitment. It avoids the research ramp in this short comparison. Its availability and implementation burden are assumptions, and the fixture does not estimate the cost of obtaining that qualification. This is a disciplined operating comparator, not a proof that conventional equipment is always cheaper or that learning has no option value.

For the automotive decision pack, proceed only when the candidate improves the specified complete-kit service beyond an available conventional configuration after its qualification, support and cash burdens. A research investment can still be justified by broader task range or future changes, but those benefits need their own demand and acceptance evidence. Do not assign them automatically because the platform is general-purpose.

The hospital and utility packs inherit the **test structure**, not these rates or outcomes. Hospital candidates must preserve urgency, identity, custody and fallback; utility candidates must produce accepted point measurements with review and residual physical duties. A policy score, a collected episode or an integrated model is still an intermediate output. Both packs need their own eligibility, trial-resource, retained-cohort and supported-delivery records before this mechanism can be populated with operating evidence.

#### What this closes and what remains open

Sixteen exact worlds add bounded DE-CF14 no-learning/no-transfer and DE-CF09 qualified-configuration supply delay. The shared support priority strengthens the partial DE-CF10 mechanism. Twelve named families now have bounded implementations; the 111 total worlds do not close empirical whole-system costs or full campaign acceptance. Earlier scenarios and source claims are preserved.

The next decision-changing investigation should seek an actual sustained deployment whose operator, provider, hardware configuration, support obligation and change history can be connected. It should identify what changed in accepted service after an update, what stayed on the old configuration, what the deployment consumed and what was paid or still owed. Until then, candidate gains, trial thresholds, engineering supply, module delivery, rights and costs remain explicit hypotheses. A successful replay verifies the accounting and causal gates of that hypothesis, not the real-world business case.

## 6. Digital infrastructure and physical production

Authoritative chapter: [research/digital_infrastructure.md](research/digital_infrastructure.md).

Research edition 0.1, retrieved 25 October 2026. This is the H1/H6 infrastructure contribution to the campaign, joined to I's configured-system supply analysis. Evidence records HI-001–HI-006 live in `data/digital_infrastructure_evidence.json`. Quantitative observations below do not calibrate the illustrative robot model.

### The decision

For a humanoid company, the relevant question is not whether AI infrastructure is growing. It is whether the particular compute, memory, power, manufacturing and operating complements required by a proposed production system will be available at acceptable cost and latency. The reverse question—whether robots can accelerate construction, manufacture or operation of AI infrastructure—needs its own service-level evidence. Neither direction follows automatically from growth in the other.

The current evidence supports a constrained, two-way relationship. Digital capability production already depends on large physical investments and grid connections. Robot policy development can use that infrastructure, but an onboard controller and a cloud accelerator do not consume interchangeable memory modules. Robotics might help selected infrastructure workflows, yet the public sources reviewed here do not measure a humanoid-induced reduction in data-center construction time or commissioning cost. That link remains a question, not a numerical feedback coefficient.

### What the latest infrastructure evidence establishes

The IEA's *Key Questions on Energy and AI*, published 16 April 2026, estimates global data-center electricity consumption at 485 TWh in 2025 and projects approximately 950 TWh in 2030. The latter is a central projection, not observed consumption or an independently booked pipeline. The population is all data centers, not exclusively LLM inference. Its estimate of 17% growth in total data-center electricity consumption in 2025 and 50% growth in AI-focused consumption describes different populations. Adding the two would double count the AI subset. [HI-001](https://www.iea.org/reports/key-questions-on-energy-and-ai/executive-summary)

The report describes simultaneous efficiency improvement and growth in more intensive workloads. It states that energy per individual AI task has fallen rapidly, while reasoning, video and agentic applications can use much more energy per request than simple text generation. These are not contradictory observations. Aggregate demand depends on service volume, output mix, quality requirements, retries and hardware/software efficiency. A cheap text query is therefore an inappropriate proxy for a long-horizon robot planning episode or an enterprise-agent workflow. No universal energy-per-token or energy-per-robot coefficient is inferred here.

IEA identifies grid access, energy equipment, advanced chips and high-bandwidth memory among constraints, and describes financing as consequential as projects exceed internally funded balance-sheet capacity. Its discussion of an HBM shortage extending at least through 2027 is a dated assessment, not a measured allocation to a robot OEM. The appropriate model input is an accessible qualified resource pool with lead times and contractual commitments, not a share of global semiconductor sales. Regional and application differences remain essential.

The October 15, 2026 TSMC management report supplies a more recent financial observation. It reports third-quarter consolidated capital expenditure of US$13.84 billion and year-to-date expenditure of US$40.64 billion. It reports 4.476 million 12-inch-equivalent wafer shipments in the quarter, with HPC representing 68% of net revenue. Those are distinct denominators: expenditure is not qualified capacity; wafer-equivalent shipments are not AI accelerators; HPC revenue is not exclusively robotics or even exclusively AI. The report attributes a sequential gross-margin decline partly to the initial N2 ramp. This is an actual example of new-process ramp affecting economics, not evidence for a universal robotics manufacturing learning rate. [HI-002](https://investor.tsmc.com/english/encrypt/files/encrypt_file/qr/phase4_reports/2026-10/f53a64fd40fc0b453f94e077e5aba8cad506f27b/3Q26%20ManagementReport.pdf)

The decision consequence is to separate four states in supplier diligence: announced investment, expenditure already made, qualified output, and output actually available to the customer. Even a profitable expanding supplier does not establish an individual robot firm's access to a specific grade, package or delivery window. Conversely, an aggregate AI bottleneck does not prove every embedded module is unavailable. The present evidence cannot quantify an OEM's priority, contracted price, inventory or qualification yield.

Micron's September 30, 2026 prepared remarks make the contractual allocation problem more concrete. Management says it has signed 26 multiyear take-or-pay strategic customer agreements, estimates they represent more than 35% of revenue through 2030, and reports US$32 billion of customer financial commitments, mostly deposits. Those are issuer disclosures and forward estimates with different meanings. Deposits cannot be added to future revenue as another economic benefit. The company says agreements cover the vast majority of its calendar-2027 HBM bit supply; that does not reveal an individual robot firm's allocation or establish a general LPDDR shortage coefficient. [HI-006](https://s25.q4cdn.com/621799436/files/doc_financials/2026/q4/Q4-FY26-Prepared-Remarks.pdf)

The same source distinguishes initial wafer output from meaningful production ramp several quarters later. Initial output dates for Idaho, Japan, Singapore and New York are management expectations spanning 2027–2030, not current qualified deliveries. This supports a supply-model structure with lead times, ramp and pre-existing commitments, but does not calibrate the timing of a humanoid OEM's production. It also shows a financing mechanism: customer commitments may support supplier investment while tying up customer cash and limiting uncommitted supply available to entrants. Whether a new robot company can secure similar terms is unknown.

Micron predicts that future humanoids could have memory requirements comparable to highly autonomous vehicles. That is a supplier's prospective market thesis, not a measured humanoid bill of materials. The configured products below are concrete counterweights to converting that thesis into a fixed per-robot memory requirement. Future architectures might use more onboard memory, smaller policies, remote inference or heterogeneous processors; workload and latency evidence must decide.

### Memory and compute are configuration-specific

The NVIDIA product pages retrieved at kickoff make the nonfungibility concrete. Jetson AGX Orin 64GB is specified with 64GB LPDDR5 and 204.8 GB/s memory bandwidth, with configurable module power in the 15–60W range. Jetson T5000 is specified with 128GB LPDDR5X and 273 GB/s at 40–130W. H200 instead uses 141GB HBM3e with 4.8 TB/s bandwidth. These are manufacturer specifications of different products, not measured performance in a matched robotics workload. [HI-003](https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/autonomous-machines/embedded-systems/jetson-orin/), [HI-004](https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/autonomous-machines/embedded-systems/jetson-thor/), [HI-005](https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/data-center/h200/)

Several comparisons would be misleading. Orin TOPS and Thor FP4 TFLOPS are not a common precision/performance measure; a ratio of their advertised values does not identify a robot-speed improvement. Module power is not total robot power, which also includes actuation, sensors and other loads. Installed memory is not all available to model weights: runtime, sensor processing, intermediate activations and state compete for it. Remote inference may relax onboard capacity but introduces connectivity, latency, service-dependence and recurring-cost questions. An inference-capable module does not establish qualified operation in heat, vibration, dust or the target software stack.

The Orin live page itself contains differing introductory and tabular specifications for some NX/Nano variants. This edition uses the clearly identified AGX Orin 64GB fields and does not combine values from different variants or modes. Product-page claims remain dated snapshots; exact carrier board, JetPack release, model precision, thermal design, duty cycle and actual robot installation remain necessary before modeling throughput. The H200 page's illustrative performance comparisons change batch sizes and workload settings; they are not matched per-request latency experiments for robotics.

### A common resource graph with separate units
