The relevant graph starts with physical resources and ends with an accepted customer service:

| Layer | Unit to preserve | Qualification or boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Capital allocated to digital infrastructure | currency by funding date and owner | Budget, committed funding and spent cash are different states. |
| Fabrication and packaging | qualified dies/packages by process and period | A generic wafer-equivalent total cannot serve every design. |
| Datacenter accelerators and HBM | configured accelerator-hours and compatible memory | Training and remote inference share only explicitly common pools. |
| Datacenter power and cooling | available kW and delivered kWh by site/time | Connection rights, energized capacity and utilization differ. |
| Onboard compute and memory | modules and memory/bandwidth by configuration | LPDDR embedded configurations are not HBM inventory substitutes. |
| Robot integration | engineering/validation hours and compatible releases | A better model reaches a fleet only after integration and validation. |
| Productive operation | accepted services at stated quality and deadline | Outputs remain limited by physical control, support and demand. |

Actor identities may recur across layers. A platform provider can sell modules, support training software and supply inference products. That does not establish a common bottleneck, a single transfer price or a consolidated share of customer value. Similarly, expenditure reported by a cloud customer and revenue reported by its chip supplier describe a transaction along the chain, not two independent contributions to final economic surplus.

In the common scenario engine, resource quantities must remain explicitly synthetic unless source records establish the population, grade, contract boundary and period. A hypothetical remote-inference quota can test whether a larger fleet would exhaust service capacity; it must not be presented as the fraction of global AI compute available to robotics. The engine should disable unsupported hardware-choice productivity multipliers rather than infer them from TOPS.

### Three bounded mechanisms and their counterarguments

**Lower reasoning cost may improve robot economics without changing robot capability.** The source-side evidence supports hardware/software efficiency improvement and changing workload mix. The target requires measured calls per accepted service, retry rates, latency, support and the relevant tariff. A counterexample is a mechanically constrained workflow where inference is a small cost and cheaper calls do not change accepted throughput. Permitted use: scenario structure and a research question, not a copied cost decline.

**Physical infrastructure can delay digital contributions to robots.** Training or remote inference may depend on constrained datacenter capacity and financing. Yet a particular deployed policy may run onboard, use existing contracted capacity, or be small enough that other constraints dominate. This mechanism should be tested with local versus remote architectures and documented allocations. It cannot justify imposing the same HBM delay on all Jetson-equipped robots.

**Robots might relieve infrastructure labor bottlenecks.** Candidate market stages include construction installation, equipment manufacturing, inspection and maintenance. A faster robot process only accelerates the completed facility if that process is on the critical path, its quality and permits remain acceptable, and upstream equipment or grid delays do not bind instead. Existing operator evidence of inspection or a construction task demonstration does not establish whole-project acceleration. The falsifier is a matched project comparison in which the robotic stage improves but energization or output does not. No positive feedback growth exponent is assigned.

### Strategic implications and remaining evidence

For RQ-SC-05, qualify a workload/configuration before selecting an onboard compute roadmap. Memory, latency, power and thermal requirements should precede peak-compute comparisons. For RQ-LLM-01/06, separate capital announcements from operational supply and customer access. For RQ-LLM-09 and the construction/utility cells, test an explicit critical path before claiming that physical automation accelerates AI development. These conclusions narrow the scenarios even without estimating a global robotics market.

The next useful public-evidence step is a matched robotics workload on a pinned embedded module and software stack, documenting memory use, end-to-end latency, sustained power and accepted output. A parallel supplier step is to identify disclosed product-specific qualification and delivery evidence. Neither an aggregate capex series nor a product page supplies those measurements. Public data may not resolve OEM allocations or negotiated commercial terms; those fields stay null and become later access-dependent questions.

### Focused follow-up: Onboard workload qualification and operating deadlines

Authoritative analysis: [research/onboard_workload_qualification.md](research/onboard_workload_qualification.md).

Research edition 0.1, 25 October 2026. This bounded H/I follow-up addresses RQ-SC-05 and the robot/control contribution map. It adds one same-board policy comparison and an explicit qualification gap; it does not update scenario coefficients. The associated records are `data/onboard_workload_qualification.json`. Requested worker configuration was GPT-6 Astra / Ultra; actual serving identity and effort remain unverified. Repository provenance is recorded separately in ignored `sync_receipt.json`.

#### Finding and decision

A primary NVIDIA recipe makes a substantially stronger claim possible than the Orin/Thor specification comparison in HI-003/004: a particular GR00T N1.7 policy pipeline can be exported, quantized and benchmarked on a Jetson AGX Thor Developer Kit using inspectable code. The tutorial reports approximately 81 ms for its baseline TensorRT pipeline and 39.9 ms for an optimized mixed-precision pipeline on that same device. These are vendor-reported short benchmark results, not measurements reproduced here. The intervention combines graph restructuring and precision changes; it does not identify a pure hardware or quantization effect. [OWQ-E01](https://github.com/NVIDIA-AI-IOT/jetson-ai-lab/blob/2fcbe216dbaf6301103e03a177a1980a426c6ae7/src/content/tutorials/vla/groot_n17_on_thor.md#performance)

The important limit emerges from the released harness. Its quantity called “E2E” is the elementwise sum of separately measured preprocessing, backbone and action-head timing arrays. The default recipe repeats the first observation of the first recorded episode. The published median is therefore not a measured sensor-to-actuator cycle, sustained policy frequency or hard deadline guarantee. No recovered artifact supplies a long-duration latency distribution together with board power, temperature, memory occupancy, competing workloads and accepted physical output. This is a useful partial result: a qualified optimization candidate exists, while the required deployment measurement remains missing. [OWQ-E02](https://github.com/NVIDIA/Isaac-GR00T/blob/9c7e746b2cd37a810070a98ef41d290a07e806c2/scripts/deployment/benchmark_inference.py#L168)

#### The matched source comparison

The tutorial file was last changed on 13 August 2026; its optimization patch was added on 31 July. Those are source-file commit dates, not disclosed experiment dates. The upstream code is pinned to 8 July 2026. Actual experiment dates are unspecified. The exact source revisions and SHA-256 content digests are recorded in the evidence package.

| Item | Published configuration or result | Qualification boundary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Device and operating mode | Jetson AGX Thor Developer Kit, JetPack 7.2; MAXN; `nvpmodel -m 0`, `jetson_clocks` | Exact tested board revision, measured watts, cooling, ambient temperature and thermal soak are not supplied. MAXN is a mode, not measured power. |
| Workload | `GR00T-N1.7-LIBERO/libero_10`, LIBERO_PANDA; one camera, batch one, four denoising steps | A simulated manipulation checkpoint and recorded input; no matched production assembly/tote result. Exact checkpoint and dataset revisions are not pinned by the download commands. |
| Software | CUDA 13.0 container base, PyTorch 2.10.0, TensorRT 10.15.1.29; ModelOpt 0.39.0 for quantization | The recipe identifies versions but does not provide the original container digest, generated-engine hashes or complete measured-run manifest. |
| Baseline TensorRT | Approximately 81 ms reported E2E; backbone 27.0 ms, action head 45.0 ms | Baseline recipe uses FP32 vision, BF16 language/action head and BF16 auxiliary components. |
| Optimized TensorRT | 39.9 ms median, 41.0 ms mean, 1.8 ms standard deviation in expected output; backbone 13.56 ms, action head 17.18 ms medians | Mixed recipe uses FP8 vision, NVFP4 language/action-head weights with selected FP8 layers, FP16 auxiliary components; graph restructuring also changes execution. |
| Measurement population | Twenty measured iterations per timing array; advertised five warm-up iterations | Code actually uses ten preprocessing warm-ups, five eager warm-ups, seven compiled warm-ups and at least ten TensorRT warm-ups under defaults. |

All numbers in this table refer to the tutorial's reported configuration or its published recipe. None is a robot duty-cycle measurement. The optimized runtime hoists cross-attention keys/values, precomputes timestep constants, fuses projections and captures the action head in a CUDA graph. These changes and mixed precision are bundled; the two-row comparison cannot assign their separate effects. The reported cosine similarity of 0.999743 is a forward-output comparison, not an accepted manipulation success rate. [OWQ-E01; OWQ-E02]

The recipe is materially inspectable. We downloaded source text only, checked the static patch against its pinned repository copy, applied it to the thirteen relevant source files, and parsed the twelve resulting Python files without importing or executing the workload. The patch applied successfully; raw downloads lacked an executable bit expected on one export script, producing a file-mode warning rather than a content rejection. This verifies a source patch, not an install, engine build, inference result or license clearance. No model weights, datasets, GPU runtime or hardware were used.

#### What the timing includes—and what it omits

`benchmark_components` times backbone and action-head calls with CUDA synchronization before and after each stage. Input processing is benchmarked separately and its same timing array is reused across backend comparisons. `compute_e2e_from_components` then sums those arrays; the summary takes the median of the sums and computes frequency as `1000 / median_ms`. It does not time a single uninterrupted production request. Adding component medians would also not necessarily reproduce the median of their sums. [OWQ-E02, functions `benchmark_data_processing`, `benchmark_components`, `compute_e2e_from_components`, `main`]

The recipe invokes `BenchmarkConfig` without enabling `use_trajectory`, whose default is false; the harness extracts episode zero, step zero. A trajectory option exists, but its existence does not establish that the published latency used it. Model loading, engine construction, video capture, observation freshness, network serving, final action denormalization/robot command transport, actuator dynamics and safety handling are outside the measured component sum. The code times `action_head.get_action` and discards its result; it does not measure motor execution or a completed task. There are no recovered per-iteration result arrays tied to the published run.

For the tutorial's advertised 25 Hz, the corresponding nominal interval is 40 ms. The reported 39.9 ms median leaves only 0.1 ms against that interval, while the reported mean is 1.0 ms above it. These are arithmetic comparisons using rounded, component-sum statistics, not a target robot's validated deadline or an estimate of the fraction of missed cycles. The source frequency of 25.1 Hz is the rounded reciprocal of 39.9 ms. Neither a reciprocal median nor a mean determines sustained throughput under queueing, thermal limits and contention. A robot might execute an action chunk with a faster independent low-level controller, but the correct scheduling and feedback contract must then be specified and tested; the nominal policy-call frequency is not its motor-control frequency.

Memory qualification is similarly incomplete. The tutorial's approximately 60 GB free-space recommendation concerns disk storage for containers, engines and assets, not peak runtime RAM. The pipeline's 8192 MB builder-workspace default is a build limit, not a measured runtime footprint. The harness explicitly deletes prior policy instances to avoid holding two full copies simultaneously, with a comment referring to Orin out-of-memory behavior. That comment is evidence of a software resource precaution, not a measured Orin/Thor capacity comparison. HI-003/004 remain the source of module memory specifications; actual memory occupancy, headroom for perception and worst-case concurrent workloads remain null. [OWQ-E01 Hardware; OWQ-E02 `PipelineConfig.workspace`, policy cleanup]

#### Conflicts and adverse applicability evidence

The downloadable patch contains a second performance table: 93.8 ms baseline TensorRT and 38.9 ms optimized mixed NVFP4, with a 144.9 ms eager baseline. Its own header says JetPack 7.2. The tutorial instead reports approximately 81 ms, 39.9 ms and approximately 126 ms and explains a prior 2.41× comparison as a JetPack 7.1 baseline. Without run manifests, that explanation does not resolve why the patch table itself names 7.2. We retain separate source tables and an unresolved provenance mismatch; we do not select the most favorable endpoints or calculate a cross-table speedup. [OWQ-E03](https://github.com/NVIDIA-AI-IOT/jetson-ai-lab/blob/522b964711d2a02cd880f0a2f2cea7ef313d14fc/public/code-samples/groot_n17_on_thor/groot-n17-trt-optimization.patch), added README “Optimized TensorRT Result”]

The patch also reports 97.3% success versus 97.5% for PyTorch, averaged over three rounds of a 200-episode **LIBERO Spatial** closed-loop benchmark on Thor. This is a different named task suite from the `libero_10` timing checkpoint. No associated trial-level outcomes, seeds, exact evaluated checkpoint digest or statistical noninferiority test were recovered from these artifacts. The tutorial's “no measurable loss” statement cannot establish equivalent physical task competence, nor can the Spatial result be attached silently to the timed checkpoint. It is retained as an attributed vendor claim with a distinct population. [OWQ-E03]

The tutorial itself gives deployment counterevidence: the bundled policy server accepts no TensorRT-engine path and serves PyTorch only. Serving the optimized runtime requires a separately implemented wrapper. Its warning about older patched scripts hanging or segfaulting during interpreter shutdown also shows why output timing does not qualify process lifecycle behavior; the inspected patch adds explicit engine cleanup. Neither issue proves failed deployment, but both create integration work excluded from the latency table. [OWQ-E01, “Deploying in Your Own Code” and Step 10]

As a bounded alternative screen, NVIDIA's Isaac ROS 5.0 performance documentation is unusually explicit about its own metric: throughput targets less than 5% frame loss, can include runs slightly above that target, and averages five runs after discarding the minimum and maximum. “Latency” is first-sent-frame to first-received-frame in a separate 30 Hz trial, omitted without a valid measurement. This is not a percentile distribution of all steady-state frames. Its publicly listed Orin/Thor rows therefore do not resolve the missing deadline qualification merely because they have millisecond labels. No cross-board performance ratio is imported into this follow-up. [OWQ-E04](https://nvidia-isaac-ros.github.io/v/release-5.0/performance/index.html#methodology)

#### Consequences for the existing economic cells

For `CELL-M05-auto-assembly-US-2025` and `CELL-M08-3pl-tote-US-2025`, the source supports investigating an onboard policy optimization before committing to a larger compute module or remote inference architecture. It does not establish accepted assemblies or totes per hour. The target tests require the exact camera/proprioception inputs, action horizon, control/replanning contract, concurrent perception load, safety/recovery process and accepted-output denominator. Quantization must be evaluated on that task distribution, including consequential failures. These are model-structure and diligence implications, not empirical coefficients.

The actual Unitree G1/Go2 configurations in `physical_evidence.json` have no evidenced installation of this Thor/LIBERO pipeline. Optional Orin language on a product page cannot establish compatibility with the inspected NVFP4 engines; the tutorial explicitly requires rebuilding architecture-specific TensorRT engines when hardware or static batch changes. Neither configuration inherits the benchmark. Conversely, a smaller conventional controller or a specialized fixed machine can remain sufficient for the matched service. Module selection should follow the workload and deadline rather than the newest platform label.

The highest-value missing artifact is a single immutable run bundle: board/carrier revision and power configuration; checkpoint/dataset/container/engine hashes; actual action horizon and sensor input shapes; raw timestamp and deadline-miss records; memory/power/thermal traces over a stated duration; and matched closed-loop outcomes with retries and human assistance. Its collection would require a separately authorized execution environment; this CPU-only investigation does not initiate it. A second priority is reconciliation of the two published tables and the Spatial-versus-`libero_10` success linkage. Accessible OEM supply, negotiated prices, supported deployment scale and full customer service costs remain separate unknowns. The bounded conclusion is an inspectable optimization recipe with incomplete execution provenance and unqualified sustained operation, not an onboard-control performance certification.

## 7. Physical supply, embodiments, quadrupeds and drones

Authoritative chapter: [research/physical_supply_field.md](research/physical_supply_field.md).

Edition 0.2 · Evidence retrieved 25 October 2026 · Input repository `campaign launch snapshot (see ignored sync_receipt.json)`.

This is an intermediate research contribution to I/J, not completion of the seven-day campaign. The structured record is [physical_evidence.json](data/physical_evidence.json). The requested configuration was GPT-6 Astra with Ultra reasoning; the worker inherited its configuration and has no runtime identity/effort inspection. Available delegation metadata lists Astra/Ultra, but that does not independently verify this worker's actual setting.

### Findings that change deployment judgment

The strongest evidence supports choosing a complete service configuration before choosing a robot morphology. In the cases below, mobility, measurement, interpretation, action and business value are successive conditions. Success at an earlier condition cannot substitute for failure at a later one. PG&E's drone test is particularly informative: an inspection aircraft can fly successfully while producing unusable inspection imagery. A model crediting every successful mission with a completed inspection would overstate useful output before reaching any debate about manufacturing cost.

Quadrupeds have direct productive roles in manufacturing and infrastructure. They need not be justified as a temporary precursor to humanoids. The examined customer accounts support sensing, facility information and remote inspection roles. They do not establish a general replacement rate for production or maintenance workers. A humanoid strategy should therefore distinguish entry into existing sensing services from expansion into manipulation and intervention. The second step changes the accepted output, qualification requirements and physical interfaces; it is not a free extension of the first.

The sources also separate available robots from supported productive capacity. Site mapping, route validation, networking, operator training, human review and spare parts can bind even when chassis supply is ample. A manufacturing announcement does not remove those constraints. Conversely, a more capable policy may widen the feasible service set while increasing onboard memory, power, sensors or support requirements. The same advance can expand addressability and make an existing configuration inadequate.

### Service boundaries and the shared atlas

Cases link to the common atlas without adding independent labor totals. BMW supports the manufacturing anchor `CELL-M05-auto-assembly-US-2025`, but its observed service is UK plant maintenance and information capture, not US assembly output. National Grid, Equinor and PG&E inform `CELL-M03-utility-inspection-US-2025`; Equinor's CO₂ facility is an infrastructure comparator rather than an electrical-utility employment observation. Rwanda informs `CELL-M10-hospital-logistics-US-2025`, with interfacility distribution kept separate from movement inside a hospital. Agricultural application is a bounded service-business comparator within the atlas's agriculture family, not a seventh deep market estimate.

Three rules follow. Keep foreign evidence in its observed geography. Preserve the historical service window instead of treating retrieval in 2026 as current field performance. Allocate an inspection, delivery or treated application once even when several robots and people contribute. A drone observing an asset, a quadruped collecting another viewpoint and an engineer approving maintenance can be one service chain, not three sources of avoided labor.

### Quadruped dossiers

#### PHY-Q01 — BMW Hams Hall: repeated customer-reported use

BMW's 9 February 2026 account identifies continuing SpOTTO work since 2024: visual, thermal and acoustic collection supports maintenance and the plant digital twin. Experts use the information for planning and quality work. This is stronger than a launch announcement because the customer describes an ongoing role after introduction. It remains customer narrative rather than an audited mission dataset. [PHY-E01: BMW](https://www.press.bmwgroup.com/global/article/detail/T0455457EN/production-milestone-for-bmw-group-plant-hams-hall:-british-engine-plant-celebrates-25-years?language=en).

The economic boundary should begin with accepted observations and their use in a decision. Robot-specific capital, recurring support, inspection acceptance, intervention, downtime and before/after staffing are undisclosed. The plant-wide energy improvement is not a robot treatment effect. A larger or richer digital twin could create value through changed planning while leaving payroll unchanged; that is a different business case from labor substitution.

For an entrant, the relevant complements are access to the plant, its equipment history and decision systems, plus integration authority. Equal chassis access would not instantly reproduce those complements. For the incumbent, pre-existing systems and engineers could lower integration effort, but legacy interfaces could also increase it. Neither direction warrants an ownership multiplier. The next useful observation is the cost and acceptance of one recurring route, including reviewer time and resulting actions.

#### PHY-Q02 — National Grid Blyth: a bounded trial, not an established fleet

National Grid's 17 February 2023 primary account reports a three-day North Sea Link converter-site trial, with Roboverse Reply assisting. Visual, thermal, acoustic and lidar collection were tested; results were still being assessed before an investment decision. The direct page timed out/returned 403, but its primary text was recovered through the search index. [PHY-E02: National Grid](https://www.nationalgrid.com/who-let-the-dogs-out).

The trial boundary is a useful negative constraint on interpretation, not evidence that the project failed. There is no verified later continuation, abandonment, accepted inspection count or return on investment in this edition. Do not combine it with US Sandy Pond narratives as if both were one operating cohort. The examined record establishes a test of asset access and sensing under site conditions, not continuous operation.

The possible economic advantage is broader access to information while equipment operates. Valuing it requires the actual alternative: inspection during an outage, remote views, fixed sensing or other access arrangements. An avoided outage cannot be booked merely because a robot entered an energized area. The outage would have to be attributable to the replaced inspection requirement and actually avoided. This is a high-priority follow-up because trial-to-routine conversion is more decision-relevant than another demonstration of locomotion.

#### PHY-Q03 — Northern Lights: commissioning and operational authority

Equinor's 13 January 2026 description says Roberta needs local route and site training, virtual and physical testing, and training for the operating team. Sture operators can dispatch inspections. More independent fault detection and mission planning are described as goals. The text does not establish an OEM/model revision, mission denominator or uninterrupted unattended service. [PHY-E03: Equinor](https://www.equinor.com/magazine/svein-ivar-the-robot-trainer).

This case identifies a commissioning production function: the purchased system supplies general ability, but useful output requires site-specific configuration and people with operating authority. The distinction matters for expansion. Reusing a route or configuration at a similar site may save engineering, but differences in equipment, hazards and response procedures can recreate work. Count that reuse as a testable reduction in commissioning hours, not an assumed universal fleet-learning coefficient.

Remote inspection also changes where labor occurs. Fewer trips to the inspected area can coexist with control-room dispatch, review and exception work. A service model must retain those activities. Data rights and operational responsibility are not disclosed, so no learning rent or independent operator margin is assigned. The public source supports a constrained role and required complements; it cannot calibrate a supervisor-to-robot ratio.

Woodside was investigated as an additional case. Its Spector URL now redirects to a news index; a primary indexed excerpt supports image delivery through FUSE, but the full source was not recovered. It remains an access gap rather than a fourth completed dossier (PHY-E22).

### Drone dossiers and the limits of their economic evidence

#### PHY-D01 — PG&E: successful flight can fail the service test

PG&E's EPIC 3.41 final report, v1.0 dated 27 May 2025, covers April 2022–May 2025. At Silverado, Vendor A met flight criteria while failing image acceptance; Vendor B encountered communications, waypoint and imaging problems. The report's printed pages 15–17, Tables 3–4 (one-based PDF pages 19–21), preserve the distinction. Vendors remain anonymous. [PHY-E04: PG&E final report](https://www.pge.com/assets/pge/docs/about/corporate-responsibility-and-sustainability/pge-epic-project-3.41.pdf).

Later results are mixed rather than uniformly negative. Vendor A was disqualified before Demonstration 3. Vendor B met the Templeton flight criteria but failed Silverado criteria; image capture was not evaluated because its known sensor hardware remained below PG&E standards. The distribution-sensor alert integration was deferred. These findings appear on printed pages 17–20 (one-based PDF pages 21–24), including Tables 5–6 and section 4.5.

This is a field demonstration with meaningful adverse findings, not evidence that all docked drones fail. The aircraft, sensors, communications, dock placement and inspection requirements jointly determine service feasibility. Later hardware may change that result, but cannot retroactively improve the tested cohort. A drone fleet model should measure accepted imagery separately from completed routes, then include review and any repeat visits.

The permitted transfer to ground robotics is structural: measure the entire service, including sensor quality and workflow. The numerical failure rate does not transfer. A camera on a grounded platform faces different standoff, motion, reach and access limits. Likewise, historical BVLOS approval does not establish economic utilization. Permission, installed infrastructure, technical performance and customer demand remain separate gates.

#### PHY-D02 — Rwanda: observed service outcomes, unresolved cost-effectiveness

Nisingizwe and colleagues studied 20 facilities using 2015–2019 administrative data and drone deliveries during March 2017–December 2019. The initial edition used the PubMed abstract; this revision checks the publisher full text retrieved through Exa. Road times are estimated because planned collection of hospital road records was prevented by COVID-19 restrictions. There is no control group. The 67% reduction in expirations is an interrupted-time-series estimate per facility at 12 months, not a randomized treatment effect. [PHY-E05: full article, Methods, Results and Discussion](https://www.thelancet.com/journals/langlo/article/PIIS2214-109X(22)00048-1/fulltext).

The time boundaries materially change interpretation. The reported mean of 49.6 minutes includes order preparation and packaging; the 41-minute median used in the facility comparison excludes it. The principal road comparator is a hospital vehicle making a round trip to collect blood. The paper reports median advantages of 79 minutes against CBT driving estimates and 98 minutes against Google Maps estimates for this arrangement. A hypothetical center-origin, one-way road service reduces the corresponding median advantages to 19 and 28.5 minutes. These are different service organizations and statistics, not interchangeable speed multipliers. Table 1, Table 2 and the adjacent Results paragraphs provide the locators.

The authors also state in the final Discussion paragraph that drone delivery was more expensive than road delivery in Rwanda at publication. That adverse cost direction belongs in the dossier even though the paper supplies no matched dollar cost or provider margin. Faster delivery and less waste could still have health or economic value, but the cost-effectiveness calculation remains unresolved. No accepted clinical-SLA fraction or causal mortality benefit is established.

This case is materially stronger evidence for a useful delivered service than a flight demonstration, but it is not a provider-margin study. The mechanism includes geography, dispatch, perishable inventory and timely availability. Comparing only aircraft travel to a courier's journey omits launch preparation, receiving, reserve capacity and inventory design. Counting reduced waste once in the logistics model and again as a separate platform benefit would overstate value.

The humanoid analogy largely fails at the physical mechanism. Aerial transport can bypass road geometry; better ground manipulation cannot inherit that shortcut. The useful research question is whether a different production network changes service quality and total cost. For US hospital operations, indoor handoffs, interfacility deliveries and stock positioning need distinct scopes. This study does not supply an indoor robot productivity coefficient or a current US health-logistics adoption rate.

#### PHY-D03 — Agricultural services: activity and illustrative budgets stay separate

North Dakota's regulator reports licensed unmanned application activity for 2025. This is an exposure denominator, not a profitability estimate, and repeated versus unique treated acreage remains unresolved. Missouri Extension's March 2025 T40 budgets separately illustrate farmer ownership and custom-service configurations. Its modeled costs are preserved as hypothetical nominal USD per applied acre; they are not observed costs for the North Dakota population. [PHY-E06: NDAC](https://aero.nd.gov/studies/aviation-statistics/); [PHY-E07: Missouri Extension](https://extension.missouri.edu/publications/g1274).

Missouri Table 2 changes the system as well as annual acreage: one versus two aircraft, one versus two crew members, and different driving, cleaning and wage assumptions. Its cost difference cannot identify a scale effect holding the production configuration fixed.

The operating-model contrast is valuable. Farm ownership uses equipment within one production business; a custom operator must obtain customers and schedule work across sites. Higher annual utilization can spread ownership costs, yet narrow application windows, travel and weather can prevent the apparent capacity from being sold or used. A two-aircraft business is not automatically twice the useful capacity of one aircraft if crews, loading or time windows bind.

The source's discussion associates its roughly 980-acre threshold with inconsistent comparator wording; this edition therefore does not adopt that threshold as a validated break-even. A defensible matched comparison also needs agronomic efficacy, coverage, timing and drift conditions. The analogy to inspection-service entrants is conditional fixed-cost spreading under usable demand, not a transferable cost curve or proof that robotics-as-a-service is more profitable.

### Actual configurations and the connected physical value chain

Two configuration maps in the JSON pin the commercial SKU and retrieval date: Unitree G1 base and Go2 EDU. They preserve actuation, sensors, energy, compute, communications and known quantities while leaving supplier identities, costs, production yield and qualified capacity unknown. The base G1 and optional EDU configurations are not merged. Go2's low headline entry price is not used as the deployed price of its EDU configuration. [PHY-E08: G1 parameters](https://www.unitree.com/g1/); [PHY-E09: Go2 parameters](https://www.unitree.com/go2/).

These are real product architectures rather than hypothetical BOMs, but they are not teardowns. Joint counts do not disclose actuator part numbers, per-start scrap allowances, life or interchangeability. A shared vendor is insufficient evidence that two systems draw from one fungible actuator pool. The same caution applies to sensor grades, connectors, thermal solutions and spare assemblies.

Compute needs an equally strict boundary. Unitree's optional Orin references do not identify an exact embedded module or installed memory capacity. The lead's common evidence [HI-003–HI-005](research/digital_infrastructure.md) owns Orin/Thor/H200 specifications. Embedded LPDDR and datacenter HBM are separate resource classes. A richer policy could require a memory or power upgrade even where nominal accelerator throughput appears sufficient; the effect on accepted output must be measured on the configured workload.

| Value-chain layer | Inspected connection | Consequence and remaining uncertainty |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Materials/processes | G1 motor/bearing and Go2 structure/cooling architectures identify input classes | Magnet, winding, bearing, cell and conversion facilities remain unmapped; no commodity shortage coefficient is assigned |
| Actuation and integration | Unitree configured joints; Mobis announced Atlas actuator supply | Distinguish architecture from a named future relationship and from qualified delivered units |
| Compute/memory/perception | EDU compute options and sensor packages; HI shared semiconductor evidence | Pin module, memory, power, interfaces and workload before proposing substitution |
| Assembly/test/calibration | Commercial systems exist; ODRI provides assembly/testing documentation | No public qualified throughput, yield or compatible spare rates established here |
| Software/control | SDK, middleware, model and flight-control artifacts below | Artifact rights and practical dependencies differ by layer |
| Deployment/support | BMW, National Grid, Equinor and PG&E workflows | Commissioning, networking, review and maintenance are real complements, quantities mostly unknown |
| Finance/customer output | Customer operating cases plus agricultural budgets | Provider margin, contract cash flows, financing and data ownership largely undisclosed |

The [7 January 2026 Mobis–Boston Dynamics announcement](https://bostondynamics.com/news/hyundai-mobis-forms-strategic-collaboration-framework-with-boston-dynamics/) is a known supplier relationship (PHY-E11), not a volume calibration. [ANYmal X's manufacturer page](https://www.anybotics.com/robotics/anymal-x/) identifies a different qualification proposition, including stated Zone 1 IIB certification (PHY-E12); site applicability and the certificate chain still require inspection. This can change feasible embodiment choice before cost comparison. It is not evidence that an arbitrary payload or robot variant inherits the same approval.

Regional coverage remains partial: China-origin Unitree configurations, a US/Korean Atlas supplier relationship, a Swiss industrial quadruped offer, and customers in the UK, Norway, US and Rwanda. DEEP Robotics' supplied product URL returned HTTP 520. Ghost, regional integrators, actual manufacturing facilities and qualified component suppliers need expansion. This is a dated coverage register, not a market-share ranking.

### Layered openness cards

Six cards are pinned by exact repository commit in the JSON, with model-card revision separate where relevant. Licenses were read, not inferred from repository badges. No hardware build, firmware deployment or checkpoint execution was performed.

| Artifact and pinned version | Rights inspected | Economic interpretation and decisive missing complement |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [Unitree SDK2 `d1abf001`](https://github.com/unitreerobotics/unitree_sdk2/blob/d1abf001220d3e5f59e00a0c4aaa595e00089a4a/LICENSE) | BSD-3-Clause SDK | Integration reuse is possible within scope; hardware manufacturing, installed control and service rights are separate |
| [UniFoLM-WMA `3e198de6`](https://github.com/unitreerobotics/unifolm-world-model-action/blob/3e198de68de55f93f24b3ad623dd499390aaee45/LICENSE) | CC BY-NC-SA 4.0 | Noncommercial restriction prevents treating SDK permission as blanket commercial policy permission |
| [ODRI actuator hardware `66af1522`](https://github.com/open-dynamic-robot-initiative/open_robot_actuator_hardware/tree/66af1522b4fba0ec4a1d7790e66f5e4652208d30) | BSD-3-Clause repository; design/assembly/testing materials | More reproducible research hardware; purchasing, calibration, yield and industrial reliability still consume resources |
| [ROS2 rclcpp `e2386a0e`](https://github.com/ros2/rclcpp/blob/e2386a0ec6d062579d44ca2f27574c554b42adc1/LICENSE) | Apache-2.0 client-library source | Middleware reuse is not a license audit of DDS, device drivers, all ROS packages or a complete robot |
| [Octo code `241fb351`](https://github.com/octo-models/octo/blob/241fb3514b7c40957a86d869fecb7c7fc353f540/LICENSE), [Base 1.5 `ee3c10e8`](https://huggingface.co/rail-berkeley/octo-base-1.5/blob/ee3c10e8edd6ce2e8b1e8744d3c6fba4097bed48/README.md) | MIT code and declared model-card license | Upstream dataset rights and adaptation/validation are separate; permissive weights do not establish target capability |
| [PX4 `22b15b91`](https://github.com/PX4/PX4-Autopilot/blob/22b15b91fba5e79f01248d1c644ef942382af517/LICENSE) | BSD-3-Clause autopilot source | Complementary hardware/operations businesses remain possible; airframe rights, qualification and operating permission are not included |

The commercial hypothesis is that reusable artifacts reduce some duplicated engineering and shift differentiation toward reliable deployment, customer integration and support. The magnitude and incidence are unmeasured. It is equally plausible that missing complements dominate total cost, or that an OEM captures more hardware demand while maintaining control of scarce interfaces. Therefore openness remains a displayed rights matrix, never a hidden adoption or productivity multiplier.

### Matched embodiment comparisons and transfer tests

`PHY-C01` compares one accepted utility condition inspection, with equal coverage, resolution and response interval. A digital-improved human process, fixed sensing, wheeled inspection, quadruped, aerial sensing and a possible humanoid are alternatives only where they meet those constraints. Fixed sensing can be attractive for stable viewpoints; accessible level routes can remove the need for legged mobility. Stairs, terrain, energized access and hazardous-zone requirements can change feasibility. A sensing-only service provides no evidence that humanoid hands justify their additional burden. Adding repair or manipulation changes the service and requires another comparison.

`PHY-C02` compares one usable blood order delivered by its deadline. Road courier plus digital dispatch, aerial transport with ground handling and local inventory/replenishment are competing networks. Ground robots may complement indoor handoffs but are not established substitutes for the rural aerial leg. Road access, urgency, payload eligibility and sufficiently dense demand can reverse the preferred arrangement. Neither comparison has enough whole-system cost evidence for a numeric ranking. The correct present result is a feasibility map and break-even condition, not invented prices.

The five structured transfer tests preserve separate source and target evidence. PG&E supports separating navigation from useful information. Rwanda rejects copying an aerial geography advantage into humanoid labor productivity. PX4/ODRI versus Unitree supports testing artifact-specific engineering reuse while rejecting blanket openness. The [FAA's July 2024 UTM authorization](https://www.faa.gov/newsroom/faa-makes-drone-history-dallas-area) motivates shared intentions and operating authority for mixed fleets, but neither airspace rules nor throughput coefficients transfer. Agricultural budgets provide a fixed-cost and usable-demand mechanism, without empirical cost parameters for inspection robots. Each card states a target prediction and falsifier; none calibrates the common model.

### Supply-to-deployment model implications

The common engine owns the time-indexed scenario; this contribution supplies its evidence boundaries. Use distinct compatible component pools, reserve service spares for installed cohorts, apply assembly acceptance loss once, retain lead times and finished inventory, and commission only within site/support capacity. Replacements are not net additions. Qualified receipts cannot be generated from announced annual capacity, and net receipts cannot have external commitments deducted twice.

An explicit supported-fleet target needs a backcast of surviving stock, retirements, replacement and expansion units, required accepted completions, assembly starts, component receipts, service inventory and commissioning/support labor. That is necessary-condition logic. It does not prove orders, customer finance or accepted-service demand. The model agent uses virtual configurations for synthetic coefficients; the named G1/Go2 maps must not be treated as cost-calibrated versions of those virtual systems.

Three decision changes follow. First, product strategy should fund the measurement and workflow bottleneck before assuming extra robot capability creates value. Second, an inspection-service newco needs site access, contracts, review and dense support, not only robot capital. Third, expansion planning should test a support-bound case alongside component qualification and manufacturing delays. The available evidence permits these causal structures but does not establish which bottleneck dominates across the economy.

### Coverage and next bounded questions

This edition supplies three quadruped dossiers across manufacturing/infrastructure, three drone dossiers across utility inspection/medical delivery/agricultural service, two real product maps, six openness cards, two matched qualitative comparisons and five physical transfer tests. Routine use is customer-reported and lacks independent route telemetry. Costs, contractual allocation, manufacturing yields and qualified capacity remain mostly unknown. The report and common dashboard must retain these limitations.

The highest-priority next work is a matched recurring inspection cohort: accepted outputs, interventions, review hours, repeat visits, downtime and full configured costs. Next retrieve the disposition of the Blyth trial and configuration-specific service manuals/BOM compatibility. Then inspect data/repair rights and add non-Spot operator cases with comparable exposure. Actual agricultural-service and medical-delivery economics should be investigated before using either as proof of an attractive operating business. The JSON records precise stopping conditions; additional publicity without those denominators would not resolve the decision.

### Focused follow-up: Non-Spot operator evidence and retained-deployment limits

Authoritative analysis: [research/quadruped_operator_followup.md](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md).

#### Project expenditure, replication and realized service, edition 0.43

The February 2019 **Project GRAID Closure Report** provides an actual operator project account, improving the cost evidence while preserving its limits. The [public ENA project record and documents](https://smarter.energynetworks.org/projects/nggtgn02/) host this report; the 2021 upload is not a new operating observation. GRAID is a UK specialist in-pipe inspection comparator, not an additional quadruped deployment. QOF-E19/E20 describe two parts of this one report.

##### A project can be under budget and need owner funding

Table 6 on printed page48 reports these amounts in **GBP thousands**. The populated categories reconcile exactly; blank categories remain unspecified rather than zeros.

| Project category | Budget | Actual expenditure |
| --- | ---: | ---: |
| Labour | 936.06 | 646.58 |
| Equipment | 141.77 | 265.39 |
| Contractors | 5,424.44 | 5,561.32 |
| Travel and expenses | 45.50 | 33.43 |
| Total | 6,547.77 | 6,506.72 |

The total budget includes a GBP 243k voluntary contribution. Its GBP 41.05k underspend coexists with actual spending GBP 201.95k above the implied non-voluntary envelope of GBP 6,304.77k, consistent with the rounded GBP 202k final owner contribution in the narrative. This is a reconciliation of reported accounts, not a newly estimated economic saving. The narrative's separate 3.71% overspend figure has an unspecified base; using that implied envelope gives approximately 3.20%. Preserve the discrepancy. Do not add a funding contribution to the resource expenditure it finances. [QOF-E19, pp.48–49]

The material exclusion is site access: Bacton's connection construction had separate internal funding, while inspection handling, launch installation and cleaning entered the project account. Therefore even a reconciled total is not the whole deployment cost. Internal role changes and work reassignment also affected the labor budget; lower project spending does not establish removed operating payroll. Contractor payments disclose customer expenditure, not the supplier's resource cost or margin. [QOF-E19]

##### Replication is a different cost object

Sections 10.1 and10.3 give an indicative GBP 0.5–1m platform range and GBP 100k launch vessel, with variable site connection cost in addition. These historical equipment indications do not price present qualification, data preparation, site access, operation, cleaning, support, replacement or financing. Their price-year and tax bases are unresolved. Neither the development total nor the two endpoints of a range should be added as though they were separate items in one current quote. [QOF-E20, pp.55–56]

The report's roughly GBP 58m over 20 years is an objective. Section8 says realization requires continued use and condition-model updates; Section11 anticipates progress from pre-commercial demonstration to full commercial application. A trial, a plan, and a realized service cash series are separate evidence. This historical record establishes neither current retained deployment nor failure/abandonment. [QOF-E20, pp.4,50,57]

##### What changes in the utility decision

The customer can value information that changes an excavation, replacement or maintenance decision even if paid inspection staffing stays constant. That requires an accepted observation, the decision it changed, a feasible alternative and all costs on both sides. A supplier must price the operating/support service beyond equipment. A newco must fund the surrounding qualification and site organization; an acquisition must establish usable rights and inherited obligations. Grant or owner funding can make development possible without making a deployment profitable.

[QOF-AUDIT-COST-03](data/quadruped_operator_followup.json) therefore separates the project account, replication equipment and realized service economics in the three packs and existing dashboard. It adds no synthetic case or coefficient. Eight outcome groups remain unknown, including off-project site costs, recurring accepted service, paid-hours effects, current support/rights and realized net benefit. The next decisive artifact is a current operator deployment closeout joined to the payer and accepted-service ledgers.

Access limits remain explicit: direct retrieval of National Gas's performance index returned HTTP403 and was not retried; ENA's generated project-summary PDF timed out and was not retried. The distinct full closure report was obtained through the ENA page's documented public download. A later secondary account was a search lead only and supplies no accepted finding here. Older closed routes remain closed.

#### Specialist inspection and the wider service, edition 0.42

EDF's [26 June operator account](https://www.edf.fr/arret-programme-de-l-unite-ndeg2-controle-des-vis-de-cloisonnement-du-coeur) describes a purpose-built underwater ultrasonic robot, a 960-bolt inspection scope, and retained positioning, guidance, measurement checks and specialist interpretation. It reports more than 30 people across day/night teams over nearly two weeks and no detected defect requiring replacement. These are operator statements, not an independent staffing or cost study. [QOF-E17]

The linked [Bugey unit-2 outage account](https://www.edf.fr/la-centrale-nucleaire-du-bugey/les-actualites-de-la-centrale-nucleaire-du-bugey/des-controles-cles-pour-l-unite-de-production-ndeg2-lors-de-son-arret-programme) supplies the wider program: an outage beginning 2 May and more than 12,000 control/maintenance activities alongside fuel work. Its hydraulic tests are a different activity. The HTML title retains 9 June 2026; the current page displays a 5 November update, which its publication metadata also uses. Both dates are preserved. The link supports the Bugey attribution, which the bolt article itself does not state. These related pages are not independent replications. [QOF-E18]

The decision changes at the counterfactual. If direct access is unavailable, a qualified remote inspection service can be valuable without replacing a feasible direct-human task. Compare qualified specialist processes at the same acceptance, access and time boundary before comparing embodiments. This case neither adds a quadruped deployment nor sets a US utility labor coefficient.

##### From inventory to an accepted service

The proposed unit is one uniquely identified due bolt with a qualified measurement, recorded reviewer disposition and timely handoff. Repeated attempts and rejected measurements are work, not extra accepted units. The reported inventory is insufficient to audit that unit. A finding that does not call for replacement says nothing about the paid resources needed to reach it or maintain readiness for another result.

The linked outage creates a second boundary. Inspection completion matters economically through what it changes in the wider program. Without a task schedule and counterfactual, neither the inspection window nor the outage's start proves robot-caused downtime, an avoided outage, or a shorter critical path. The hydraulic-test result cannot validate ultrasonic measurement acceptance or robotic throughput.

##### Who works, who pays, and what can change?

| Service obligation | Decision-relevant record still required |
| --- | --- |
| Position, guide, acquire and check | Role-specific attendance and qualified operating capacity, including retries |
| Interpret and disposition | Reviewer time, backlog, acceptance authority and handoff obligations |
| Maintain readiness and restore service | Separate inspected-asset response from robot repair, standby, spares and fallback |
| Complete the wider program | Residual duties, constrained people and the actual task/production critical path |
| Change a paid commitment | Feasible baseline, staffing decision and actual payroll, overtime or hiring consequence |
| Fund and own the service | Customer cash, supplier/partner resources, title, support and data rights |

Multiplying a multi-shift headcount by an elapsed calendar window would invent attendance. Even measured task time would still need a roster and workload decision before it becomes paid labor removed. Record released capacity, avoided hiring/overtime and payroll separately. Keep customer fees and the provider resources they fund separate in payer accounts, then count resource use once in consolidation. The available pages price none of those accounts.

For a customer, the viable purchase may therefore be accepted information or access capability. For a provider, operating, review and restoration duties determine whether hardware delivery becomes a supported service. For a newco, acquisition or hybrid entrant, qualified teams and assignable operating/data/support rights remain necessary; ownership alone cannot establish them. Digital review or scheduling is a separate improvement hypothesis with its own acceptance and paid baseline, not a measured saving in these articles.

[QOF-AUDIT-SERVICE-02](data/quadruped_operator_followup.json) exposes eight unknown outcome groups and six decision gates in the existing dashboard. Existing utility and crew/renewal scenarios remain synthetic and numerically unchanged. The priority is a closeout joining the complete accepted-service ledger to actual role/shift work, payer accounts and usable rights; no extra favorable scenario can supply it.

Follow-up edition 0.6 · 9 November 2026; earlier TenneT investigation dated 25 October · Repository input provenance is recorded in ignored `sync_receipt.json`; it is not asserted to be the current remote revision.

This investigation adds one operator-coauthored ANYmal field study and a later operator-described pilot, both attached to `CELL-M03-utility-inspection-US-2025` as foreign service comparators. It does not add a labor market or claim a complete operating-cost denominator. The accompanying [structured record](data/quadruped_operator_followup.json) preserves source-level provenance, quantities, rejected inferences and exact remaining gaps. The earlier worker’s requested Astra/Ultra configuration was unverified; current campaign serving identity also remains unexposed.

The consequential result is that industrial inspection coverage can remain incomplete after autonomous navigation works. TenneT’s 2018 offshore trial assessed 385 instruments in three rooms, with room-specific inspectable shares of 92%, 85% and 54%. Hidden valves, doors and operating restrictions constrained coverage. Its later onshore program explicitly selected points suited to the robot, while the public evaluation material left costs and results unfilled. Neither episode supports a percentage reduction in maintenance employment. The public record also does not establish that either project was abandoned.

#### Episode QOF-D01: offshore ANYmal B300, 2018

The [field study](https://doi.org/10.1007/978-981-15-9460-1_18) by Gehring and colleagues includes TenneT authors Marcel Potz and Lars Gerstenberg alongside ANYbotics researchers. Its ETH bibliographic wrapper dates publication to January 2021; the observed deployment occurred in 2018 on an unnamed TenneT offshore HVDC platform in the North Sea. This is firsthand, operator-coauthored evaluation, with supplier participation—not independent third-party telemetry. The full chapter was retrieved through Exa; a separate direct ETH PDF request returned HTTP 429. Section and figure locators are retained rather than inventing PDF page offsets. [QOF-E01]

The tested system was ANYmal B300 with a prototype inspection payload, not today’s ANYmal X. The chapter describes stronger actuators for steep stairs, onboard visual/thermal/acoustic sensing, lidar-based mapping and a charging station. It does not publish a software build or serial revision. A qualified operator could program missions, observe status and intervene remotely. The supplied GUI produced a post-mission report covering navigation, inspection and unexpected events.

##### Exposure and the service denominator

Section 6 reports more than 30 autonomous missions over two weeks. One illustrated route covered 19 points of interest. The study reports an average tour time of approximately 25 minutes, including six minutes of inspection on average; the timing sample and distribution are not specified. Those figures describe route timing and a field-test window. They do not establish 30 successful missions out of 30 attempts, continuous two-week unattended uptime, or a productive rate applicable to every instrument. The contemporaneous [manufacturer announcement](https://www.anybotics.com/news/worlds-first-autonomous-offshore-robot/), dated 25 October 2018, instead describes a one-week September pilot and a video mission with 16 points. The publication does not explain whether these are different subsets or phases. They remain distinct observations; this edition does not average the windows or add the routes. [QOF-E01 §6/Fig.11; QOF-E02]

The study’s more useful denominator is its coverage assessment: 385 instruments across a pump room, water-treatment room and HVA/C room. It reports that 92%, 85% and 54% of the respective points could be inspected. Individual room counts are absent, so even a pooled percentage cannot be calculated defensibly. These are coverage results, not repeated-measurement precision, defect-detection sensitivity, mission reliability or completed maintenance actions. They also concern an evaluated subset, not a complete inventory of every mandatory site inspection. Several HVA/C valves faced walls or were otherwise hidden from view. [QOF-E01 §6]

Section 3.1 adds two upstream constraints. About half the site was inaccessible to both people and the robot while energized because of discharge risk; access occurred during the annual shutdown. Of approximately 200 rooms, the authors identify 50 as interesting for robot inspection during normal operation. Neither ratio is a measured labor-substitution share. The robot did not erase the site’s operating restrictions.

##### Human work and unfinished integration

Field engineers needed safety training. The test team installed Wi-Fi and charging infrastructure because the working areas lacked a wireless network. Commissioning involved manually guiding a mapping pass, a second pass to register inspection points using an AR marker, defining pressure/temperature criteria, editing paths, and programming reactions to communications loss and unexpected obstacles. Simulation and real-site tests followed. Waiting for operator instructions at an unknown obstacle is a specified fallback, not evidence that its labor cost was zero. [QOF-E01 §§3.1, 4.3, 5]

The study identifies closed doors as a major remaining barrier. Fire rules prevented automating every door in both directions; the authors proposed manipulation as a possible solution. An arm might also improve sensor visibility and operate switches, but its reliability and economics were not tested in this case. The conclusion describes daily inspection as a future possibility relative to monthly human inspections and says platform control-system integration and long-distance communications still needed work. The demonstrated local Wi-Fi GUI must therefore not be read as an already operational, integrated onshore maintenance service. [QOF-E01 §7]

The inspected sources give no intervention count, reviewer minutes, verified defect dispositions, avoided trips, maintenance work orders, downtime avoided, all-in purchase/support price, battery replacement cost or routine-use continuation. No acceptance tolerance is provided for the room-coverage percentages. Useful data acquisition is demonstrated; a priced, fully accepted inspection-and-action chain is not.

#### Episode QOF-D02: Sparky at Wateringen, 2024

The strongest operator artifact is [Thijs Pruijssers’ TenneT presentation](https://www.nen.nl/media/wysiwyg/Presentatie_TenneT_Digitale_transformatie_onder_hoogspanning.pdf), hosted publicly by NEN and dated 18 September 2024 on its title slide. Slide 11 describes a nine-month January–September 2024 pilot at a 380/150 kV substation. That is a stated project window, not nine months of measured robot exposure. Slide 13 visibly depicts an ANYbotics-branded quadruped with visual, thermal and ultrasonic sensors, lidar, a spotlight and optional measurement payloads; it does not pin the hardware/software revision. A [same-day operator post](https://www.linkedin.com/posts/thijspruijssers_innovation-digitalization-ai-activity-7242222109484023808-_RHU) confirms a visit discussing Sparky and inspection learning with other European transmission operators. Neither source establishes a retained production fleet. [QOF-E03, QOF-E04]

A provenance detail matters: slide 10 reproduces a planning card dated **24 October 2022** inside the 2024 presentation. It calls for selecting 36 points that are frequently inspected and can be inspected well by a robot, securing communications, ensuring physical access, assigning robot maintenance, testing output quality and cost savings, and reviewing whether to scale. Its cost, benefit and result fields are blank. Thus 36 is a planning selection, not a completed measurement count or proof that the 2024 pilot covered all required assets. Visual review and PDF text extraction confirm the older footer and empty financial fields. [QOF-E03 slide 10]

This selection rule could yield a high pass rate within the chosen route while leaving expensive residual human visits untouched. A matched service evaluation needs the original scheduled inspection inventory, excluded points and the retained human route. A robot that eliminates an entire mandatory visit has a different economic proposition from one that collects more frequent readings while people still visit for the remaining checks. Both can be valuable, but their labor accounting differs.

##### What the public economic claims actually establish

In a [June 2024 interview](https://solarmagazine.nl/nieuws-zonne-energie/i37700/robothonden-en-drones-efficienter-werken-aan-betrouwbaar-hoogspanningsnet-en-meer-capaciteit), Pruijssers describes 37 inspection missions at Wateringen and says deployment across 350 locations could save 55,000 person-hours per year. The statement is conditional on broad deployment being considered; it is not an observed annual saving, a staffing reduction or a utilization study. It does not net out commissioning, human review, exceptions or robot servicing. [QOF-E05]

An [August 2024 trade-press visit](https://energeia.nl/slimme-technologie-bespaart-op-schaarse-menskracht/) describes 31 programmed missions and reports €90,000 **excluding sensors**. Unlike August’s explicitly programmed repertoire, June’s 37-mission figure does not specify whether it describes a repertoire or executions, or over what counting interval. The figures therefore cannot establish a subtraction, trend, completion rate or accepted-inspection denominator. The price is a reported figure without a procurement contract, tax basis, service term or full configuration; it cannot be used as deployed lifecycle cost. The article describes a two-person human access rule and potential release of technician time. This motivates measuring a route’s staffing boundary, but does not verify net saved labor. [QOF-E06]

A [June NRC site report](https://www.nrc.nl/nieuws/2024/06/13/de-onderhoudsinspecteur-bij-de-hoogspanningscentrale-van-tennet-iseen-robothond-a4856397) observes inspectors reviewing a robot photograph on a tablet and interpreting the absence of a leak. This is concrete evidence of human interpretation within the inspection workflow, albeit one reported scene rather than a time study. It describes the test lasting to the end of 2024, differing from the September presentation’s January–September window. That discrepancy is retained. The reports do not justify a claim that the robot independently diagnosed, authorized and executed corrective maintenance. [QOF-E07]

The interviews also discuss fixed sensors, drones, data-driven maintenance and network capacity. Their combined benefits cannot all be assigned to Sparky. In particular, weather-based dynamic line rating is not a measured quadruped effect. No avoided-outage or network-capacity coefficient is taken from these accounts.

#### What changes relative to National Grid and PG&E

| Question | Earlier checkpoint | What this follow-up adds | Remaining limit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Does a ground robot cover the required measurement set? | National Grid’s three-day trial gives no accepted-output denominator | Offshore study reports a 385-instrument assessment and material room-specific gaps | No all-site mandatory inventory, per-room counts or repeated accuracy study |
| Does mobility imply useful inspection? | PG&E flight success did not establish image acceptance | ANYmal can reach rooms yet fail to see hidden instruments; doors can block routes | No transferable numerical failure rate across aircraft and ground robots |
| Is pilot success representative? | Trial status remains distinct from routine use | TenneT planning explicitly selects inspectable points | Actual excluded work and residual human route are unpublished |
| Does the service remove labor? | Remote operation and review remain complements | Tablet review is observed in press; operator describes a two-person access baseline and conditional network opportunity | No net time study, staffing change or full support cost |
| Did the customer retain or abandon the system? | National Grid continuation remains unknown | A later, separate TenneT program is documented | It does not prove continuation of the 2018 platform cohort; post-2024 rollout/exit remains unknown |

References to the checkpoint reuse `PHY-E02` and `PHY-E04`; they are not new independent evidence. Both TenneT episodes remain foreign utility-inspection comparators, not observations of US utility labor or adoption.

#### Bounded search result and next evidence request

The search covered operator/coauthored technical results, later procurement/pilot outcomes, and adverse cost/selection evidence. Grace’s Düren case was also examined. Its [supplier-published account](https://www.anybotics.com/news/anymal-contributes-to-chemical-facility/) quotes a customer’s extrapolated 1.5% annual uptime increase after describing six months of regular operation. It does not disclose the failure counterfactual, complete costs or accepted-output denominator. A later distributor republication is the same evidence lineage. It was therefore not promoted to an independent operator-economic validation or added as a third dossier. BASF and PETRONAS searches mainly returned supplier descriptions of trials and partnerships, so they were not used to pad coverage. [QOF-E08; search log in JSON]

This bounded investigation found two decision-relevant episodes but no public, complete cost per accepted inspection and no verified 2025–26 retention decision for either TenneT cohort. Search absence is not evidence of abandonment. The next valuable artifact is a dated pilot closeout with: the full due-inspection inventory; selected and excluded points; attempts, accepted measurements and interventions; reviewer/support hours; maintenance actions and their counterfactual; a complete supplier/integrator cost schedule; and the scale, repeat, redesign or stop decision. That would distinguish labor substitution from improved monitoring, and technical possibility from a repeatable operating business.


#### Decision-campaign extension, 7 November 2026

The [residual-visit analysis](research/utility_inspection_decision.md) and QOF-CMP-UTILITY-RESIDUAL in the existing structured package now compare qualified fixed, wheeled, quadruped and mixed sensing against complete due service. QOF-E09..E11 retain their separate source roles and access limits. The executable DE-CF03 challenge uses synthetic assumptions; it does not add a third quadruped economic dossier, resolve the historical retention gaps or price actual avoided outages.


#### Rollout, retained work and robot repair, edition 0.32

The new **QOF-AUDIT-READINESS-01** adds a European steel-inspection comparator to the same evidence package. It strengthens the customer-work and support challenge without adding labor totals, a calibrated economic dossier or a synthetic world. Earlier TenneT cohort and field-coverage gaps remain unresolved.

Outokumpu’s [June 2023 announcement](https://www.outokumpu.com/en/news/2023/outokumpu-is-the-first-in-the-steel-industry-to-use-robotics-in-safety-management-%E2%80%93-supporting-the-vision-of-achieving-the-lowest-accident-frequency-rate-in-the-industry-by-2025-3286722) frames exposure and hazardous plant-repair reductions as expectations, while describing partial task automation without replacing the workforce. [QOF-E12]

The [June 2024 operator update](https://www.outokumpu.com/en/news/2024/outokumpu-to-expand-the-use-of-robotics-in-safety-management-%E2%80%93-first-anymal-robot-%E2%80%9Cjokkeri%E2%80%9D-started-operating-at-the-company%E2%80%99s-site-in-tornio%2C-finland-3460793) reports three operating robots and Avesta inspection frequencies. It attributes the safety percentages to the earlier estimate. This supports dated rollout, not a measured realization of those benefits. [QOF-E13]

An [October 2023 interview republished by ANYbotics in November](https://www.anybotics.com/news/this-is-how-outokumpu-wants-to-make-work-safer/) describes substantial internal learning, IT/supplier coordination and manual anomaly escalation before integration. Purchase language in its introduction and RaaS language in a caption remain unreconciled; neither determines executed title. [QOF-E14]

These sources alter the measurement contract in four ways.

**Separate the two maintenance systems.** The robot inspects a plant whose equipment may need repair; the robot itself also needs support, parts and qualification. A claim about avoided plant repairs cannot set the robot’s replacement probability, technician hours or spare budget. An avoided hazardous intervention may matter without reducing a paid shift. Both systems need their own event and work records before their costs or benefits can be combined.

**Keep the customer’s work visible.** Supplier fees and robot support do not automatically cover site knowledge, mission design, IT access, data integration, review, escalation and action authority. Those tasks may use existing staff without extra headcount, yet still consume capacity. Reassigning a worker is not a zero-resource event. The appropriate counterfactual includes a competent human inspection route with digital records and qualified fixed sensing, plus the same decision and maintenance obligations.

**Count accepted due service.** Repeated point frequencies can represent useful monitoring without expanding unique coverage. Define the original due set, measurement quality, deadline and accepted disposition. Identify excluded points, failed captures, repeats and residual physical duties. More data is not itself an avoided visit, a correct diagnosis or a completed repair. An increased-monitoring case needs a separate action/outcome counterfactual rather than a payroll-saving proxy.

**Resolve the applicable service boundary.** The [public support portal](https://support.anybotics.com/) distinguishes operator/researcher entry; the followed manual and knowledge-base routes required authentication. No detailed maintenance procedure was read. [QOF-E15] The [onboarding page](https://www.anybotics.com/get-started/) advertises serviced RaaS, commissioning and maintenance/repair. It supplies no executed target restoration or inclusive-work contract. [QOF-E16] The training website’s terms concern its learning service and were not promoted to robot warranty terms. Current product marketing cannot identify a historical deployment’s exact configuration.

##### Consequences for the three packs and the model

For **utility inspection**, require the exact sensing configuration, complete due-point ledger, customer review/integration work and funded robot repair/requalification. Keep fixed sensing, human routes and residual field visits. For **hospital logistics**, transfer only the question: clinical handoff, urgency coverage, process authority and fallback remain target obligations. For **automotive presentation**, retain line-side integration, generation validation and intervention work; inspected points are not accepted production kits. No steel-site rate, price, title or maintenance entitlement is imported into those packs.

The [component execution](research/component_execution.md) assumes replacement labor and parts inside an existing paid support bundle, with supplier consignment before issue. These public records do not price or validate either assumption. They add a distinct customer-work requirement to any empirical test. Keep the synthetic mechanism and all earlier numerical outcomes unchanged until matched evidence supports a separate, reviewable parameter change.

Proceed with a bounded evaluation when accepted service, exact configuration, work ownership, support rights and funded fallback reconcile. Wait on labor-substitution or rollout economics while those records remain missing. Reject a proposed inference that turns plant-repair projections into robot spare rates, repeated capture into complete service, a service label into unlimited coverage, or retained staff into zero cost.

##### Retrieval and remaining evidence

Five public HTML records were read and fingerprinted on 8 November 2026. Manufacturer-republished operator statements remain one attributed lineage, not independent validation. Detailed maintenance access was not bypassed, and no registration, contact or purchase occurred. A TenneT-domain robots restriction was respected. Later retention was not established by this bounded search; absence is not abandonment.

Six outcomes remain explicitly unknown: accepted due-point service, all-actor work, robot replacement/requalification, configured cost/rights, realized exposure effects and later operating retention. Seek a dated configured maintenance-and-service ledger or an executed applicable support scope. The next independent research priority is the substantial-H critical path from digital improvement to qualified operating benefit, retaining failed work and conventional alternatives; do not repeat these access-limited searches merely to add sources.

#### Virtual inspection and retained field work, edition 0.46

The [SP Distribution closeout](https://smarter.energynetworks.org/projects/nia_spen_0063/) changes the utility decision from an imagery-throughput question to a whole-service comparison. Its 2021–22 drone/virtual-inspection trial found additional defect visibility but projected approximately 44% higher programme Opex for like-for-like replacement. Physical checks and manual review remained. This is a historical trial-based cost projection, not measured current savings, a current legal statement or an all-payer cost account. QOF-E21 and QOF-AUDIT-SERVICE-03 preserve those boundaries.

| Reported stage | LV/HV poles | EHV poles | Interpretation |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Attempted capture scope | 243 | 162 | Different circuits and access conditions |
| Poles with images | 93 | 160 | Acquisition, not complete statutory inspection |
| Poles available for virtual review | 67 | 140 | Identity/image usability is another filter |

The printed capture percentages are 35% and 98%; direct count arithmetic gives about 38.27% and 98.77%. Preserve both. The available cohorts sum to 207 while the separate desk-review account reports 208 over six days; a joined object ledger is needed before declaring unique accepted output. The report's 104 defects judged likely missed from ground level are neither an AI-only accuracy measurement nor 104 avoided failures. Its experienced desk inspector represents a small trial, not a scalable staffing ratio.

A defensible comparison starts with the same set of due poles and all required checks. Count capture attempts, useful images, qualified reviews, unique findings, timely escalations and complete inspections separately. If a field visit remains necessary, a faster desk process does not automatically remove the visit, its travel, the qualified worker or a paid shift. Conversely, a more informative inspection may be worth buying even when it costs more. The decision is whether the additional accepted information changes a maintenance action or risk outcome enough to justify the complete incremental cost, with any increased repair work included.

The report offers a particularly useful warning about timing: later review can discover critical hazards after the field team has left. A procurement specification therefore needs an escalation deadline, responsible role and fallback, not just an image-resolution or per-day target. Finding more defects can create valuable work; it can also create another review queue. Neither queue length nor defect count establishes avoided loss.

For the customer, identify the actual field and desk roster, mandatory residual duties, software charges and maintenance disposition. For the provider, price and fund image identity, review workflow, supported defect classes, storage and ongoing support; a per-object fee is not the provider's resource cost. For a newco or acquisition/hybrid operator, verify that the relevant competence, contracts and access rights exist and can be used by the entity responsible for delivery. Buying imagery, software or a customer business alone does not supply them.

The source supplies a negative whole-process substitution result alongside a positive information result. It does not rank modern drones against quadrupeds or establish that the same restrictions persist today. The six-cell reassessment and hospital/automotive/utility packs use the obligation, identity and response principles only; no UK pole rate, cost premium, staffing ratio or legal requirement is transferred to another task. Existing model cases remain synthetic and unchanged.
