Needed next evidence is a reconciled due-object/acceptance/disposition history, current paid work and absolute costs by payer, rather than another favorable image count.

#### Demonstration, approval and the build-versus-buy decision, edition 0.46

A separate [NGET AATIS closeout](https://smarter.energynetworks.org/projects/nia2_nget0018/) supplies a different stopping condition. A thermal-inspection prototype and draft safety case were demonstrated/prepared in 2023, but later rollout deliverables remained on hold. The operator paused development amid equipment, weather, site-access and staff/review constraints, then terminated it after judging commercial alternatives more capable. Its July 2025 closeout recommends further commercial trials; it does not report an operating replacement, realized savings or actual project expenditure. QOF-E22 and QOF-AUDIT-DEVELOPMENT-04 preserve this separate case.

That sequence matters for a robotics development decision. Technical progress can be real while the best next investment changes. Compare the remaining spend, qualified personnel, rights, approval and service path against the competent alternative available at the decision date. A sunk development payment belongs in the historical account; it does not by itself justify another payment. Equally, an attractive supplier offering does not arrive with the customer's site authorization, competent roster or accepted service history. A change from build to buy still needs a funded qualification and operating plan.

The report's draft safety case was delivered to the operator for review and submission. That is evidence of preparation, not evidence of regulator approval. Reported prototype feasibility is not a complete service acceptance rate. Its proprietary-data and rights limits also prevent treating the described model work as freely available training material. Additional details would require a separate request and possibly terms or payment; no such action was taken.

For substantial H/K, the useful comparison is a dated outside option and the remaining cost of a qualified solution, not a fixed generic intelligence multiplier. I/J require the real equipment, field access and personnel; customer/provider/financier choices require the responsible payer and executable rights. A company acquisition cannot resolve an unmet operating authorization or make proprietary adaptation transferable. The existing architecture fixture's first-successful-admission cost convention remains a disclosed limitation; this source does not calibrate failed-development spending or a learning rate.

These two utility closeouts are not pooled replications. SP Distribution challenges whole-service substitution at its historical trial boundary; AATIS challenges continuing bespoke development when qualification is incomplete and alternatives improve. Neither supplies current target-site all-payer economics. The remaining requirement is an actual accepted-service and decision ledger linked to paid work, support and every payer's costs.

### Focused follow-up: Utility inspection, fixed sensing and residual visits

Authoritative analysis: [research/utility_inspection_decision.md](research/utility_inspection_decision.md).

#### Price the whole service beyond the project, edition 0.43

The [historical GRAID cost audit](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md#project-expenditure-replication-and-realized-service-edition-043) supplies a reported development/trial account and separate equipment replication indications. Site-connection construction can sit outside the project ledger. Funding contributions, invoices and underlying resources must reconcile once; an under-budget project is not a deployment-return result.

The utility proceed gate now asks which intervention accepted information actually changes and which payer funds the complete service. Keep access, qualification, retained work, support and current rights in the comparison. Historical equipment indications cannot price a current mobile inspection alternative, and forecast avoided work cannot be recognized as realized cash. Existing synthetic utility outputs remain unchanged.

#### Operator evidence changes the purchase question, edition 0.42

The [specialist-inspection audit](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md#specialist-inspection-and-the-wider-service-edition-042) adds an operator-supported counterexample to a simple substitution story. The economically relevant alternative may be another qualified remote method, while attended acquisition, acceptance, response and wider duties remain. A capability purchase can be justified without a labor-removal claim; neither is yet priced here.

The utility pack now requires a feasible matched baseline, unique accepted due-point dispositions, actual customer/partner attendance, a changed paid commitment and the whole-program effect. Separate inspection from any subsequent maintenance, and maintenance on the inspected asset from support of the robot. Do not use elapsed windows to impute staff hours or a no-replacement result to erase standby resources. All eight synthetic utility worlds and every numerical assumption are preserved; this evidence supplies no coefficient.

Edition 0.19 continues the same Deployment and Entry Decision campaign. The question is narrower than whether a robot can move around a substation: **which required service is accepted, what physical work remains, and which paid commitment actually changes?** The existing utility decision pack remains provisional because a matched all-actor lifecycle ledger is still unavailable. The new [replay](model/results/utility_inspection.json) supplies eighteen synthetic service-boundary challenges, not empirical unit economics.

#### What the evidence changes

The [existing operator follow-up](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md) remains the anchor for incomplete coverage, selected-route bias and retained human interpretation. Its [structured package](data/quadruped_operator_followup.json) now adds QOF-E09..E11 and the comparison QOF-CMP-UTILITY-RESIDUAL. They widen the alternative set without adding another labor total or claiming a third quadruped economic dossier.

QOF-E09 is the public publisher summary for [CIGRE B3_12057_2026](https://www.e-cigre.org/publications/detail/b3-12057-2026-experience-from-pilot-implementation-of-substation-inspection-robots-in-thailands-high-voltage-substations.html); the full paid paper was not accessed. QOF-E10 is [FLIR's fixed-monitoring application description](https://www.flir.com/en-ca/discover/instruments/utilities/thermal-imaging-cameras-for-substation-monitoring/), a supplier source rather than a matched customer cost study. QOF-E11 is [EPRI's public research overview](https://transmission.epri.com/p37_substations/public/robotics/substation_robot_application/), whose objectives must remain distinct from completed performance. Exact factual claims, dates, access limits and ambiguous denominators are retained once in the evidence records. Detailed EPRI specifications/test plans returned login restrictions; no access was bypassed and no purchase or contact was made.

These sources support a qualification-first decision. A mobile inspection proposal must establish useful measurement coverage at the exact site and configuration. A fixed-sensing proposal must demonstrate the same required information at the same deadlines, including visibility, sensor modality, communications and reviewer disposition. Neither movement nor a permanent sensor mount alone establishes that equivalence. A thermal measurement cannot silently stand for an unrelated visual, acoustic, contact or hands-on task. The synthetic channels therefore receive explicit point-level qualification assumptions rather than a generic embodiment efficiency multiplier.

The analysis separates three propositions that need different evidence: a sensor supplies a useful reading; that reading completes the required inspection set; and the completed set permits an actual paid visit or shift to be removed. A reliable new stream of measurements may be valuable without the latter two. Its value would then need a defensible information/maintenance counterfactual, not an invented labor saving or avoided outage.

#### Matched service and the competent alternatives

The service key is **site, due window, asset/point, measurement specification and accepted disposition**. Keep a complete original inventory, including inaccessible and unattractive points. Record attempts separately from unique accepted outputs. More samples of an already accepted point do not fill a missing point or create additional completed inspection sets. An independently due field duty stays separate even when its site visit can be shared.

| Alternative | Evidence needed before substitution | Costs and work that can remain |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Organized human route with digital records | Complete due inventory, qualified crew, routing/deadline and review protocol | Travel, access/setup, field measurements, interpretation and retained roster |
| Fixed sensing | Qualified information at every assigned point, communications and ongoing verification | Installation/maintenance, sensor or network failures, review and uninstrumented work |
| Wheeled mobile inspection | Same information gate plus traversable paths and repeatable viewpoints | Site adaptation, charging, support, recovery and excluded points |
| Quadruped inspection | A site-specific reason legs improve accepted service over wheels/fixed sensing | All sensing/review obligations plus mobility support; no automatic manipulation value |
| Mixed sensing and people | Union of complementary accepted coverage, shared deadlines and exact responsibility | Overlap still costs resources; a residual field duty can preserve the visit |

A humanoid or manipulator should enter this comparison only when a measured residual action or access limitation is consequential and the additional system qualifies for it. An arm's possible ability to open a door is a testable service hypothesis, not permission or proof that the entire operating boundary is satisfied. This keeps morphology downstream of service requirements.

#### What the executable challenge establishes

[Methods](model/UTILITY_INSPECTION.md) specify four independent windows, twelve required readings per window, exact review and fallback timing, paid field capacity, provider resources and Treasury settlement. All amounts and durations below are illustrative inputs. Eighteen full inputs/outcomes can be replayed; they are one bounded DE-CF03 family, not eighteen empirical observations or completed campaign requirements.

The organized human baseline completes all four windows with 592 field person-minutes. Mobile coverage of eleven points per window releases 176 field person-minutes, but every human visit remains. Once review and provider support are counted, modeled used person-time falls by only 48 minutes. Committed crew cash remains $1,000 over the four windows, and operator outlay rises from $1,080 to $1,640. Initial integration/manufacturing labor is not enumerated, so this is not a complete real labor ledger. It is a concrete counterexample to converting selected-task time directly into payroll savings.

With all twelve readings remotely accepted, no field duty and assumed replacement authority, human visits fall to zero. Under the committed-staff contract, crew pay remains $1,000. Under an actually avoidable per-visit contract, the same monitoring operation has $560 of operator outlay and no field-crew bill. Its human comparator costs four times the crew visit bill plus $80 travel. The synthetic equality is **$120 of crew cost per avoided visit**. This equality excludes lifecycle assets, financing and failure value; it is a conditional contract switch, not a real quoted break-even price.

The final point matters discontinuously. One rejected or unqualified reading retains all four visits; under the default per-visit contract, operator outlay becomes $1,840 rather than $560. Review capacity creates the same result: twelve readings need 24 reviewer person-minutes per window, while 23 leaves one point without an accepted disposition. Reducing review cost without preserving the quality/deadline gate cannot close that point.

The fixed-sensing and mixed worlds test a genuine alternative rather than assuming mobility is necessary. If fixed sensing is assumed qualified for all twelve points, its declared window fees produce $340 of outlay with no visits under the avoidable contract. This is a conditional mechanism, not evidence that an actual fixed installation is cheaper. In the mixed complementary case, 56 raw captures produce 48 unique accepted point-windows and no visits. In the overlap case, 76 captures still leave the final point missing in every window. Increasing capture count is therefore neither a service-completeness measure nor a justification for charging the same benefit twice.

Doubling mobile captures leaves accepted service and duplicate-suppressed review unchanged, but increases provider resource cash by $48. A provider fee does not add physical resources by itself: another world pays $440 in customer fees while the provider cannot fund its resource batch, so no remote captures occur and people still complete the route. That held cash is not earned profit; actual refund and service-credit terms remain unknown.

A required field duty or presence policy keeps visits even with complete readings. The field-duty comparison includes a matching human baseline with the same additional work. A late-fallback case accepts eleven readings per window but misses all four complete-service deadlines. The human-only route can meet that shorter deadline by starting at window opening. This exposes the declared wait-then-dispatch policy; it does not prove mobile sensing inevitably prevents timely fallback. Earlier human reservation would be another intervention with an explicit cost.

#### Recommendation and reversal tests

**Proceed with a bounded qualification comparison** when the full due-point inventory, acceptance and timing requirements are explicit; each candidate has an exact coverage/quality map; review and fallback resources are funded; and the responsible operator has established which obligations can change. This research recommendation does not start a field trial or assert operating authority.

**Wait on the labor-substitution or rollout business case** until actual residual visits, roster/contract changes, support exposure, configured lifecycle bills and rights are observed together. The present evidence narrows the question but does not answer those empirical economics. A separate increased-monitoring thesis may remain worth examining if it has an explicit maintenance-response counterfactual.

**Reject a particular proposed substitution** when it drops a due point or duty, assumes review is free, misses fallback deadlines, treats captures as accepted service, or requires cancelling a paid commitment that remains. Rejecting that business case does not imply the sensor has no informational value.

The next decisive artifact is a complete point/window ledger linked to crew schedules and maintenance work orders. It should show pre-existing requirements, eligible and excluded points, capture/review outcomes, failures and repeats, remote-support and field person-time, visit purpose, actual staffing/contract changes, cash timing, exact configuration and supplier responsibility. Compare improved human records, fixed sensing and the least complex qualified mobile configuration on that same population. Until then, the model's switch points organize measurement; they do not justify an investment ranking.


#### Inspection readiness challenge, edition 0.32

The [rollout/readiness audit](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md#rollout-retained-work-and-robot-repair-edition-032) adds a European steel-inspection comparator. Keep customer integration/review, plant-maintenance benefits and robot-maintenance burdens separate. Historical rollout, repeated point activity and serviced-RaaS marketing do not establish complete accepted due service, avoidable paid visits or configured repair coverage. The eighteen synthetic inspection worlds remain unchanged.

#### Retained field service and useful information, edition 0.46

The [SP Distribution closeout audit](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md#virtual-inspection-and-retained-field-work-edition-046) supplies a historical counterexample to whole-process replacement. Its trial found extra defect visibility while retaining physical checks and manual review, with a projected programme cost premium. The choice is a qualified complete or hybrid inspection service with timely disposition, not an image-volume target. Preserve unresolved source denominators, original date and missing paid-work/cost ledgers. No source rate, cost premium or historical regulatory condition calibrates this US utility cell.

#### Reproduced inspection-window worlds

## 8. Frontier robotics models, deployment and value capture

Authoritative chapter: [research/frontier_models.md](research/frontier_models.md).

Current extension: [the regional source audit](research/regional_frontier_models.md) adds RLWRLD, Unitree, AgiBot and RynnBrain dossiers, artifact-specific rights, operator references and a verified source-arithmetic discrepancy. This chapter retains the first K evidence wave; the new packet now has eight dossiers and preserves all earlier unresolved boundaries.

Workstream K, source edition 0.6, as of 25 October 2026. The question is when a model supplier can create and retain value independently of the robot maker and the customer operating the service. The evidence supports several coexisting structures. It does not establish a universal model-lab margin, a general robot-policy winner or a calibrated cost advantage for one architecture.

Four contrasting dossiers examine Physical Intelligence, Skild, Figure and OpenVLA. A broader role map includes FieldAI, RLWRLD, Google DeepMind and NVIDIA, with Zebra recorded as a separate transaction counterparty. The [evidence packet](data/frontier_model_economics.json) preserves fourteen primary-source claims, exact artifact pins where available, release/access/rights cards and a four-architecture comparison design. The [source summary](model/results/frontier_model_summary.json) reproduces Zebra's disclosed transaction arithmetic. A separate [conditional architecture scenario](research/intelligence_architecture_scenarios.md) is now implemented; real target costs and qualification remain unestablished. Synthetic assumptions do not fill those evidence gaps.

### The industry boundary moves with the work a supplier assumes

| Actor or ecosystem | Roles established by the examined sources | What the evidence does not establish |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Physical Intelligence | Model research, public code/checkpoint routes, beta managed adaptation and hosted inference | Exact beta checkpoint, complete compatible-hardware matrix, price/SLA and customer data/fine-tune terms |
| Skild | Model research, deployment/integration activity and an acquired robotics/orchestration business | Audited contribution margins, complete fleet exposure, uniform contracts or validated deployment-to-learning gains |
| Figure | Integrated robot/model/data development and a company-run whole-body evaluation | Outside-OEM model availability, comparable industrial service economics or generality beyond the tested settings |
| OpenVLA | Inspectable research code, model artifacts and adaptation/evaluation interfaces | Turnkey deployment, unseen-body zero-shot capability or a settled blanket commercial-rights conclusion |
| FieldAI | Claimed body-specific dynamics, mission and multi-robot layers with local inference | Complete configuration, field reliability, resource and unit-cost measurements |
| RLWRLD | Industrial dexterity/model and factory-data partnership positioning across Korea, Japan and US offices | Contract/data rights, validated deployment economics or a reliable count from animated website counters |
| Google DeepMind | Distinct VLA, embodied-reasoning and on-device product roles with a tester waitlist | An immutable executed system, universal compatibility, public service terms or complete organizational autonomy |
| NVIDIA | Compute, simulation, training and deployment ecosystem inputs; model/platform complements | Independent validation of a customer's economics or fungibility between datacenter and onboard resources |
| Zebra | Robotics-business seller and recipient of equity consideration at closing | Current ownership percentage, model capability or a second deployment count |

The map is a dated, incomplete view of roles and relationships, not a census or a market-share ranking. China and continental-European model/OEM ecosystems need direct follow-up, as do Generalist and Dyna's distinct learning/deployment claims. Regional partner examples are not a measured installed footprint. An actor can occupy several rows of a value chain without creating several robots, customer payments or units of useful output. [K-E001–014]

### Physical Intelligence: buying model infrastructure while retaining the deployment problem

[Pi's October 13 beta announcement](https://www.pi.website/blog/api-beta) describes uploaded data, managed fine-tuning and hosted inference. Selected partners use the service to iterate from demonstrations and failures. The post also reports connectivity difficulties in some applications. It does not name an immutable latest hosted checkpoint or supply a complete price, hardware, service-level and data-rights contract. Public pi0/pi0.5 research lineage must therefore not be substituted for the unspecified beta model. [K-E001]

This structure could reduce the ML infrastructure a robot builder needs to own. The customer still has to specify useful work, collect appropriate examples, integrate its control stack, validate behavior and handle exceptions. The relevant economic comparison is the total cost and time to a repeatable service outcome. An elapsed iteration anecdote cannot establish that cost without failed attempts, demonstration labor, engineering work, inference, monitoring and recovery. A report of no person inside a machine's cab does not establish absence of remote supervision.

The public [openpi repository](https://github.com/Physical-Intelligence/openpi/tree/215abfb217dbac7d5f1273282331b9b1866c0479) is a separate route. Its pinned README offers checkpoint locators and robot examples, with GPU-memory guidance that differs across inference, LoRA and full fine-tuning. Its remote-serving examples move policy compute off the robot; they do not remove network latency, controller interfaces or robot-side qualification. The repository LICENSE is Apache-2.0, while effective rights for every external checkpoint, component dataset and hosted beta agreement were not independently resolved here. No model was downloaded or run. [K-E002]

An independent supplier's advantage depends on reusable adaptation across customers. If each new site still needs extensive custom engineering and support, the supplier may be selling a labor-intensive integration service despite a common model. If model improvements transfer reliably and contractual reuse is permitted, shared research and serving infrastructure could lower the next deployment's cost. Those are alternative hypotheses, not an automatic data-network premium. For a customer-side operating newco, access to such a service may reduce one startup burden while leaving acquisition, site operations, capital and accepted-output risk intact.

The next discriminating evidence is a versioned, repeated service comparison with the same hardware and workload: adaptation hours and failures, all-attempt inference cost, latency tails and fallback, supervision, update/rollback obligations and data/fine-tune rights. A beta access form is neither that evidence nor authorization to submit an application.

### Skild: model development with deployment and acquired distribution

Skild connects general model development to field deployment and integration. Its [S1 source](https://www.skild.ai/blogs/s1) describes a video prompt as task context without a task-time weight update. This is a meaningful alternative to collecting and post-training a specialist for every change. It does not eliminate pretraining, scene setup, qualification or ongoing human work. The publisher's approximate demonstration-equivalence crossing is interpolated, and the same discussion acknowledges that extensive post-training can exceed the one-prompt reference. [K-E003/004]

The headline unseen-task comparison needs its actual measurement boundary. The source describes an average cumulative **per-step** score and uses human interventions to recover rollouts, especially for its language-conditioned baseline. Its 66% and 9% figures are not the probability that a full production task finishes autonomously. Internal tasks, data filtering, compute and prompting conditions matter; no immutable public run manifest or full service-cost ledger was established. A study of data scale under stated controls does not yield a universal learning exponent. [K-E003]

The source's metadata says August 18, 2026, its timeline says August release, issuer promotion was dated August 25, and September accounts use inconsistent relative launch timing. This study preserves those dates and does not manufacture a single exact release date or checkpoint. The named S1 product, a particular experiment and a deployed configuration remain separate records. [K-E003/005/014]

The [September company account](https://skild.ai/blogs/skild-crosses-100m-arr) reports crossing $100 million ARR, $50 million recognized revenue since deployments began ten months earlier and 60+ paying customers. These have different denominators and cannot be added. Its examples also use different maturity descriptions: deploying, working towards deployment and piloting. They do not establish uniform production status, installed units, retention, support burden or margin. A supplier's repetition of the same claims is not an independent audit. [K-E005/014]

The acquisition has stronger transaction-specific evidence. [Zebra's quarterly filing](https://www.sec.gov/Archives/edgar/data/877212/000162828026034109/zbra-20260404.htm) dates completion to March 27, 2026; the joint announcement followed April 15. Disclosed consideration is $20 million: $9 million cash, $9 million equity-investment fair value and $2 million indemnification-escrow fair value. The filing reports $15 million of net assets sold and a $5 million seller gain. The common source-arithmetic helper reconciles those amounts without treating all consideration as cash or seller gain as Skild operating profit. [K-E006]

Acquired AMR and orchestration capabilities can add distribution, installed relationships, service experience and assets. They also add operating responsibilities. A joint model/deployment business must be evaluated by what it actually supplies, who owns hardware and data, who bears failures and support, and which cash flows belong to each function. Buying a robotics business does not itself establish productive model integration or permission to train on every customer's data. The proposed feedback loop requires permitted capture, useful diversity, filtering, model improvement, compatible upgrades and a measured effect on service.

This route need not displace independent licensing or customer-side newco formation. A model-led provider may serve incumbent operators, support new entrants, acquire deployment assets and license to OEMs simultaneously. The economic record should retain those roles and their transfers rather than assigning the company to a single mutually exclusive category.

### Figure: integrated whole-body learning with a bounded transfer test

[Helix 2.5](https://www.figure.ai/news/helix-2-5-zero-shot-30-home-generalization), dated September 17, reports three behaviors in 30 unseen Bay Area homes. The behaviors were specified by training data elsewhere. Each behavior used a fixed checkpoint across the evaluation homes; this is not evidence that one unchanged checkpoint learned arbitrary new tasks on arrival. Zero-shot refers to the held-out homes and objects. The exact hardware revision and executable artifacts were not established from the inspected article. [K-E007]

The publisher reports 56% versus 9% whole-task success for Index-pretrained and scratch policies under its stated controlled comparison. Safety intervention aborts and fails a rollout, and task rubrics include time limits and completion conditions. This differs materially from S1's per-step study with recovery interventions. Both sources can inform adaptation and evaluation questions, but their percentages cannot rank models or become a common robot-uptime input. [K-E003/007]

The separate scaling claim concerns action-prediction loss over four nested data subsets spanning an eightfold range, with model size and downstream training held fixed. It does not demonstrate a general law for accepted service, human support or manufacturing cost. Figure's reported compute commitment is also an input claim, not proof of expenditure, delivered capacity or deployable robots. [K-E008]

The integrated OEM can co-design embodiment, sensing, low-level control, policy and data collection. That may reduce interface friction and make failures easier to diagnose across the system. It can also restrict portability and require customers to depend on one hardware/software update path. These economic consequences require actual compatibility, servicing and contract evidence. Household evaluation does not establish an industrial assembly case, just as a factory demonstration would not settle household service economics.

The existing Figure 02/03 system records remain related OEM context. They are not silently relabeled as the exact Helix 2.5 evaluation configuration. For Workstream I, model progress therefore joins a specified body and control stack only when the source establishes that link. For F/G, co-design and control rights can affect adoption and value capture without proving a universal preference for vertical integration.

### OpenVLA: an inspectable outside option with adaptation and rights obligations

[Pinned OpenVLA code](https://github.com/openvla/openvla/tree/c8f03f48af692657d3060c19588038c7220e9af9) offers a serving interface and robot/dataset-specific action processing. The [pinned model card](https://huggingface.co/openvla/openvla-7b/blob/47a0ec7fc4ec123775a391911046cf33cf9ed83f/README.md) describes normalized end-effector actions and limits zero-shot use to represented embodiment/domain combinations. New setups require adaptation. A published interface is inspectable and useful; it is not a commissioned controller, safety system or enterprise workflow. [K-E009/010]

The rights record contains a real unresolved conflict. The model card labels checkpoints MIT and identifies Llama-2 ancestry; the repository README warns that model artifacts may inherit Llama Community License conditions. The code-layer MIT license is recorded separately. This research does not resolve effective model rights by selecting the more permissive statement. Dataset components, third-party dependencies and deployment contracts remain distinct. [K-E009/010]

Accessible artifacts can strengthen a builder's outside option, reducing dependence on one supplier if performance and rights are adequate. The relevant cost includes data conversion, coordinate and normalization work, fine-tuning, inference hardware or hosting, validation, integration, support and maintenance. A zero assumed license payment would not make those resources free. Equally, a paid model fee does not establish that the supplier consumes resources equal to the fee; part of the payment may redistribute surplus.

OpenVLA is a research/adaptation comparator, not a claim to be the newest or most capable 2026 policy. Its simulation and physical examples remain within their own versions and environments. We inspected code and metadata, not a trained workload or a physical deployment. The contribution to the program is a concrete alternative architecture with explicit work and rights requirements, rather than an automatic commodity-price assumption.

### Model progress, adaptation and productive service are different comparisons

| Source observation | Held-out or changed dimension | Performance boundary | Permitted economic use |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Pi beta examples | Partner applications, hardware and iterative adaptation | Selected issuer examples; full repeated outcome/support denominator unavailable | Identify infrastructure, iteration, network and contract questions |
| S1 internal study | Seen/unseen tasks and video-versus-language conditioning | Cumulative per-step scoring with recovery interventions | Test whether prompting can reduce repeated specialization work; require a full-service follow-up |
| Helix 2.5 home study | Homes and objects, with behaviors trained elsewhere | Publisher whole-task grading; safety intervention fails trial | Examine transfer and OEM co-design at the stated task/environment boundary |
| OpenVLA artifacts | Represented versus new robot/domain combinations | Code/model-card contract and bounded examples, not a local executed result | Define adaptation, configuration, dependency and outside-option requirements |

These observations cannot be compressed into one capability score. A model release is not a supported fleet, a fast adaptation anecdote is not a qualified rollout, and a successful clip does not supply the failure distribution. The [physical-requirements crosswalk](research/physical_requirements_crosswalk.md) adds posture, manipulation and population boundaries, but none of these sources supplies a robot-addressable employment coefficient.

### Four architectures at one matched-service boundary

Use the existing manufacturing material-presentation cell as a supporting comparison: a correct component reaches the designated assembly interface within the required timing and quality window. Keep workload, accepted output, fallback, digitally improved human work and specialized-machine alternatives consistent. This choice does not make assembly or warehousing the program's organizing entry gate.

| Architecture | Required economic boundary | Critical unresolved evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Integrated OEM policy and robot | Model, body, controller, integration and service resources; internal charges consolidated | Actual configured service performance, update/repair obligations and outside-option cost |
| Independent licensed policy | Same or justified matched robot plus adaptation/inference terms; model payments separated from resource consumption | Compatibility, fees, data/fine-tune rights, network/fallback and all support |
| Open policy with adaptation | Code/weight/data rights, engineering, training/serving, qualification and ongoing ownership of failures | Effective rights, target adaptation and maintenance cost; no free-turnkey assumption |
| Model-led deployment provider/operator | Workflow and field responsibility, assets/capital, customer receipts and internal model transfers | Contract scope, service exposure, title/control/data rights, cash timing and retained margin |

No inspected source qualifies all four on the same robot and production service. Actual unit costs and negotiated payments remain unknown. The [four observed records](data/frontier_model_economics.json) therefore retain unknown real costs. The separate synthetic calculator distinguishes **net resource expense** from **payments between parties**, preserves common output and all human work, and keeps compatibility gates explicit. It does not treat a model license fee as both supplier revenue and an additional consolidated resource cost.

A useful conditional test is how much adaptation and support must be avoided to cover incremental model/inference/validation resources, followed separately by the price interval that allocates gains among the customer, model supplier, OEM and integrator. For an AI-native operating newco, repeat the comparison with an incumbent having equal technology access. Earlier adoption, process redesign, capital and customer acquisition are different possible advantages; a model-access label is not one by itself. The dynamic treasury and entrant models remain separate until their shared capacity and cash are actually reconciled.

### Changes to the existing research questions

1. **B/H: evaluate the metric before transferring a capability claim.** S1 and Helix show why task novelty, environment novelty, recovery intervention, grading and horizon must remain separate. Their headline percentages cannot calibrate the annual model's capability or uptime.
2. **D: data possession is insufficient for a learning network.** Hosted APIs and deployment-led labs create possible feedback routes. Reuse rights, marginal diversity, correction/quality work, validation and deployment of compatible updates still determine whether the route produces useful learning.
3. **E/I/J: place control and compute explicitly.** Pi's hosted route, FieldAI's claimed local stack, DeepMind's control/reasoning split and OpenVLA's remote-serving interface represent different interface and failure obligations. None makes datacenter GPUs interchangeable with onboard memory, or one robot body's dynamics automatically portable to another.
4. **F/G: allocate operating responsibilities and transfers before discussing margins.** Skild's acquisition adds a concrete model/deployment integration pathway. Seller consideration, company ARR, customer savings and model-supplier margins are separate quantities. Pure newco, acquisition and hybrid customer pathways remain available without collapsing them into provider categories.
5. **Open ecosystems: compare effective options, not license labels.** OpenVLA's conflicting declarations and openpi's separate code/checkpoint/API layers show why the artifact and dependency chain must be inspected before claiming commercial substitutability or commoditization.

The next question is now concrete: for a fixed service and qualified configuration, when does a licensed policy's reduction in adaptation and support outweigh the extra resources it requires, and what price/rights allocation leaves both supplier and customer better off? The evidence packet constrains that question. The [conditional quantitative architecture model](research/intelligence_architecture_scenarios.md) supplies an inspectable mechanism. Broader regional discovery, complete field economics and integrated monthly commercial finance remain continuing work.


The [7 November Generalist/Dyna continuation](research/generalist_dyna_deployment.md) adds adaptation, data-scale, graded-metric and customer service evidence after the original campaign window. It preserves the earlier regional audit and every synthetic coefficient. Output feasibility and financial ROI remain distinct.

### Focused follow-up: Generalist, Dyna and customer service economics

Authoritative analysis: [research/generalist_dyna_deployment.md](research/generalist_dyna_deployment.md).

Continuation dated **7 November 2026**, after the original campaign window. This extends the accepted K work into B/D/E/F/G/H/I; it neither backdates research nor resets the October 25–November 1 campaign. The source cutoff is November 7. Mutable pages have retrieval dates and content hashes in the [evidence package](data/frontier_model_economics.json); these hashes identify inspected content, not a frozen upstream model or verified execution.

The decision is whether external robotic intelligence can reduce the full work needed to deliver an accepted physical service. Generalist supplies evidence about rapidly acquiring simple skills. Dyna supplies both model studies and a more detailed account of customer deployment. The two are useful for different parts of the decision; they are not competitors in a common published benchmark. Neither source set supplies enough contract, support and counterfactual staffing data to price the four accepted architecture alternatives or calibrate the shared entrant model.

#### 1. Adaptation speed and qualification are separate costs

Generalist's August 19 GEN-1.5 account reports **59% average success** across ten short manipulation tasks with a demonstration in context, and **83%** under a separate regime using ten gradient steps on five minutes of task data. It labels the accompanying ±10 and ±9 figures standard deviations. The inspected prose does not establish the exact trial denominators or the statistical population behind those deviations, so this edition does not convert them to confidence intervals. Physical prompts normally include sensorimotor trajectories, not just ordinary video; human-hands imitation is a narrower demonstrated case. [Generalist GEN-1.5](https://generalistai.com/blog/gen-1.5) (K-E031).

The economic hypothesis is specific: reusable demonstrations and a stronger pretrained model could reduce task-specific collection and updating. The claim does not remove demonstration selection, workflow definition, safety/quality qualification, integration, monitoring or recovery. Few gradient steps say little about total dollars without model size, batch size, hardware, pretraining allocation and the cost of reaching a customer acceptance standard. The 30-second context and 100 Hz action trajectories are not a measured full sensor-to-action deadline or proof of embedded hardware feasibility.

GEN-1 is a different system and evaluation regime. Generalist describes post-training, experience-based improvement and inference harnessing; its selected mastery demonstrations are not the same experiment as GEN-1.5's low-data prompting. The GEN-1 speed comparison restricts the box-folding clock to the folding portion, omitting the surrounding workflow. Its limitations explicitly acknowledge attempted tasks that do not reach the desired performance. Comparing a selected mastery result with the later adaptation average would confuse changes in task, training and measurement with a model trend. [GEN-1 system and limits](https://generalistai.com/blog/gen-1) (K-E032).

The public [homepage](https://generalistai.com/) and [GEN-1.5 waitlist](https://generalistai.com/waitlist) establish an access invitation, not a signed license, priced API, released checkpoint or qualified productive fleet. No form was submitted. Private arrangements may exist; this inspection does not establish their terms. A customer-side operating newco must therefore test access, rights and support instead of assuming the same technology is immediately procurable by every incumbent and entrant (K-E033).

#### 2. What Dyna's scaling evidence measures

Dyna-2's study varies nested human-data budgets of 1,000, 10,000, 100,000 and 1,000,000 hours, keeping source proportions fixed. A separate 100-hour human validation set and ten late checkpoints support its offline comparisons. Held-out robot prediction uses 39 internal/external tasks. The hand-pose extraction and filtering pipeline creates pseudo-action labels; human video is not a costless, already aligned robotics dataset. The study leaves model-size and compute scaling for future work. Its empirical result is evidence about this data axis and recipe, not a universal return to training expenditure. [Dyna-2, section 3](https://www.dyna.co/dyna-2) (K-E034).

The physical evaluation then **post-trains on robot data**. Four pretraining budgets use the same fourteen downstream task datasets, each with at most ten hours of demonstrations. Thirteen tasks have ten trials per budget and the language task twelve. The average reported score rises **20 → 28 → 45 → 53% of task-specific ceilings**. Some tasks score completed actions; others count objects handled. The aggregate is therefore not a 53% probability of finishing a customer job. The highest budget is reported best on nine of fourteen tasks, which also limits a claim of uniform per-task improvement. “Blind” scoring means evaluators uninvolved in model development in this account, not an independent replication (K-E035).

The report distinguishes its scaling-study variants from production-recipe models. A separate held-out-site comparison gives 46% and 87% under customer production criteria after task post-training, but does not expose the site/episode denominator, weighting or raw logs. This cannot become fleet uptime or a cross-industry success rate. Another ablation reports 35%, 67% and 96% for instruction following while awarding half credit to a correct primitive that fails to finish. That last number measures a graded score, not 96% completed tasks (K-E036/037).

These distinctions matter for H's LLM analogy. Promptability and scaling can help explain a mechanism, but physical error cost, elapsed service time, hardware wear and authority to act intervene before economic output. The data-scale experiment supports a reason to investigate human-to-robot transfer; it does not establish a fleet-level learning coefficient, a compute-optimal training plan or the marginal value of another customer's data.

#### 3. A service threshold, with financial terms still missing

Dyna's restaurant account states that a Din Tai Fung robot must deliver **1,500 table-ready napkins in eighteen hours**. It reports 95 folds per hour, 93% acceptable quality and approximately 1,590 acceptable napkins per window. Multiplying the reported rate, quality and duration gives **1,590.3**, consistent with whole-unit rounding. The excess is **90.3 napkins, or 6.02% of the requirement**. These are source-arithmetic results, not an independently observed station audit. [Dyna deployment account](https://www.dyna.co/research/scaling-customer-deployments) (K-E039).

The published earlier rates are approximate; this edition does not replace the source's “about 480” figure with a new measured value. The earlier [Dyna-1 account](https://www.dyna.co/research/dyna-1) also separates a reported 99.4% task-success result in a 24-hour run from 75% meeting its strict fold-quality threshold. Completion, acceptable presentation and reliable site service have different denominators. Multiplying all the headlines together would risk applying overlapping filters (K-E038).

At the later reported rate and window, the arithmetic quality threshold is about **87.72%**. If, and only if, the hourly rate refers to active folding time, meeting the target takes about **16.98 effective hours**, leaving about **1.02 hours** in the stated window for additional lost time. That sensitivity is not observed uptime: if the quoted hourly rate already incorporates stoppages, applying another availability penalty would double-count them. The source does not settle the time basis. The [source helper](model/frontier_models.py) keeps that condition explicit and leaves financial ROI, observed uptime and paid labor removal null.

An output margin can be consequential while remaining financially unpriced. The public account omits the subscription or purchase terms, financing, incremental human effort, servicing, consumables and counterfactual labor schedule. It announces a network rollout and a fleet in the hundreds by the first half of 2027; those forward claims are not a current site/robot inventory. Its statement about reaching an ROI threshold is therefore retained as an issuer claim, not this report's calculated return.

#### 4. The production system includes review and maintenance

Dyna describes recording robot state, controls, video and application events; storing episodes locally before asynchronous verified upload; labeling work by customer SOP; and combining automated coverage with targeted human review. It also describes calibration and hardware checks. A reported napkin-station slowdown was traced to a worn gripper: a model-regression explanation alone would have missed the needed repair. The hardware-degradation figure is explicitly illustrative and does not supply numerical failure-rate evidence. [Deployment account, sections 3–5](https://www.dyna.co/research/scaling-customer-deployments) (K-E040).

This joins E/H's digital coordination to I's physical support constraints. A labeling system can locate a failing step without diagnosing its physical cause. A data pipeline needs storage, review, curation and compatible updates. It may reduce repeated integration work, but its recurring resource use belongs in provider service cost. A training improvement and a maintenance fix are separate mechanisms, even when the same recordings help both.

Customer authority also matters. The operator defines acceptable folds and stack placement; staff still move finished work into the dining workflow. Access to recordings does not establish permission for cross-customer training, model redistribution or retention after contract termination. Model supplier, integrator, asset owner and operator can coexist within one relationship; their internal charges cannot all be added as net economic value.

#### 5. Monster Laundry: corroborated use, unresolved savings

Monster Laundry's own [robotic folding page](https://www.monsterlaundry.com/robotic-folding-laundromat/) names Dyna and Sophy Swiftfold at its Sacramento facility and frames the system as helping attendants focus on customers. This is direct operator evidence of use and intended reassignment, not a paid staffing ledger (K-E042).

Dyna's [customer case](https://www.dyna.co/news/monster-laundry), displayed as October 2025, describes RaaS and reports more than 200,000 items over a relative three-month period, ten laundry customer accounts, 99% quality acceptance, 25% higher commercial order capacity and 15% more store traffic. The account alternates garments and towels, and its opening owner quotation says thousands of hours released while the body says hundreds. Those discrepancies and the missing exact observation dates remain unresolved. Ten accounts are not ten robots or ten Dyna deployments, and neither cumulative items nor capacity growth establishes avoided payroll or cash return (K-E041).

A [KCRA site report](https://www.kcra.com/article/sacramento-laundromat-introduces-ai-robot/66895939), displayed September 17, 2025 in Pacific time, directly quotes the owners discussing attendant reassignment and demonstration collection by a Dyna staff member. It also describes weekday robot operation at that visit. Early teaching, later autonomous operation and business opening hours must retain their own dates and boundaries. The early visit does not prove later runs are teleoperated; later autonomy language does not erase the earlier development work (K-E043).

No inspected source identifies Monster's exact executed policy revision. Its operating event is registered once across these accounts. Restaurant, laundry and the synthetic floor-service case produce different services; their output and customer populations cannot be pooled.

#### 6. Decisions changed and evidence still needed

| Decision | What the new evidence changes | What remains necessary |
| --- | --- | --- |
| B/D: which improvement mechanism to model | Distinguish prompting, task fine-tuning, offline transfer, physical task performance and recurring production learning | Comparable task outcomes, exposure, exact systems and full adaptation costs |
| E/H/I: what constrains a supported fleet | Include digital recording/labeling, selective human review and physical diagnostics/repair alongside policy quality | Review workload, label error, maintenance intervals, response times and supported-unit limits |
| F/G: whether an operating newco can gain | Added capacity and staff reassignment are plausible channels distinct from removing paid shifts | Demand acquisition, retained staffing, cash collections, contract price, title and funded support |
| K: whether model access changes architecture choice | A waitlist, a research recipe and a model-led service relationship are different economic options | Executable access, rights, compatibility, qualified service and negotiated terms |

The next useful desk-research step is a matched operating-cost and workflow comparison for one customer service, including conventional specialist equipment and digitally improved human work. A source disclosing price without loading, quality review and maintenance is insufficient; a throughput claim without the time basis is insufficient. Where those documents remain unavailable, keep an explicit disclosure gap and use conditional sensitivity, rather than creating an empirical number. All earlier A–J coverage and open model-finance work remain in scope. The [sole-lead review](reviews/generalist_dyna_review.md) records the verification boundaries.

#### Matched customer-service continuation

Edition 0.12 connects this source arithmetic to [the restaurant workflow comparison](research/customer_value.md#restaurant-napkin-workflow-comparison), FG-CMP-03 in the existing customer package. It adds separately scoped employer and specialist-equipment evidence and a proposed manual baseline. The comparison preserves unique accepted handoffs, retained work, clock uncertainty and customer/provider resource boundaries. It supplies no missing price, paid staffing reduction, ROI or synthetic-model coefficient.

#### Reproduced customer-output boundary

| Reported restaurant service | Value |
| --- | ---: |
| Reported folds per hour | 95.00 |
| Accepted quality, percent | 93.00 |
| Service window, hours | 18.00 |
| Required accepted napkins | 1,500.00 |
| Rate times quality times hours | 1,590.30 |
| Surplus as percent of requirement | 6.02 |

Generated from [frontier_model_summary.json](model/results/frontier_model_summary.json), using K-E039. These are source arithmetic and issuer-reported inputs, not an independent operating audit or financial ROI. Hourly-rate timing is unspecified; no additional uptime penalty is applied. The conditional time-loss margin requires an active-time interpretation and is not observed availability. Synthetic scenario coefficients remain unchanged.

### Focused follow-up: Regional model releases, rights and operating evidence

Authoritative analysis: [research/regional_frontier_models.md](research/regional_frontier_models.md).

Regional follow-up, 26 October 2026. This pass adds four contrasting dossiers to the existing K work: RLWRLD, Unitree, AgiBot and Alibaba DAMO/Hupan's RynnBrain. Exa returned 40 search results across three research angles, with 36 distinct URLs before artifact-level follow-up. Primary papers, operator announcements, repositories, model/data cards and license files supply the accepted claims. The [evidence packet](data/frontier_model_economics.json) retains dated versions and distinctions; this is a selective map, not a global census or a ranking of deployed models.

The decision finding is that **public research access, an executable policy, a compatible control stack, commercial permission and supported customer service are separate conditions**. A code license does not settle weight/data rights. A laboratory score does not supply accepted-output economics. A customer's use of robots and its model partnership can both be real while the exact deployed checkpoint and commercial arrangement remain undisclosed. These distinctions constrain the four-architecture and shared-enterprise scenarios without converting source announcements or benchmarks into numerical coefficients.

#### 1. Public releases have different commercial boundaries

| Inspected layer | Declared public terms | Consequence for the research comparison |
| --- | --- | --- |
| RLWRLD RLDX-1 code | Apache-2.0 | Code access is distinct from the model-weight grant. |
| RLDX-1 PT / MT-ALLEX weights | RLWRLD Model License v1.0, noncommercial | The public grant includes derivative models; separate enterprise permission and price remain unknown. |
| Unitree UnifoLM-VLA package | BSD-3-Clause declared in package metadata | The declaration concerns this code package; no standalone root license was present in the inspected listing. |
| UnifoLM-VLA-Base weights | CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0 | A permissive code declaration does not replace this weight license. |
| Selected Unitree G1_Dex1_Stack_Block data card | Apache-2.0 | One dataset declaration does not license all data, models or control dependencies. |
| AgiBot GO-1 / AgiBot-World code, weight and data declarations | CC-BY-NC-SA-4.0 | Research distribution and commercial platform agreements are distinct. |
| GE-Sim 2.0 repository | Apache upstream components; other code/data noncommercial; separate policy dependencies | A project-wide blanket commercial grant is not established. Individual checkpoint terms also require inspection. |
| RynnBrain 1.1 code and inspected 2B/9B base cards | Apache-2.0 | The permissive base releases are not the same artifact as an identified evaluated VLA checkpoint or a complete robot service stack. |

The RLWRLD distinction is explicit in the pinned [repository license description](https://github.com/RLWRLD/RLDX-1/blob/cf67c31f7f168ebbca6d06e8c3c23914cb94435c/README.md) and [public model license](https://huggingface.co/RLWRLD/RLDX-1-PT/blob/38fb9498af6650915a79cd44cb3169026072081d/LICENSE.md). The latter limits the model and derivatives to noncommercial use and conditions redistribution on attribution/share-alike terms. It does not disclose a separately negotiated enterprise agreement. An independent operating newco cannot simply treat these public weights as a zero-fee commercial input; an incumbent's private agreement is also not established by the public grant.

Unitree provides a useful counterexample to a single brand-level openness label. The [VLA package declaration](https://github.com/unitreerobotics/unifolm-vla/blob/ff6c39aeb0454cfb95418c66aef40ca777f935c1/pyproject.toml), [VLA-Base weight license](https://huggingface.co/unitreerobotics/UnifoLM-VLA-Base/blob/06fee5014922ba6791cfe48d1e4aefac995dd8a2/LICENSE) and [selected dataset card](https://huggingface.co/datasets/unitreerobotics/G1_Dex1_Stack_Block/blob/0b3905f83f5ef5fe64a44af3a1d829830192e7e5/README.md) differ. Its deployment instructions also point to the separately licensed world-model-action repository already recorded in PHY-E15. That repository's current HEAD still matches the earlier inspected revision. SDK, dataset, weight, controller and hardware layers therefore need separate checks; no change to one layer clears the others.

The [AgiBot-World repository](https://github.com/OpenDriveLab/AgiBot-World/blob/98f136ea6b345db1a08451a2f543d67c1789a809/README.md) and [GO-1 model card](https://huggingface.co/agibot-world/GO-1/blob/0bc37be9caa20b5b3d4a569ec462409072d3e244/README.md) state noncommercial terms. The method introduction in March 2025, repository's September 19 release entry and later model-card snapshots are distinct milestones. The documented Genie Studio and remote-inference workflows identify possible integration routes; their commercial service terms, reliability and realized customer costs remain unobserved.

These are artifact-level observations, not a legal-clearance opinion about a complete system. A publisher may offer different private permissions, support or indemnities. Those agreements must be inspected rather than inferred from public availability or from a partnership logo.

#### 2. Experimental metrics require their original task and scoring boundaries

RLDX-1's ALLEX average combines two binary task/selection measures with graded card-manipulation and pouring progress. The paper specifies task-specific fine-tuning and 24 trials per task. Its pouring task uses plastic balls, and the card test uses a restricted height for safety. The reported 86.8 mean is therefore not a complete-job success probability. Partial progress can still be useful engineering evidence, but stages are not equal units of human time, cost or accepted customer service. [RLDX-1 report, sections 6.3 and G.3](https://arxiv.org/html/2605.03269v2).

A separate issue occurs in Table 9. The PDF's stated full/partial/failure weights and printed outcome counts do not reproduce its score column. Direct HTML and visual inspection of PDF page 53 show the same values. The calculation below preserves the discrepancy; it does not decide which printed field should be corrected or supply a corrected empirical effect. [RLDX-1 v2, page 53](https://arxiv.org/pdf/2605.03269v2#page=53).

The count/rule reconstructions are 9 + 0.5 x 8 = 13 and 14 + 0.5 x 8 = 18, versus printed score sums of 16 and 20. The generated report table and [saved source-check summary](model/results/frontier_model_summary.json) expose these inputs side by side.

The [source-arithmetic implementation](model/frontier_models.py) retains both versions of the score calculation and marks the empirical effect unresolved. It also prevents a mixed progress average from becoming a whole-job probability. Resolving this table needs source clarification or trial-level records; running a different synthetic scenario would not resolve it.

RynnBrain's evaluation is more explicit about two distinct measures: complete-task success and subtask progress. In its three-task, 20-trial-per-task protocol, the reported per-task and generalist final-success means are 86.67 and 91.67, while process-score means are 91.28 and 94.14. The final percentages are consistent with 52 and 55 complete trials out of 60 under the stated protocol; those counts are reconstructed from percentages, not independently inspected logs. The Qwen-initialization comparison uses matched downstream data and optimization settings, while other baselines use different action-chunk lengths. None of these results is a universal cross-embodiment learning coefficient. [RynnBrain 1.1 v2, sections 4.5 and 5.6](https://arxiv.org/html/2607.17977v2).

The two studies use different robots, tasks, fine-tuning and statistics. Putting their headline percentages into a common league table would erase those differences. The packet deliberately supplies no cross-model ranking and imports no benchmark score into the economic scenarios.

#### 3. A model's control interface and serving hardware are part of its productive configuration

The RLDX technical account reports an all-modality median inference reduction from 71.22 to 43.70 milliseconds, approximately 1.63 times, for a specific RTX 5090/Core Ultra desktop setup, input shape and action/denoising configuration. This is evidence about that reported optimization experiment. It does not establish a tail-latency guarantee, embedded-board requirement or complete camera-to-actuator deadline. [RLDX technical account, section 6.1](https://www.rlwrld.ai/en/rldx-1).

The [AWS/RLWRLD infrastructure account](https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/physical-ai/putting-dexterous-robots-to-work-how-rlwrld-builds-physical-ai-with-aws/) separately identifies H200 training instances, L40S simulation instances, scheduled GPU capacity and storage/orchestration services. Training infrastructure, simulation evaluation and robot-side serving are different resource uses. Their presence does not disclose total paid compute, per-customer serving cost or qualified onboard supply.

RynnBrain's real-robot tests use an attached RTX 4090 workstation. Its framework has separate coordination and interpolation/control loops; the latter's 200 Hz setting is not the VLA's measured inference rate. Its G1 configuration also uses a separate SONIC representation and hand outputs alongside embodiment-specific masking. A shared action interface consequently still requires a compatible embodiment/controller implementation. [RynnBrain control and deployment protocol](https://arxiv.org/html/2607.17977v2).

The [RynnBrain repository](https://github.com/alibaba-damo-academy/RynnBrain/blob/5b8641993fd265300dba89f079d9b708ffe19ed4/README.md) and inspected [2B](https://huggingface.co/Alibaba-DAMO-Academy/RynnBrain1.1-2B/blob/36069aa841fc58f500d307316cbfdf379777ce8a/README.md) and [9B](https://huggingface.co/Alibaba-DAMO-Academy/RynnBrain1.1-9B/blob/22faf91e92a87a3d72d0b61fdae78cf864445a45/README.md) cards identify released cognition/grounding/planning bases. The exact executed checkpoint for the separately reported VLA experiments was not identified in the inspected release listings. Permissive base weights can contribute to downstream R&D without constituting an identified, qualified customer-service policy.

This distinction matters to H and I: a richer general model can improve a downstream starting point, while action interfaces, serving location, latency distributions, sensor availability and support remain additional inputs. The research does not infer that a larger model fits a given robot's compute, memory or power envelope.

#### 4. Collection, simulation and productive service have different denominators

AgiBot World's Beta card reports more than one million trajectories from 100 collection robots and 2,976.4 recorded hours. Alpha has its own updated task/hour description. These variants are kept separate because their overlap and raw corpus quality were not audited. A robot collecting demonstrations is a resource used to build a dataset; it is not automatically an installed robot producing paid customer service. The public cards expose noncommercial and access-agreement conditions. [Beta card](https://huggingface.co/datasets/agibot-world/AgiBotWorld-Beta/blob/2252b3fdc88dc67fa5d5e4b771e1b86f5e71c278/README.md), [Alpha card](https://huggingface.co/datasets/agibot-world/AgiBotWorld-Alpha/blob/128665c9e0244c45d1cbe5c13f5a4706afd24f27/README.md).

[GE-Sim 2.0](https://github.com/AgibotTech/GE-Sim-V2/blob/508c60f1a737a54ad2aaa974e63489c7f0c1b1a2/README.md) is an action-conditioned video/world simulator with separately served policies. Its code/data declarations distinguish imported permissive components, other noncommercial material and upstream policy terms. G01/G02 robot support and simulated rollout clips do not establish a new GO-2 policy release or physical operating exposure. This is a potentially useful robotics-R&D complement whose sim-to-physical validation and component permissions remain part of the adoption problem.

The same distinction applies to RLWRLD's hotel relationship. The coauthored AWS account describes workplace skill capture, dedicated space and multi-year data-partnership discussions at LOTTE, with full-scale deployment aimed at 2030 subject to validation. This can be relevant access to human skill and operational context while remaining different from current productive robot service. The account does not provide an operating robot denominator, signed service duration, accepted throughput or customer margin. [AWS/RLWRLD account](https://aws.amazon.com/blogs/physical-ai/putting-dexterous-robots-to-work-how-rlwrld-builds-physical-ai-with-aws/).

#### 5. An operating customer can also be an investor, data partner and requirements authority

CJ's September 3 announcement reports two dual-arm humanoid robots placing cushioning material in customer-order packing at Yangji Olive Young. It names Robotis for hardware cooperation, Aidin Robotics for hand technology and RLWRLD for RFM cooperation. This is an operator-reported operating use, with a named place and task. The exact deployed model/checkpoint, hardware configuration, asset title, human support, quality-adjusted throughput and payment terms are not identified. [CJ operator announcement](https://www.cjlogistics.com/ko/newsroom/news/NR_00001386).

CJ's later announcement describes a September 18 MOU, earlier equity participation and logistics-specific co-development, requirements and planned PoCs. Its repetition of the same two-robot deployment is not another installation. Nor does a named RFM partnership alone identify RLDX-1 as the executed policy in those robots. The records preserve both the operating claim and the development relationship without merging their maturity or denominators. [CJ co-development announcement](https://www.cjlogistics.com/ko/newsroom/news/NR_00001393).

This is directly relevant to the customer-side inquiry. An incumbent can supply operational data, define acceptance requirements, control worksite access and participate in a model supplier's capital formation while still relying on external hardware, hand technology and software. Those roles can coexist. Their investment, purchases and service payments are separate flows; the public announcements disclose neither a complete ownership/control contract nor a consolidated economic result.

#### Implications and remaining evidence

The evidence changes three decision checks. First, a proposed commercial architecture needs artifact-specific rights and an actual service agreement; public availability alone does not settle access, price or reuse. Second, model comparisons need common accepted-work criteria and a qualified control/serving configuration before resource or cash comparisons become meaningful. Third, operator evidence should distinguish customer authority, data capture, model cooperation and productive use, even when they appear in the same announcement.

The shared monthly model can expose the cost of an unfunded installation, support period or customer obligation. It cannot supply the missing license, intervention rate, deployment identity, retention term or supplier cost. The conditional architecture model likewise remains a mechanism comparison with unobserved target costs and compatibility. The new source records constrain what would need to be measured or contracted; they do not turn either model into an observed forecast.

Further work should test consequential counterparties and configurations, including additional regional developers and the still-unreviewed Generalist/Dyna mechanisms, while retaining the broader end-market, digital-AI, supply and quadruped/drone inquiries. Exact deployed-policy identity, commercial rights, complete support exposure and accepted-output economics remain high-value gaps. No outreach, model execution or GPU/hardware run is implied by this source audit.
