### Focused follow-up: Conditional architecture resources and value allocation

Authoritative analysis: [research/intelligence_architecture_scenarios.md](research/intelligence_architecture_scenarios.md).

Edition 0.7 implements the conditional four-architecture comparison proposed in [the K evidence chapter](research/frontier_models.md). The [common-engine method](model/ARCHITECTURES.md) holds a service boundary and configured-system assumptions while making adaptation, validation, serving, support and commercial transfers explicit. Every numerical profile is synthetic. Actual model prices, effective rights and qualified target service remain unknown in the evidence packet.

The first decision is whether a policy/integration arrangement creates a resource advantage over the available process. The second is whether the contract distributes that advantage so the participants can sustain it. The model now separates those questions. A license payment is an expense to its payer and a receipt to its supplier. Changing that payment alone leaves consolidated resources and output unchanged. Combining the model supplier with an OEM or operator eliminates an internal transfer; it does not remove the associated engineering, compute or support work.

#### A positive combined result can coexist with an undercompensated supplier

The reference manufacturing example at the selected 2030 conditions has a positive allocated cash benefit for the independent-model architecture. At the invented $4,000 annual model payment, however, its model supplier has a negative $200 annual-equivalent amount after the declared costs and upfront payment. The computed model-fee interval begins at $4,200 and ends at $11,554.90 per year under the zero-surplus-hurdle assumptions. These are conditional accounting results, not market prices or forecasts of a lab's margins.

Raising only the fee to $7,000 moves $3,000 a year from customer/operator to model supplier. It changes neither the architecture's $35,415.65 annual-equivalent resource cost nor its $11,629.90 combined allocated cash benefit. The source of the benefit must be an avoided resource or changed service outcome; a pricing change cannot create it. Hardware price and service payment obey the same rule within the specified actor boundary.

For an integrated OEM, hardware and integration contributions can share an entity with the model activity. The feasible bundled payment can therefore differ from an independently licensed model's fee, even with some common costs. This does not prove a superior model or reveal an internal business-unit margin. The separate legal-entity and logical-role traces show exactly what has been combined.

#### Adaptation, operating support and outside options affect different mechanisms

Adding 100 licensed-policy adaptation-engineering hours at the assumed $100/hour increases the annual-equivalent cost by $2,000 over the five-year life. It leaves the assumed physical output unchanged and raises the supplier's fee floor by the same annual amount. This makes the adaptation question concrete: reduced fine-tuning effort matters through all the labor, compute and validation it actually avoids, not a generic claim that a model is easier to use.

Increasing intervention demand has a broader effect. It both adds support expenditure and reduces accepted robot capacity under the existing operating equation. Human fallback maintains the common service target. In the exported support-stress witness the independent architecture's combined cash benefit becomes negative; reallocating a fee cannot repair a negative total under the fixed other assumptions. This connects model performance to the full operating process rather than merely pricing a successful inference.

The default open-adaptation profile has more engineering and maintenance work. A separate witness lowers those explicit inputs and reverses its resource-cost ranking against the licensed alternative. The result is conditional on the inputs; an open/proprietary label has no automatic cost or productivity advantage. Rights and target qualification remain separate assumed premises. A false premise returns an unadmitted case, not a zero-cost or equivalent-performance substitute.

The moving human alternative is equally consequential. With stronger human digital efficiency, the combined robot process can become cheaper while its incremental cash benefit shrinks. The exported improved-human witness reproduces that mechanism in the architecture comparison. The model does not import an LLM productivity estimate or a robotics benchmark score to choose the change.

#### Customer formation and provider organization remain distinct

The same technology and costs produce the same service comparison for incumbent and newco labels. An explicit $20,000 additional newco setup charge adds $4,000 a year over the five-year reference life; it does not change robot capability or useful output. An incumbent retooling charge can be specified separately. These are cost differences to investigate, not inherent advantages assigned to an ownership label.

In the model-led operating-provider structure, the provider owns the purchased robot and bears the remaining service work. Customer payment and provider costs are separate, and the customer's unavoidable released-payroll proxy remains visible. Internal policy payments cancel within the combined provider. The computed service-price interval is conditional on this responsibility boundary; it is not a real RaaS quote.

A positive lifetime annual equivalent is still insufficient for deployment. Upfront resource charges and purchases need funding before later benefits arrive. Contract duration, cancellation, credit, working capital, shared factory/service capacity and actual staffing schedules are not supplied by this static reference. The earlier treasury and monthly paid-physical fixtures show why timing matters, but they are not yet integrated with these architecture or entrant comparisons.

#### What this closes and what remains next

The first-edition K architecture target now has an executable matched-service mechanism with human/digital and specialized alternatives, explicit resource-versus-transfer accounting, qualification assumptions, adverse sensitivities, equal-technology customer paths and reproducible fee bounds. It supplies no calibrated vendor ranking, commercial-rights clearance or jointly feasible company plan.

The next priority is the missing monthly commercial extension: sale, independent lease and provider service over the same paid physical plan, with separate title, cash, customer/operator obligations and support consequences. That work should use the existing integer-cent treasury and shared physical ledger. It must preserve the distinction between an attractive allocated lifetime result and an operation that can actually fund its next obligation.

### Focused follow-up: Funded intelligence roles and monthly qualification

Authoritative analysis: [research/monthly_intelligence_execution.md](research/monthly_intelligence_execution.md).

The annual architecture reference shows how resources and fees can be separated over an assumed lifetime. The new monthly execution asks a different, necessary question: which actual entity has the funds and qualified resources to deliver the service when each obligation falls due? It uses the existing calendar/physical/Treasury engines and explicit role mappings, without importing lifetime avoided-cost credits into bank balances. [Methods](model/MONTHLY_ARCHITECTURES.md) specify the contract; all numerical inputs remain synthetic.

#### A fee can change survival without changing resource cost

In the funded independent-license world, raising the annual model fee from $4,000 to $12,000 per supported robot leaves physical service and external resource outlay unchanged. Over 28 supported robot-months, the model supplier receives $18,666.76 more and the operator pays exactly that amount more. The few extra cents relative to an unrounded annual ratio arise from rounding each monthly invoice once. No second inference expense or consolidated value appears.

That transfer can nevertheless determine whether the recipient can pay its own resource bills. With no model-supplier opening cash, the standard upfront and monthly fees do not cover initial adaptation, allocated development and serving resources; no robot is admitted. The whole contingent booking rolls back, including its model fee. Paid human staff still deliver 4,000 zones over the eight months. The result does not say that free or open models cost nothing, nor that the customer saved the entire robot bill.

A one-unit threshold makes the mechanism inspectable. Initial model-supplier resources are $10,500 and first-month inference/maintenance adds $225. Incoming upfront and monthly fees are $3,333.33. Under this atomic contract, $7,391.67 of opening model-supplier cash admits the unit; $7,391.66 does not. This is an exact boundary for one declared policy, not a market fundraising requirement or optimal financing solution.

#### Aggregate capital can conceal an unfunded operator

The four standard architectures start with the same $600,000 total logical-role capital, consolidated into their declared legal entities. A merged entity can use its own combined cash; a separate firm cannot spend another firm's bank balance without an explicit transfer or financing arrangement.

In the model-led operating-provider world, two new robots are bought but their subsequent model qualification cannot be funded. The operator finishes with $418.10 while the customer and OEM retain substantial cash. Moving $100,000 of the initial allocation from customer to operator, with aggregate capital unchanged, qualifies four units instead of two and increases supported exposure from 15 to 28 robot-months. It does not create additional robots or customer demand. This is a counterfactual opening allocation, not an unrecorded mid-run rescue.

Both worlds still deliver the same 4,000 zones and pay the same $32,000 staffing commitment, because the specified human fallback remains available. More qualified robot exposure changes work allocation and cash; it does not automatically improve delivered output or eliminate a shift. The result points to a real diligence question—who funds adaptation and operating obligations?—without establishing a real operating-provider return.

#### Open adaptation can be constrained by engineering rather than license fees

The source catalog assumes 200 adaptation and 100 validation hours per initially qualified open-policy unit. With 400 monthly engineering hours, only one of two opening robots can be admitted in the first month. Under the existing support-first physical policy, that shortfall also blocks the one scheduled month-zero manufacturing start. The second opening unit qualifies later, but the missed start is not automatically rescheduled.

At 600 initial engineering hours, both opening units receive support and the start proceeds. Four units eventually qualify; the separate physical integration pool still commissions only one of the two new arrivals per month. At 200 engineering hours, no whole initial open-policy qualification fits. These are capacity and policy boundaries, not evidence that open adaptation has these measured costs or that a different work decomposition could not proceed incrementally.

The lower short-window cash expenditure of a blocked world is not economic superiority. It can mean that investment never happened, purchased assets remain unqualified, or paid humans perform the service. A conventional planner should be permitted to choose and reschedule feasible starts, avoid redundant additions and use the same resource information. That stronger comparison is the next substantial H task; the present fixed policy cannot establish an agent or coordination premium.

#### Role labels do not create productivity

An additional matched test gives all four role structures identical resource/physical assumptions and ample funds. Accepted service, supported exposure and consolidated resource outlay are identical. Grouping and fees change where cash sits; only specified resource, timing, rights or operating constraints change feasibility. The default profiles differ in those explicit assumptions, so their raw table cannot identify an intrinsic advantage from an OEM, licensed, open or operating-company label.

The service provider pays the actual fixed staff and operating obligations; the customer pays only the declared service invoices after delivery. No hypothetical human-cost credit enters cash. Model inference, maintenance and network bills replace the scalar digital bundle, and internal fees disappear at the correct legal-entity boundary. Asset title, custody, workflow authority and source assumptions are recorded independently.

#### Implications for the three decision packs

Hospital logistics still needs the actual request population, urgent/excluded work, operator burden, staff commitments and support contract. Utility inspection still needs the complete measurement obligation, residual visit and valuation of additional information. Automotive presentation still needs the qualified workcell, takt/rework, new-generation support and rights. None of the cleaning-zone amounts calibrates those services.

For each pack, specify the entity that pays for configuration work, qualification, inference, maintenance, physical support and retained labor; the dates and conditions on which it receives fees; and what service survives a missed obligation. Distinguish spare capital elsewhere in the relationship from spendable capital at that entity. Retain simpler human, fixed-machine, wheeled and mixed alternatives. An architecture can be technically feasible but commercially unfunded, while a fully funded architecture can still add no valuable service.

DE-CF08 now has a bounded funded-architecture implementation; DE-CF10 gains the engineering/support-before-expansion mechanism. Five challenge families have bounded implementations, while the full support, transfer/learning, strongest conventional, acquisition/hybrid and empirical acceptance requirements remain partial. This checkpoint does not complete the 168-hour campaign.

#### Dated resource and part composition, edition 0.33

[The combined extension](research/architecture_resources.md) retains these architecture role accounts and contingent payments while adding the shared annual support/integration admission contract and compatible inventory. The original four unreserved executions preserve their actual outcomes. Thirteen complete worlds include a common integration reservation, less adaptation work, absent model cash, delayed qualified supply and a fixed-staff human alternative. Distinct engineering, demonstrations, support and integration resources remain separate; neither internal transfers nor a lower upstream work requirement guarantees the full-fleet date. Original limitations concerning actual rights, prices and service costs remain.

#### Intelligence roles inside actual customer awards, edition 0.45

The [joined architecture/award execution](research/shared_workforce.md#intelligence-roles-staffing-payers-and-actual-awards-edition-045) now places the four existing arrangements in the actual entry, paid-crew, annual-resource and qualified-part history. Each case has one architectural target and a human-led rival. It preserves the old single-business fixture and every earlier numerical result. A separately employed crew and owned robot do not move with customer-company equity; equal total capital can leave the service operator unable to perform even without robots. Fees redistribute cash and affect liquidity while resource expenditure stays separately reconciled. No measured architecture ranking or automatic customer payroll saving follows.

#### The outside option can improve during qualification, edition 0.46

The [AATIS operator closeout](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md#demonstration-approval-and-the-build-versus-buy-decision-edition-046) supplies a historical example of demonstrated prototype progress followed by a decision to stop as the commercial comparison changed. This does not rank current architectures or calibrate development cost. A compatible learning/architecture extension must represent paid unsuccessful work and remaining qualification separately, while preserving a dated competent alternative and the rights of each actor. A draft case is not operating approval; a public model description is not a reuse license.

### Focused follow-up: Intelligence architectures, qualified parts and physical commitments

Authoritative analysis: [research/architecture_resources.md](research/architecture_resources.md).

Edition 0.33 joins the four funded intelligence arrangements to annual physical commitments and compatible parts in one execution. The decision question is whether a reduction in model/adaptation work actually advances qualified operation after the other necessary conditions and the human alternative are included. All coefficients remain declared synthetic assumptions. No new source establishes real adaptation productivity, staffing, rights, robot repair cost or target profitability.

#### Service and comparison boundary

The complete obligation is 500 accepted cleaning zones each month from July 2026 through February 2027. All thirteen worlds deliver all 4,000 zones, including the digital-human-only case. Actual paid staffing stays at $32,000 for the eight months. The human operation has 588 whole-zone monthly capacity under the existing efficiency premise, so robot activity substitutes for already-paid work without proving additional service, avoided hiring or removed payroll.

The same logical-role capital allocations and physical objective underlie the four profiles. Distinct legal consolidation, assumed engineering, model resources and process integration can change which party runs short and which unit can operate. This is deliberately stronger than placing architecture cost tables alongside an independent supply forecast: every reported date refers to the same physical unit, supported cohort, part stock and actual payer ledger. It still does not estimate the real economics of any named architecture provider.

| Declared arrangement | Unreserved first full four-robot fleet | With 8,400 integration minutes available in 2026 | Supported robot-months, unreserved → committed |
|---|---|---|---|
| Integrated OEM | September 2026 | September 2026 | 28 → 28 |
| Licensed independent | September 2026 | January 2027 | 28 → 24 |
| Open adapted, original work requirement | Not reached | Not reached | 15 → 15 |
| Model-led operator, original role funding | Not reached | Not reached | 15 → 15 |

“Not reached” is within this eight-month window and declared plan. It is not permanent impossibility. Full-fleet support is also not the date at which the customer's service first becomes deliverable: paid humans fulfill that obligation throughout.

#### A digital gain can expose a physical bottleneck

The original open-adapted profile requires 200 adaptation plus 100 validation engineering hours per initially qualified unit. Its 400-hour monthly engineering allocation admits only one of two opening robots in July. The existing installed-service priority blocks that month's new manufacturing attempt. The second opening robot qualifies later, but the missed start does not reschedule itself. More model capability, a source announcement or next month's spare engineers cannot retroactively execute that order.

The controlled productivity premise halves adaptation work to 100 hours per unit while retaining 100 validation hours, 400 hours of monthly engineering capacity, demonstrations and the same accepted-service assumptions. Both opening robots now qualify in July and the new batch can start. With unreserved annual resources, the four-robot fleet is supported in October: the open profile's two 105-hour integrations still exceed the 200-hour monthly allocation, so the second new unit follows the first by a month. Supported exposure rises to 27 robot-months.

With only 8,400 integration minutes available in 2026, the identical lower-adaptation premise supports one new unit in September, but its second new unit waits until January. Exposure is 24 robot-months. Further reducing adaptation to 50 hours lowers itemized resource cash but does not move that January physical date. This is the relevant distinction for substantial H: improved digital work may reduce one resource cost or make an upstream action feasible without shortening the remaining binding path. It requires an explicit unchanged-quality premise; an actual faster tool that worsens validation would need a different contract.

The licensed boundary is exact. Each new unit needs 87.5 hours, or 5,250 minutes, of physical integration. The full two-unit addition can be commissioned in September with 10,500 remaining 2026 minutes; at 10,499, one waits until January. The integrated profile's two 70-hour integrations fit the common 8,400-minute allocation. This ordering follows declared work requirements, not evidence that integration is intrinsically easier for one ownership structure. A measured target requirement, quality failure, different support priority or shared staff arrangement can reverse it.

#### Qualified supply and payer liquidity remain independent gates

Moving board qualification and the manufacturing attempt to August moves the first licensed commissioning to October. Under the same annual integration reservation, the full fleet still waits until January and supported exposure falls to 23 robot-months. If qualification moves but the July order does not, no new units are manufactured: later inventory is usable stock, not an automatically executed order. Compatible receipts, qualification and an explicit action date are all necessary.

Eliminating model-supplier opening cash prevents the licensed profile's initial atomic qualification. Annual minutes, initial engineering, demonstrations and replacement parts remain unconsumed; fixed staffing and site obligations continue. A prospective fee or pooled company valuation is not automatically usable working cash for the payer at that phase.

Conversely, the model-led operator starts and pays for two new units but never successfully commissions them during this window under the original funding allocation. Its $72,000 manufacture payment, 48 manufacturing actuators and two boards remain consumed. Qualification failure does not refund successful upstream work. The two opening units still provide some supported service. These are different failure contracts: an unperformed atomic candidate consumes nothing, whereas an executed earlier action remains sunk. The separate learning-execution branch also retains actually performed failed R&D costs; it is not merged or overwritten here.

#### Implications for the three decision packs

For hospital logistics, keep the complete deadline/custody service and feasible human dispatch beside every proposed model improvement. Identify whose workflow integration, testing, repair and site work is required, which pools are actually shared, and which entity must prepay them. A reduction in model work should advance a demonstrable qualified service date or reduce a relevant paid commitment before it enters the proceed case.

For utility inspection, preserve accepted due measurements, review and residual physical visits. A better onboard or remote model does not supply the operator's integration staff, qualified replacement part or valid service entitlement. The [inspection-readiness evidence](research/quadruped_operator_followup.md#rollout-retained-work-and-robot-repair-edition-032) leaves actual customer work and robot-maintenance coverage open; repeated activity cannot calibrate this cleaning model's resource requirements.

For automotive workcells, retain the same accepted kits, deadline and competent specialist/manual alternative. The [workcell and learning models](research/learning_execution.md) show why eligible data and physical validation matter; this arrangement adds the cash/resource interaction, but does not combine those independently executed cases into one feasible portfolio. No cleaning-zone output is converted into kits, hospital trips or inspection points.

The resulting recommendation is a conditional wait gate on unverified economics and a proceed gate for bounded measurement: document accepted service, configured qualification, retained work, shared versus distinct resources, compatible parts and every payer's dated obligations. Reject a claim that faster model work alone proves earlier complete service, removed payroll, self-funded expansion or a preferred ownership architecture. Unknown lifecycle costs, terminal assets, financing and real rights still prevent a defensible target ROI or investment ranking.

#### Falsifiers and next evidence

The bottleneck result would change if integration were actually completed earlier, the lower-adaptation configuration failed the unchanged acceptance criterion, the same engineers were shared across supposedly distinct pools, the role receiving a fee could legally and operationally finance the needed payer, or human capacity could not meet the complete obligation. Collect dated per-unit admission, actual staff assignments/work, configuration/part releases, invoices and accepted service before claiming any of those changes. An executed target ledger or an explicit simultaneously conserved competing workload would be more valuable than another favorable scenario variant.

The [methods](model/ARCHITECTURE_RESOURCES.md) and [complete worlds](model/results/architecture_resources.json) support exact replay. The [self-review](reviews/architecture_resources_review.md) distinguishes bounded implementation from empirical acceptance. The active campaign and original deadline remain unchanged.

All results in this section are **synthetic illustrations**. Each selected cell has a named service unit; unlike services must not be summed as a single output quantity. Assumed labor avoidability is separate from observed payroll removal. Fractional fleets represent continuous cohort relaxations, not an integer deployment schedule. Annual capital envelopes are assumptions; they do not establish available financing. See [model methods](model/METHODS.md) for exact scope and limitations.

### What the scenario contrasts teach

The delayed-supply case ends with 60 active expected units versus 44 in the reference, but produces only 249,690 cumulative baseline-equivalent hours versus 376,256. Later cohorts avoid some endpoint retirements. A terminal fleet snapshot therefore gives a misleading ranking of productive contribution; the full time path matters.

Ample supply with unready customers produces 35,302 cumulative baseline-equivalent hours. Service stress produces 68,534. These are constructed counterexamples to the proposition that manufacturing capacity alone determines useful scale. They do not estimate the prevalence or magnitude of those bottlenecks in actual markets.

Digital-first produces 297,633 robot-delivered baseline-equivalent hours, compared with 376,256 in the reference. Less robot deployment is not necessarily a welfare loss: the human-led alternative also improves, changing the incremental economic case. This preset changes both human time reduction and robot coordination; it does not isolate either channel. Hold coordination fixed for a one-control comparison of the human-time mechanism. The model does not estimate economy-wide welfare from these scenario outputs.

In the separate monthly entrant case, delayed incumbent response requires peak funding of $516,500; prompt response requires $716,000 and exposes a $116,000 gap under the same assumed commitment. Equal robot access does not guarantee equal commercial survival. This comparison isolates an assumed response mechanism; it is not evidence that those prices, demand shares or financing commitments are attainable. Its robots are not separately allocated from the annual fleet resource pool, so the two paths must not be added as jointly feasible output.


### Annual digital paths and a moving alternative

Model 0.2.0 implements the reviewed three-channel design and retains the corrected 0.1.1 hardware boundary. Constant paths preserve all 69,315 existing numerical fields across eight presets and both configurations. The six generated counterfactuals below use the same tote-handoff cell at 2030, five scenario years and no learned increment. Values are synthetic constant-2026 USD/site/year; reference resource costs and customer contract savings are separate measures.

| constant_reference | 49,560.00 | 27,473.94 | 22,086.06 | 6,316.37 | 12 | 10.00 |

The improving-human case lowers combined resource cost while closing new robot admission. Existing stock persists; the inherited acquisition/payback eligibility cap can suppress modeled output and is not an optimal continuation rule. Cheaper additional robot service can change that commercial result without directly changing capability. The conventional combined case retains the digital human baseline and required robot control, removing only the incremental coordination/service bundle. These alternatives neither calibrate an actual product nor form a jointly funded company model. See the [annual analysis](research/annual_digital_scenarios.md) and [implementation review](reviews/annual_trajectory_review.md).

Changing a fee alone reallocates the same gain. Adaptation changes resources without directly improving physical output; intervention affects both support expense and robot contribution. Leaner open adaptation can reverse a conditional ranking. A better digital-human alternative can reduce robot value even when combined cost falls. Compatibility/rights/quality premises that fail produce no economic ranking. See [architecture methods](model/ARCHITECTURES.md) and [implementation audit](reviews/architecture_economics_review.md).

## 10. Local experiments: cash settlement and transaction state

These experiments are separate, deterministic **synthetic software witnesses**. They do not supply empirical coefficients, alter the eight deployment scenarios, or establish a jointly funded physical fleet. The treasury design's full monthly physical integration remains incomplete. The coordination fixture neither imports nor runs RMF/ROS and controls no robot. Their narrow purpose is to make important failure cases reproducible before attempting a larger model or target installation.

### Treasury: a positive assumed spread can coexist with a cash shortfall

The first financial core records legal actors, cash, obligations, disjoint earmarks, restricted deposits and explicit credit contracts. It settles invoices against actual available balances with recorded event order. A future receivable or an undrawn commitment cannot be spent as current cash. Paying an invoice consumes its own earmark once; an unrelated invoice cannot use that reservation. Principal repayment changes the loan balance rather than being labeled an operating expense. All figures below are constructed accounting examples in USD.

Generated source: [treasury_witnesses.json](model/results/treasury_witnesses.json). The complete events and counterparty balances are retained there. See [the core boundary](model/TREASURY_CORE.md) and [independent review](reviews/treasury_core_review.md).


| Financial witness | Selected actor | Closing cash, USD | Interpretation |
| --- | --- | ---: | --- |
| witness_1_unfunded | provider | 30,000.00 | Payroll paid; proposed start blocked before future collection |
| witness_1_funded_next_month | provider | 50,000.00 | Explicit draw funds the start; later collection and principal repayment complete |
| witness_2_start_costs_once | provider | 88,000.00 | Two starts paid; one accepted sale collected; no second manufacturing charge |
| witness_3_restricted_deposit_refunded | provider | 30,000.00 | Restricted deposit cannot fund start; refund restores original provider cash |
| witness_5_unpaid_fee | entrant | 8,000.00 | Full-or-nothing fee remains unpaid; no automatic overdraft |
| witness_7_earmark_consumed | payer | 0.00 | Own invoice consumes earmark; unrelated payment is blocked |

The funded and unfunded paths are alternative worlds, not balances to add. A financial settlement test does not prove that a robot was manufactured, commissioned, supported or reassigned. The stage-1 yield case tests the payment boundary only; the fee case tests an unpaid liability only. The core also lacks an automatic payment scheduler, credit-maturity collection/default and interest accrual. Actual financing terms and supplier working capital remain separate empirical questions.


### Monthly physical progress must also be paid and supported

The next isolated fixture connects that cash core to integer unit identities, compatible components, deterministic accepted/rejected counts, shipment, commissioning and supported service. It uses one provider-owned system, fixed operator assignments and preplanned actions. Opening robots are historically funded stocks; their acquisition cost lies outside the horizon. Output is an exact rational synthetic service flow, not measured customer throughput.

Generated source: [monthly_deployment_witnesses.json](model/results/monthly_deployment_witnesses.json). Each row below is a separate constructed world; closing stocks count units, cash is USD, and output shows each operator's last modeled month. Source hashes cover both the monthly implementation and the treasury dependency.


| Witness | Closing physical stocks | Provider closing cash, USD | Last-month accepted service |
| --- | --- | ---: | --- |
| witness_8_funded | wip: 0; rejected: 0; finished: 0; transit: 0; installed: 1 | 2,700.00 | incumbent: 100 |
| unfunded_future_receipt | wip: 0; rejected: 0; finished: 0; transit: 0; installed: 0 | 70,000.00 | incumbent: 0 |
| funded_without_components | wip: 0; rejected: 0; finished: 0; transit: 0; installed: 0 | 40,000.00 | incumbent: 0 |
| unfunded_commissioning | wip: 0; rejected: 0; finished: 0; transit: 1; installed: 0 | 0.00 | incumbent: 0 |
| paid_rejected_start | wip: 0; rejected: 1; finished: 0; transit: 0; installed: 0 | 4,000.00 | incumbent: 0 |
| witness_4_static_allocation | wip: 0; rejected: 0; finished: 2; transit: 0; installed: 5 | 6,000.00 | incumbent: 250; entrant: 25 |

A paid start at month 0, phase 5 is accepted at month 1, phase 4 and can ship in phase 5 of that same month. With one-month commissioning lead, it commissions at month 2, phase 4 and delivers 25 units at phase 6 under one quarter-rate ramp; its next full month delivers 100. There is no extra finished-stock month or second annual half-year penalty. If commissioning cash is missing, the already paid unit remains in transit and produces zero. If the start is rejected, its manufacturing expense and consumed components remain sunk.

The static shared-allocation case begins with five finished and two installed units. Common support and commissioning limits permit two new incumbent assignments and one entrant assignment, leaving two finished units. The entrant's first-month output is 25, even though its request is ten robots. This tests conservation and actual assignment; it does not connect the separate ENTRY-FG-01 business, simulate customer competition, or establish an entrant return.

Existing installed support has strict priority. Receipts arriving after its phase-3 support attempt cannot retroactively repair that month's service; any unsupported opening installed unit blocks additions under this deliberately conservative policy. Contingent prepayment bookings can be canceled when work is not delivered, while previously earned obligations are not erased. The fixture does not schedule every opening company obligation, model sale/lease terms or operator cash, enforce default/recovery, or make endogenous investments. See [methods and limitations](model/MONTHLY_DEPLOYMENT.md) and [independent review](reviews/monthly_deployment_review.md). The full shared-treasury/entrant requirement remains partial.

### Coordination: retained uncertainty is sometimes the correct result

The local fixture compares blind resubmission with a durable business-intent ledger plus explicit authoritative reconciliation. Each policy receives the same invented order, fault and mock lookup capability. The deliberately naive comparator is a weak baseline, not an evaluation of a mature industrial integrator or an LLM. The reconciler relies on an added ability to find all tasks for a business key and establish dispatch finality; the inspected RMF sources do not establish that capability.

Generated source: [coordination_witnesses.json](model/results/coordination_witnesses.json). “Effect units” are invented irreversible actions, with no mapping to deliveries, elapsed time, wages or physical throughput. “Verified records” counts reconciled local completion; the naive comparator has no such ledger and therefore reports unknown rather than crediting API responses as completed business work.


| Injected fault | Policy | External tasks | Synthetic effect units | Verified local records |
| --- | --- | ---: | ---: | ---: |
| lost_ack | naive | 2 | 4 | Unknown |
| lost_ack | durable | 1 | 2 | 1 |
| restart | naive | 2 | 4 | Unknown |
| restart | durable | 1 | 2 | 1 |
| cache_eviction | naive | 2 | 4 | Unknown |
| cache_eviction | durable | 1 | 2 | 1 |
| lookup_unavailable | naive | 2 | 4 | Unknown |
| lookup_unavailable | durable | 1 | 2 | 0 |

When authoritative lookup is unavailable, blocking a retry can prevent a duplicate while leaving completion unverified. A local journal cannot make a remote effect and its local acknowledgment atomic. Even an empty lookup is insufficient if the original request might arrive later. Separate cancellation traces retain partial effects after acknowledgment, and completion racing with cancellation requires review. This establishes behavior of the constructed protocol under its declared assumptions, not incident rates or economic benefits in an installed system. See [fixture methods and limits](model/COORDINATION_TRANSACTION_FIXTURE.md) and [independent review](reviews/coordination_fixture_review.md).

### Inspecting the scenario calculation

The dashboard's joint comparison tables now expose the four branches through **Trace inputs and costs**. The trace calls the same engine and shows human/site digital cost, robot operating cost, annual capital consumption, accepted robot output and retained human hours. Exact inputs, source-function excerpts and matching scenario/engine hashes are available in the dialog. The reference follows the selected annual year and its resolved digital conditions, without learned increments; it remains a matched site contrast, not an installed-fleet estimate. A changed scenario or selected scope invalidates an open trace. This closes a bounded explanatory gap, while result-specific lineage for the rest of the dashboard remains incomplete.

The **Evidence fields** panel adds exact recorded geography, system/configuration, embodiment, harness, openness-layer and license selectors. Missing fields can be selected explicitly. A compound geography description remains the source's compound value, and a license label leaves commercial and hardware rights to the full artifact terms. These filters change evidence navigation only; they do not reclassify unknown records or alter scenario populations. Dedicated resource-grade filtering and comprehensive typed joins remain incomplete. See [filter semantics](dashboard/EVIDENCE_FILTERS.md).


## 11. What remains unresolved

The sources establish bounded workflows, relationships and constraints. Public disclosures often do not establish accepted-output exposure, all human support, retained deployment, negotiated prices, avoidable staffing, qualified supply or complete costs. These gaps limit market ranking and investment conclusions. A functioning mechanism model does not resolve them by assigning synthetic inputs.

Workstream K now contributes nine actor-role records, four contrasting dossiers, six model/release cards and eight artifact-rights cards. Their source and cost boundaries remain explicit. The four-architecture service comparison now has a separate synthetic calculation and sensitivities. Actual target costs, effective rights and qualified configurations remain unknown; the calculation does not fill those source gaps. The next research priorities are: reconcile field service/intervention denominators; challenge shared-resource and financial accounting; validate digital coordination against a strong simpler baseline; test customer entry against a prompt incumbent with equal technology access; qualify specific supply/workload dependencies; deepen labor crosswalks without invented physical shares; and expand non-Spot operator and nonadoption evidence. Each has a bounded action, evidence need and stopping condition in the continuing roadmap.

The acceptance register records individual requirements and limitations. Source counts and view counts do not themselves establish substantive completion. The original campaign window has ended with incomplete requirements. This separately dated continuation preserves the earlier edition and its gaps; the handoff records the interruption and next actions without backdating work or resetting the week.
